# Usher M4 — Ingest Pipeline Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Turn a registered source's library into canonical catalog state — every item matched to a `Title` (or visibly parked in a review queue), every copy tracked as a `MediaItem` whose availability is only ever retracted by a walk that provably finished, every episode hung off its series, and every watch record carried inbound without a single walk ever writing a zero over real play history.

**Architecture:** Four services (`MatchService`, `IngestService`, `WatchStateSyncService`, `EnrichService`) sit on nine repository ports and are driven by a Postgres-backed priority queue (`JobQueue`, `SELECT … FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`). Ingest walks a `SourceAdapter` **in batches**, and every catalog write in the batch path is a `COPY` into an `UNLOGGED` staging table followed by exactly one set-based statement — the path M2 already built and measured, reused rather than rediscovered, because 1,126,674 source items against 1,271,138 titles makes a per-row ORM write a design defect rather than a performance note. The reconciler's availability sweep runs **only** after a walk returns normally, and even then refuses to retract more than a configured fraction of a source in one run. The `SourceWatchState` port DTO gains a third state — *absent* — distinct from zero, and the merge that consumes it is `COALESCE`-shaped, so the failure this milestone was handed cannot be written even by a careless caller.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.13 · SQLAlchemy 2.0 async + asyncpg binary `COPY` · Alembic · PostgreSQL 17 (`pgvector/pgvector:pg17`) · httpx · pydantic v2 · loguru · OpenTelemetry (spans, links, W3C trace context) · pytest · testcontainers · ruff · mypy strict · import-linter

---

## The one decision this milestone was handed

M3's live run against Emby 4.9.5.0 found that a **listing** reports `PlayCount: 0` and omits `LastPlayedDate` entirely, for the very item whose **single-item** fetch reports `PlayCount: 2` and a real date. `PlaybackPositionTicks` and `Played` are correct in both. Neither `Fields=UserDataPlayState`, `Fields=UserData`, `EnableUserData=true`, nor restricting the listing to explicit `Ids` changes it.

`SourceAdapter.watch_state()` walks listings. So today it yields `play_count=0` and `last_played_at=None` for items that have genuinely been watched, and M4 is the first caller that would write those values.

**The decision: absence becomes representable, and zero stops being the way to spell it.**

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SourceWatchState:
    external_id: str
    position_seconds: int
    played: bool
    # `None` means "this read could not determine it", NOT "zero plays".
    play_count: int | None = None
    last_played_at: AwareDatetime | None = None
    source_user_id: str | None = None
```

Three consequences, each of which is a task below rather than a hope:

1. **`EmbyAdapter.watch_state()` yields `None` for both**, always, because the listing route on this build cannot say. `EmbyAdapter.get_watch_state()` — a new port method, one request, the single-item route the live run measured — yields the real values.
2. **`WatchStateRepository.merge_from_source` is `COALESCE`-shaped.** `None` leaves the stored column exactly as it was; `0` overwrites it, because a source that *can* count and says zero is reporting a reset, and reporting a reset is a thing PRD 03 requires to work (`test_watch_state_emits_a_zero_state_rather_than_skipping_it` exists for the same reason one rung down). A walk therefore *cannot* zero history — not because the caller is careful, but because the value it carries is not a number.
3. **Recovering history is a bounded, queued backfill, never a walk.** One request per item against 1,126,674 items is not a walk this project will ever run. But `played = true AND play_count = 0` is a *observable* "history unknown" state, and it is bounded by the household's actually-watched items — thousands, not a million. The merge enqueues a `watch_history` job for exactly those, at background priority, and the job calls `get_watch_state`. The predicate converges: Emby's own `POST /Users/{u}/PlayedItems/{item}` never leaves a played item at `PlayCount: 0`, so a row that has been backfilled once stops matching.

**What this costs, stated plainly.** `SourceWatchState.play_count` was `int` and is now `int | None`, so every read site needs a null check — that is 1 mapper, 2 adapters, 2 harnesses, and the contract suite. `SourceAdapter` grows a tenth abstract method, which every future adapter must implement (Jellyfin's listing may well carry history correctly; the contract permits that and does not force it to lie). `watch_states.play_count` stays `int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` rather than becoming nullable — so "played but count unknown" is spelled `played AND play_count = 0` rather than `play_count IS NULL`, which is very slightly lossy (an item genuinely played once whose history a source then cleared is indistinguishable from one never backfilled) and costs one redundant single-item fetch in that case. Accepted deliberately: making the column nullable would push a three-valued `int | None` into `WatchState`, PRD 02, every API response, and every dashboard query, to represent a state that self-heals after one background request.

**Why not the alternatives.**

- *Leave the port alone and have M4 just not write the two fields.* This is what M3 left behind, and it is a rule in a docstring rather than a property of a type. The next caller — M5's push lane, which re-walks `watch_state(since=…)` — inherits the same trap with nothing to stop it. The whole argument for a port is that it makes the wrong call unspellable.
- *Split `watch_state()` into a cheap walk and a rich walk.* Two methods with the same name shape and different truthfulness is worse than one method that says what it does not know.
- *Have the adapter fetch each item to fill the gap.* 1,126,674 requests at the 1–5 s per request PRD 01 measures. Not a walk; a week.

---

## Scope: what M4 is, and four boundary calls made deliberately

PRD [09](../prd/09-roadmap.md) states M4 as "Ingest → match → enrich → index; priority queue; stub-on-sight; unmatched review". Four places that boundary is ambiguous, decided here:

**1. The `index` stage is not built. M6 owns it.** PRD 03 stage 4 is "update the search document and compute the embedding", and both artefacts belong to M6 (`Full-text, autocomplete path, embeddings, RRF fusion`). No search document column, no `title_embeddings` table, and no embedder exists. PRD 03 itself says this stage "is a pure function of catalog state and can be rebuilt from scratch at any time" — which is exactly the licence to build it after the state exists rather than alongside it. M4 therefore ships **no `index` job kind**: a job kind whose handler is a stub is a queue that grows forever, and it is the thing this plan's own no-placeholder rule forbids. M6 adds the kind, the handler, and one backfill enqueue over `titles`.

**2. The `enrich` stage *is* built, and it populates `Title`, `Season`, and `Episode` — not `Person`, `Credit`, `Collection`, or `Image`.** The three 🔶 markers on `usher.ports.metadata.MetadataProvider` all name M4 and all block on "those models do not exist yet", so M4 has to settle them. It settles them by building the models the *ingest pipeline itself stores* — 999,827 of this deployment's 1,126,674 items are episodes, so `Season`/`Episode` are not optional for M4 in any reading — and by having the enrich stage cache TMDb's full `append_to_response` payload verbatim in `raw_payloads`. `Person`, `Credit`, and `Collection` are first *read* by M7 (rows: "more from this director" is a join; franchise completeness); `Image` is first read by M9 (the image proxy). Each lands with the milestone that reads it, re-derived from the cached payload with **no second network call** — which is precisely what PRD 02 says `raw_payloads` is for. `EnrichmentResult` (Task 22) is a frozen dataclass with a `payload` field, so adding `people: tuple[Person, ...]` later is an added field, not a signature change.

**3. Push, reconnect-delta, demand promotion and SSE are M5.** M4 builds the *full reconcile* lane and the *delta* lane driven by an explicit cursor (`sync_runs.cursor_at`), which is all PRD 03's reconciliation table assigns to something other than a socket. The queue's promotion mechanism (`ON CONFLICT … SET priority = GREATEST(…)`) lands here because it is one clause of the enqueue statement and leaving it out would mean rewriting the statement in M5; nothing in M4 *calls* it with `JobPriority.DEMAND`.

**4. No HTTP route is added.** PRD 07 lists `POST /admin/sources/{id}/sync` and `GET /admin/unmatched` · `POST /admin/unmatched/{id}/resolve`; all three are M9's surface, and M9 is where PRD 07's error-envelope vocabulary gets defined. M4 delivers the same capability through `usher.cli`, the project's established second composition root — exactly the shape M2 used for `bootstrap`, and for the same reason: the operator trigger has to exist before the HTTP surface does. Every service M4 writes is constructed by both roots identically, so M9 adds routers over finished services.

**What M4 delivers that M5 depends on:** a queue with a real claim/park/backoff contract and a `traceparent` column, an `IngestService` that takes a `since` cursor, and a `WatchStateSyncService` whose merge is safe to call with a partial state — which is what a `UserDataChanged` push event will hand it.

---

## Facts this plan builds on, and does not re-derive

1. **A listing's `UserData` is not an item's** — the finding this milestone exists to resolve, restated at the top of this document. Verified 2026-07-31 against Emby 4.9.5.0.
2. **The library is 1,126,674 items** — 94,438 movies, 32,409 series, 999,827 episodes. At the default page size (200) that is 5,634 pages, **56% of `MAX_PAGES`**. Any per-item network call across the whole library is out of the question; any per-item *database* round trip across it is a design defect.
3. **The catalog holds 1,271,138 titles** after M2's bootstrap — 899,828 movies / 371,310 series, 291,737 with a `tmdb_id`, 50,793 with a `tvdb_id`, 538,937 with a community rating. So tiers 1–3 of the matcher really are local, and the `tmdb_id` coverage gap (291,737 of 1,271,138) is why tier "create a stub from a trusted provider id" is not dead code.
4. **`tmdb_id` is unique per `kind`** (ADR-0011). `TitleRepository.get_by_tmdb_id` takes a `TitleKind`; any batch equivalent must key on the pair. TMDb's movie and series id spaces overlap on 26,968 ids.
5. **Bulk loading bypasses the repository, and the SQL has three traps** (`CLAUDE.md`, verified 2026-07-30 against `pgvector/pgvector:pg17`):
   - `ON CONFLICT` must repeat a partial index's predicate.
   - One statement may not hit the same conflict target twice — every staging read is `SELECT DISTINCT ON (<target>)`. **For M4 this is mandatory rather than defensive**: `SourceAdapter.list_items`' own contract says "the same item may be yielded more than once in a single walk", so a batch genuinely can contain two rows with the same `(source_id, external_id)`.
   - `xmax = 0` in `RETURNING` is the only way to tell an insert from an update; rowcount reports their sum.
6. **asyncpg's binary `COPY` is strictly typed** (a `str` into an `integer` column raises `TypeError` client-side) and CHECK constraints fire during `COPY`, so one bad row aborts its batch. Reach the driver with `(await (await session.connection()).get_raw_connection()).driver_connection` — `usher.db.repositories.bulk._raw` already does this and is reused.
7. **`ENRICHMENT_RANK` exists because `StrEnum` compares lexicographically.** `EnrichmentState.ENRICHED > EnrichmentState.SKELETON` is `False` (re-verified against this checkout while writing this plan). Every tier comparison in M4 goes through the rank map. ADR-0008.
8. **`watch_states.title_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`** (ADR-0010) and `media_items.title_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`. The asymmetry is deliberate; nothing in M4 may "fix" it.
9. **`PostgresImportRunRepository.save()` must roll back on a caught `IntegrityError`**, not just translate it, or the session is left `PendingRollbackError`-poisoned for the *next* statement. Every new repository in M4 that catches `IntegrityError` faces the same choice, and takes the same decision `PostgresTitleRepository` did — a `SAVEPOINT`, not a full rollback — because M4's services genuinely do have other pending work on the session (a batch of media items and its sync-run checkpoint commit together).
10. **`bulk_load_window()` commits the caller's session.** M4 never calls it: the catalog is live during a sync, and its own docstring says it declines on a non-empty `titles` anyway.
11. **`FastAPIInstrumentor` is wired in `create_app`**, and `SQLAlchemyInstrumentor`/`HTTPXClientInstrumentor` in `configure_tracing`. Pipeline spans started with `tracer.start_as_current_span(...)` nest under whatever is active. Task 26 pins that with a test rather than trusting it.
12. **Verified against this checkout while writing this plan:** `uv run pytest tests/unit` → **733 passed**; `uv run mypy src tests` → `Success: no issues found in 145 source files`; `uv run lint-imports` → `Contracts: 6 kept, 0 broken`; full suite 865 (733 unit / 132 integration). Head migration is `d4c9b1e37a05`.
13. **W3C trace-context propagation works out of the installed SDK with no extra dependency** — checked directly while writing this plan:
    ```pycon
    >>> from opentelemetry.trace.propagation.tracecontext import TraceContextTextMapPropagator
    >>> carrier = {}; TraceContextTextMapPropagator().inject(carrier)   # inside a span
    >>> carrier
    {'traceparent': '00-d14524c7eba73194c64d589cdd69488a-770641a119523a53-03'}
    ```
    `extract` returns a `Context` holding a `NonRecordingSpan` with `is_remote=True`, which is exactly what `trace.Link` wants.

---

## Two traps that silently produce a green suite against wrong code

Both have bitten this project. Both are cheap to disarm and expensive to discover.

**Stale `.pyc`.** A mutation test that deletes a constraint and re-runs the suite reads a cached bytecode file and reports the *old* behaviour. Before every mutation sweep:

```bash
find /home/anirudhlath/code/usher/src /home/anirudhlath/code/usher/tests -name __pycache__ -type d -exec rm -rf {} +
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
```

**The venv shebang trap.** `cp -r` of the repo to `/tmp` copies `.venv/bin/*`, whose shebangs are **absolute paths back into the original venv**. A mutation applied in the copy is then never imported: the interpreter that runs is the original checkout's, `sys.path` points at the original `src/`, and the suite runs green while proving nothing. Mutation-test **in place**, with `git stash`/`git checkout --` to revert — never in a copied tree. If a copy is unavoidable, delete `.venv` from it and run `uv sync` inside it first.

---

## What the new contract cases rule out

The suite this milestone extends is `tests/contract/source_adapter_contract.py` (40 cases today), plus five new repository contract suites. Each case below names the wrong implementation it fails, because a contract suite that passes against a deliberately-wrong implementation is hollow — M2's ratified four injected defects, two of them data-corrupting.

### `SourceAdapterContract` — four new cases

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_a_walk_never_reports_play_history_it_cannot_know` | **The finding.** An adapter whose walk reports `play_count=0` for an item that has been played twice. Asserts the walk's value is `None` *or* the true value — never a different number — so an honest source is not forced to lie and a lying one cannot pass. |
| `test_get_watch_state_is_authoritative_about_play_history` | An adapter that implements `get_watch_state` by delegating to the walk (which is the obvious lazy implementation, and is exactly wrong on Emby). Seeds `play_count=7`, asserts `get_watch_state` returns `7`, not `None` and not `0`. |
| `test_get_watch_state_returns_none_for_an_item_the_source_does_not_have` | An adapter that fabricates an all-zero state for an unknown id. That state would then be merged and would zero a row whose `MediaItem` is merely stale. |
| `test_get_watch_state_raises_when_the_source_is_unreachable` | The same failure `test_get_item_raises_when_the_source_is_unreachable` exists for, one method over: reporting a transport failure as `None`. Seeded first, so an adapter that returns `None` for everything cannot pass by accident. |

### `MediaItemRepositoryContract` — the reconciler's correctness

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_upsert_many_tolerates_a_duplicate_within_one_batch` | An implementation without `SELECT DISTINCT ON` — which raises `CardinalityViolationError` against a batch the port's own `list_items` contract explicitly permits. |
| `test_upsert_many_reports_inserts_and_updates_separately` | Returning `rowcount` for both, so a re-sync is indistinguishable from a first one and "library growth per week" (PRD 10 dashboard 1) is a straight line. |
| `test_upsert_many_never_downgrades_a_matched_item_to_unmatched` | An upsert whose `DO UPDATE SET title_id = excluded.title_id` blanks a manual review-queue resolution on the next nightly walk. |
| `test_marking_unseen_unavailable_spares_items_seen_during_the_run` | A sweep keyed on `available = true` rather than on `last_seen_at`, which retracts everything every time. |
| `test_marking_unseen_unavailable_restores_an_item_that_came_back` | A sweep that only ever sets `false`. PRD 02: "Items that vanish from a source get `available = false`" — and items that come back must come back. |
| `test_marking_unseen_unavailable_refuses_to_retract_a_whole_library` | A completed-but-empty walk (an unmounted drive; a library the operator removed by accident) retracting all 1,126,674 items in one statement. The guard raises `AvailabilitySweepRefused` and the sync run is recorded `FAILED` with its numbers. |
| `test_upsert_many_never_hard_deletes` | An implementation that "cleans up" rows absent from the batch. PRD 02: "Soft-delete availability, hard-delete nothing." |

### `WatchStateRepositoryContract` — the port decision, enforced downstream

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_absent_play_history_leaves_a_stored_count_alone` | The whole point. `merge_from_source(play_count=None)` over a row holding `play_count=7` must still read `7`. An implementation that writes `COALESCE(:play_count, 0)`, or that treats the DTO default as a value, reads back `0`. |
| `test_a_reported_zero_play_count_is_written` | Over-correcting the above into "`play_count` is never written from a merge", which makes un-marking something played impossible to propagate — the same correctness bug as filtering zero states out of a walk. |
| `test_absent_last_played_at_leaves_a_stored_timestamp_alone` | Same failure, other column. Separate case because a `COALESCE` fix applied to one column and not the other passes the first. |
| `test_a_merge_does_not_overwrite_a_newer_api_write` | PRD 03's "latest `updated_at` wins". A merge whose `WHERE` clause is missing lets a nightly walk stomp a resume position a client set thirty seconds ago. |
| `test_a_merge_rejects_a_state_attached_to_neither_a_title_nor_an_episode` | The `num_nonnulls(title_id, episode_id) = 1` CHECK reaching the caller as a raw `IntegrityError` instead of a `PortDataMalformed`. |
| `test_a_played_item_with_no_known_history_is_listed_for_backfill` | `list_needing_history` returning everything (a backfill of 1.1M items) or nothing (history never recovered). |

### `JobQueueContract` — PRD 08's failure policy

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_two_workers_never_claim_the_same_job` | `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` without `SKIP LOCKED`, which serialises workers instead of distributing them, or a plain `SELECT` + `UPDATE`, which hands the same job to both. Run against real Postgres with two live sessions — a fake cannot express this at all, and the contract says so. |
| `test_a_job_is_claimed_by_priority_then_age` | `ORDER BY priority` (ascending — the wrong direction for a scale where 100 is highest) or no age tiebreak, which starves the oldest job at a given priority forever. |
| `test_a_failed_job_is_retried_after_a_backoff` | Re-claiming instantly, which turns one broken upstream into a hot loop. |
| `test_a_job_is_parked_after_the_attempt_ceiling_with_its_error` | Retrying forever, or deleting it. PRD 08: "*parked* with its error, not retried forever and not silently dropped." |
| `test_malformed_data_parks_immediately_rather_than_backing_off` | Treating `PortDataMalformed` as transient. Its own docstring: "the upstream answered, and the answer was wrong. Retrying does not help." Asserts `attempts == 1` at park time — a ceiling-based park reports `attempts == 5`. |
| `test_enqueueing_the_same_work_twice_does_not_duplicate_it` | No `(kind, key)` uniqueness, so a nightly walk enqueues 1.1M duplicate match jobs on top of yesterday's. |
| `test_re_enqueueing_at_a_higher_priority_promotes_the_existing_job` | `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, which makes M5's demand promotion impossible without a schema change. |
| `test_re_enqueueing_at_a_lower_priority_does_not_demote` | `SET priority = excluded.priority`, which lets a background backfill sweep demote a job a client is waiting on. |
| `test_requeue_running_recovers_jobs_from_an_unclean_shutdown` | PRD 08: "Startup requeues anything left `in_progress`." Without it a killed worker's claims are lost until a human notices. |
| `test_a_parked_job_is_not_claimed` | A claim query that forgets `status = 'pending'`, so parked poison is retried forever after all. |

### `TitleMatchRepositoryContract` — matching at catalog scale

| Contract case | The wrong implementation it fails |
|---|---|
| `test_provider_id_lookup_is_namespaced_by_kind` | ADR-0011, in batch form. A movie and a series sharing `tmdb_id=550` must resolve to different titles; an implementation keyed on the bare id returns one of them arbitrarily. |
| `test_name_year_lookup_accepts_a_year_within_one` | PRD 03 stage 3's "±1". An exact-year match misses a film whose source and IMDb disagree by one, which is common. |
| `test_name_year_lookup_is_case_insensitive_and_kind_scoped` | A lookup that cannot use `ix_titles_name_lower_year` (which is on `lower(name)`), i.e. one that seq-scans 1,271,138 rows per item. |
| `test_an_ambiguous_name_year_match_resolves_to_nothing` | Picking the first of several same-name, same-year titles. PRD 03 stage 5: no *confident* match means the review queue, not a coin flip. |
| `test_a_batch_lookup_answers_every_probe_it_was_given` | An implementation that silently drops probes it found nothing for, so the caller cannot tell "no match" from "not asked". |

| `docs/prd/decisions/0014-absence-is-not-zero.md` | ADR — the port change |
| `docs/prd/decisions/0015-availability-is-retracted-only-by-a-finished-walk.md` | ADR — the sweep and its guard |
| `docs/prd/decisions/0016-raw-payloads-cache-providers-not-sources.md` | ADR — the PRD correction |
| `docs/prd/02-data-model.md`, `03-sources-and-sync.md`, `08-operations.md`, `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md`, `README.md` | *(modify)* PRD, in the same commits as the changes that invalidate them |

## Where each new fake is more forgiving than the real thing

Six fake-vs-reality divergences have bitten this project, every one making tests pass against buggy code. Each fake M4 adds carries this statement in its own module docstring, and the paired real-Postgres run is what closes it.

| Fake | Where it is more forgiving than Postgres | What closes the gap |
|---|---|---|
| `FakeMediaItemRepository` | Dict-keyed on `(source_id, external_id)`, so a duplicate inside one batch is silently last-wins — the exact `CardinalityViolationError` the real one must dedupe around. No CHECK constraints, so a negative `width` is stored happily. | `tests/integration/test_media_item_repository.py` runs the identical contract against `PostgresMediaItemRepository`. |
| `FakeWatchStateRepository` | Python `if value is not None` is naturally `COALESCE`-shaped, so the merge is correct *by accident*. The real one has to spell it in SQL, where the natural spelling (`SET play_count = :play_count`) is wrong. | Same contract, real Postgres. The mutation in Task 13 Step 6 is what proves the SQL is load-bearing. |
| `FakeJobQueue` | Single-threaded, no locks: `test_two_workers_never_claim_the_same_job` is **skipped** for it rather than passed. A fake cannot express `SKIP LOCKED` and pretending otherwise would ratify a plain `SELECT`. | The contract marks that case `requires_concurrency`, and only the Postgres subclass sets it. |
| `FakeTitleMatchRepository` | Matches on `name.lower()` in Python, so it agrees with `lower(name)` by construction; the real one only agrees if the query actually uses `lower()` and can therefore use `ix_titles_name_lower_year`. Nothing in the fake can catch a seq scan. | `tests/integration/test_title_match_repository.py` asserts on `EXPLAIN` output for the plan, not just the rows. |
| `FakeEpisodeRepository` | No FK, so an episode can be stored under a `title_id` that does not exist. | Real Postgres raises; the contract asserts `RepositoryConflict`. |
| `FakeMetadataProvider` | Serves committed fixtures, so it can never rate-limit, never 404, and never return a payload shape TMDb has changed. It also answers instantly, so nothing about the token-bucket throttle is exercised. | `tests/unit/test_adapters_tmdb_client.py` drives the real client over `httpx.MockTransport` with 429s and 404s; the throttle is tested against a fake clock. |
| `FakeSourceAdapter` (modified) | Its walk returns the **true** `play_count`, because it stores what the harness seeded. So `test_a_walk_never_reports_play_history_it_cannot_know` passes on its `is None` branch only for Emby. The fake proves the assertion is *expressible*; the Emby run proves it *bites*. | `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_contract.py` — the same case against the real adapter over `FakeEmbyServer`, whose listing renderer now omits the two fields exactly as the live server does. |

## Task 1: Absence becomes representable on `SourceWatchState`

> **Implemented 2026-07-31** (commits `c9b84f3`, `0c24968`, `ae8599a`). Tasks 1–3 are done; the corrections below are marked **⟲ as implemented** where the code diverges from what this section predicted. Eight of them, one of which inverts Task 3 Step 7's central claim.
>
> **Every "Expected: N passed" line in Tasks 1–3 is unverifiable as written.** `pyproject.toml`'s `addopts` already carries `-q`, so the plan's own `-q` makes it `-qq`, which suppresses pytest's summary line entirely — `| tail -3` then prints three lines of dots. Drop the `-q` from these commands.

The port change this milestone was handed. Two fields become optional, one method is added, and the docstrings say what `None` means so no caller has to guess.

`get_watch_state` is deliberately *not* folded into `get_item`: `SourceItem` is the shape ingest stores and it carries no watch fields at all, and a method that returned both would make every ingest walk pay for watch state it does not want.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/source.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_ports_source.py`

- [x] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Append to `tests/unit/test_ports_source.py`:

```python
def test_source_watch_state_defaults_play_history_to_absent_not_zero() -> None:
    """The finding this milestone exists to resolve, in DTO form.

    Verified 2026-07-31 against Emby 4.9.5.0: a *listing* reports
    `PlayCount: 0` and omits `LastPlayedDate`, for an item whose single-item
    fetch reports `PlayCount: 2` and a real date. A walk therefore cannot
    say, and `0` is a claim rather than an absence -- so the default must be
    `None`. If this default is `0`, every merge in M4 writes zero over real
    history and nothing anywhere reports a failure.
    """
    state = SourceWatchState(external_id="movie-1", position_seconds=90, played=False)
    assert state.play_count is None
    assert state.last_played_at is None


def test_source_watch_state_still_carries_a_reported_zero() -> None:
    """Over-correcting into "play_count is never reported" would make a
    reset impossible to propagate -- the same correctness bug as filtering
    all-zero states out of a walk. A source that *can* count and says zero
    must be able to say so."""
    state = SourceWatchState(
        external_id="movie-1", position_seconds=0, played=False, play_count=0
    )
    assert state.play_count == 0


def test_get_watch_state_is_on_the_port() -> None:
    """The authoritative read. Emby's single-item route carries the real
    `PlayCount`/`LastPlayedDate` its listing does not; without a port method
    for it, play history is unrecoverable at any price."""
    assert "get_watch_state" in SourceAdapter.__abstractmethods__
    signature = inspect.signature(SourceAdapter.get_watch_state)
    assert list(signature.parameters) == ["self", "external_id"]
    assert signature.return_annotation == "SourceWatchState | None"
```

It is kept as a regression guard against the over-correction (making the field un-reportable), which is a real risk, but it never fails and is not evidence the change happened. `test_get_watch_state_is_on_the_port` also asserts on the annotation with `inspect.signature(..., eval_str=True)` compared against the real `SourceWatchState | None`, not against the literal string `"SourceWatchState | None"`: the string form passes only if the annotation is written quoted, and the method is written bare to match `get_item` directly above it.

- [x] **Step 3: Change the port**

In `src/usher/ports/source.py`, replace the `SourceWatchState` dataclass:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SourceWatchState:
    """One item's watch state as a source reports it.

    **`play_count` and `last_played_at` are three-valued, and `None` is not
    zero.** `None` means "this read could not determine it"; `0` and a real
    datetime are positive claims a caller must honour, including the claim
    that something has been reset to unplayed.

    That distinction is not fastidiousness. Verified 2026-07-31 against Emby
    4.9.5.0: a `GET /Users/{user}/Items` *listing* reports `PlayCount: 0`
    and omits `LastPlayedDate` entirely, for the very item whose
    `GET /Users/{user}/Items/{item}` reports `PlayCount: 2` and a real
    `LastPlayedDate`. `PlaybackPositionTicks` and `Played` are correct in
    both. No `Fields` value, no `EnableUserData`, and no `Ids` restriction
    changes it. So `watch_state()` -- which walks listings -- cannot carry
    play history, and if these two fields could only say "0" then every
    delta walk would overwrite the household's real history with zeros,
    silently, forever.

    An implementation whose listing *does* carry history is free to report
    it from the walk; the contract requires only that a reported number be
    true, never that it be present. `get_watch_state` is where a caller goes
    when it needs the answer rather than whatever the cheap read happened to
    know.
    """

    external_id: str
    position_seconds: int
    played: bool
    play_count: int | None = None
    last_played_at: AwareDatetime | None = None
    # Emby is multi-user; None means "the source didn't distinguish", which
    # today is fine because everything implicitly lands on the singleton
    # default user (PRD 01's authentication seam). Cheap to carry now --
    # becomes a breaking DTO change the moment a household has two users.
    source_user_id: str | None = None
```

and add this abstract method to `SourceAdapter`, immediately after `watch_state`:

```python
    @abstractmethod
    async def get_watch_state(self, external_id: str) -> "SourceWatchState | None":
        """Authoritative watch state for one item, including play history.

        The expensive, exact counterpart to `watch_state`'s cheap walk.
        Where the walk is permitted to report `play_count`/`last_played_at`
        as `None` ("this read cannot say"), this method must report the real
        values whenever the source holds them -- an implementation that
        delegates to the walk is wrong on any source whose listing is
        lossier than its item route, which is the measured behaviour of the
        one source that exists.

        `None` means the item is gone from the source, exactly as
        `get_item` does, and for the same reason: a caller must never learn
        to tell a deletion from a 404. A transient failure to reach the
        source raises (e.g. `PortUnavailable`) and is never reported as
        `None` -- conflating them would let a flaky network merge an
        all-zero state over a real one.

        Costed deliberately: one request per item, against a library
        measured at 1,126,674 items. No walk may call this. Its caller is a
        queued, bounded backfill over the items a walk has flagged as
        played-but-history-unknown.
        """
```

## Task 2: The contract suite learns the difference, and the fake follows

The suite is the thing that makes this a port change rather than an Emby fix. It has to express "the walk may decline" without letting "the walk may lie" through the same hole.

**Files:**
- Modify: `tests/contract/source_adapter_contract.py`
- Modify: `tests/fakes/source_adapter.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_source_adapter_contract.py` (runs the suite against the fake; no change needed, it inherits)

- [x] **Step 1: Write the failing contract cases**

Replace `test_watch_state_carries_the_play_history_too` in `tests/contract/source_adapter_contract.py` with the block below, and add the three `get_watch_state` cases after `test_watch_state_raises_rather_than_truncating`:

```python
    async def test_a_walk_never_reports_play_history_it_cannot_know(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """The measured failure, expressed so it cannot be passed by lying.

        Emby's listing route reports `PlayCount: 0` and omits
        `LastPlayedDate` for an item whose single-item route reports
        `PlayCount: 2` and a real date (verified 2026-07-31 against 4.9.5.0).
        An adapter that passes the listing's zeros through would report `0`
        here and the merge downstream would write it over real history.

        So the assertion is not "the walk reports 7" (which would force an
        honest-but-lossy source to fabricate) and not "the walk reports
        None" (which would forbid a source whose listing is complete). It is
        **either the truth or an explicit absence, never a third number** --
        which is exactly the guarantee `SourceWatchState`'s docstring makes
        and the only one a caller can build a `COALESCE` on.

        `FakeSourceAdapter` passes this on the `== 7` branch (it stores what
        the harness seeded); `EmbyAdapter` passes it on the `is None`
        branch. Both branches being live across the two runs is what makes
        this a contract rather than a restatement of one implementation.
        """
        await harness.given_item(MOVIE, changed_at=T0)
        last_played = datetime(2026, 7, 20, 21, 4, 0, tzinfo=UTC)
        await harness.given_watch_state(
            SourceWatchState(
                external_id="movie-1",
                position_seconds=1840,
                played=True,
                play_count=7,
                last_played_at=last_played,
            )
        )
        states = {state.external_id: state async for state in harness.adapter.watch_state()}
        walked = states["movie-1"]
        assert walked.position_seconds == 1840
        assert walked.played is True
        assert walked.play_count in (None, 7), (
            f"a walk reported play_count={walked.play_count!r} for an item played 7 times; "
            "the port permits None (this read cannot say) or the true value, and nothing else"
        )
        assert walked.last_played_at in (None, last_played)

    async def test_get_watch_state_is_authoritative_about_play_history(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """The other half of the same contract. `watch_state` may decline;
        this may not. An adapter that implements this by delegating to its
        own walk -- the obvious lazy implementation, and the one that is
        exactly wrong on Emby -- reports `None` here and the household's
        play history is unrecoverable at any price."""
        await harness.given_item(MOVIE, changed_at=T0)
        last_played = datetime(2026, 7, 20, 21, 4, 0, tzinfo=UTC)
        await harness.given_watch_state(
            SourceWatchState(
                external_id="movie-1",
                position_seconds=1840,
                played=True,
                play_count=7,
                last_played_at=last_played,
            )
        )
        state = await harness.adapter.get_watch_state("movie-1")
        assert state is not None
        assert state.play_count == 7
        assert state.last_played_at == last_played
        assert state.position_seconds == 1840
        assert state.played is True

    async def test_get_watch_state_returns_none_for_an_item_the_source_does_not_have(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """An adapter that fabricates an all-zero state for an unknown id
        hands the merge a positive claim of "never played" about something
        it knows nothing about. `None` is the only honest answer, and it is
        the same answer `get_item` gives, so a caller never learns to tell
        the two apart."""
        assert await harness.adapter.get_watch_state("never-existed") is None

    async def test_get_watch_state_raises_when_the_source_is_unreachable(
        self, harness: SourceHarness
    ) -> None:
        """`get_item`'s most dangerous wrong implementation, one method
        over. Seeded first on purpose: against an empty source an adapter
        that answered `None` for a transport failure would look correct."""
        await harness.given_item(MOVIE, changed_at=T0)
        await harness.given_watch_state(
            SourceWatchState(external_id="movie-1", position_seconds=1840, played=True)
        )
        await harness.go_offline()
        with pytest.raises(PortUnavailable):
            await harness.adapter.get_watch_state("movie-1")
```

```python
"""...

**Where this fake is more forgiving than a real source, on purpose.** Its
walk returns the *true* `play_count`/`last_played_at`, because it yields
back the very `SourceWatchState` the harness seeded. So
`test_a_walk_never_reports_play_history_it_cannot_know` passes here on its
`== 7` branch and never exercises the `is None` branch -- the branch that
matters, and the branch the measured Emby behaviour lands on. That case has
teeth only in `EmbyHarness`, where the fake server's *listing* renderer
omits the two fields exactly as Emby 4.9.5.0 does. The fake's job is to
prove the assertion is expressible without reference to Emby; it is not
evidence that any adapter needed it.

```

and add, immediately after `_walk_states`:

```python
    async def get_watch_state(self, external_id: str) -> SourceWatchState | None:
        """Authoritative, which for a fake means "the same thing the walk
        returns" -- see the module docstring. `None` for an unknown id,
        matching `get_item`, and `_ready()` first so a closed or offline
        adapter raises `PortUnavailable` rather than answering."""
        await self._ready()
        if external_id not in self._items:
            return None
        return self._states.get(external_id) or SourceWatchState(
            external_id=external_id, position_seconds=0, played=False
        )
```

Also fix `push_watch_state`, which today reconstructs a `SourceWatchState` and would now silently drop the stored history:

```python
    async def push_watch_state(self, external_id: str, state: WatchStateUpdate) -> None:
        await self._ready()
        # Preserve whatever history is already recorded rather than
        # rebuilding the state from scratch: a real source's write-back does
        # not reset `PlayCount` (verified on Emby -- marking played advances
        # it to 1 idempotently, and a position write leaves it alone), so a
        # fake that zeroed it would make `test_get_watch_state_is_
        # authoritative_about_play_history` order-dependent.
        existing = self._states.get(external_id)
        self._states[external_id] = SourceWatchState(
            external_id=external_id,
            position_seconds=state.position_seconds,
            played=state.played,
            play_count=None if existing is None else existing.play_count,
            last_played_at=None if existing is None else existing.last_played_at,
        )
```

## Task 3: The Emby adapter stops reporting a zero it cannot know

Three files move together: the mapper gains a flag for which route a payload came from, the adapter passes it, and `FakeEmbyServer`'s *listing* renderer starts omitting the two fields the live server omits. That last one is the point — the fake previously implemented the adapter's own guess, which is how 40 contract assertions passed against a write-back that had never worked once.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/adapters/emby/mapping.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py`
- Modify: `tests/fakes/emby_server.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_mapping.py`, `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_adapter.py`

- [x] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Append to `tests/unit/test_adapters_emby_mapping.py`:

```python
def test_a_listing_payload_yields_absent_play_history() -> None:
    """Emby 4.9.5.0's listing route reports `PlayCount: 0` and omits
    `LastPlayedDate` for items that have genuinely been played (verified
    2026-07-31). Passing that `0` through as a number is how M4 would write
    zero over real history, so the mapper must report absence instead."""
    payload = {
        "Id": "movie-1",
        "Name": "Example Movie",
        "Type": "Movie",
        "UserData": {"PlaybackPositionTicks": 18_400_000_000, "Played": True, "PlayCount": 0},
    }
    state = to_watch_state(payload, source_user_id="u1", play_history_is_trustworthy=False)
    assert state is not None
    assert state.position_seconds == 1840
    assert state.played is True
    assert state.play_count is None
    assert state.last_played_at is None


def test_an_item_payload_yields_real_play_history() -> None:
    """The single-item route does carry both, and that is the whole reason
    `get_watch_state` exists."""
    payload = {
        "Id": "movie-1",
        "Name": "Example Movie",
        "Type": "Movie",
        "UserData": {
            "PlaybackPositionTicks": 18_400_000_000,
            "Played": True,
            "PlayCount": 2,
            "LastPlayedDate": "2026-07-20T21:04:00.0000000Z",
        },
    }
    state = to_watch_state(payload, source_user_id="u1", play_history_is_trustworthy=True)
    assert state is not None
    assert state.play_count == 2
    assert state.last_played_at == datetime(2026, 7, 20, 21, 4, tzinfo=UTC)


def test_a_trusted_payload_that_omits_play_count_still_reports_absence() -> None:
    """Trusting the route is not the same as inventing a value. A single-item
    payload with no `PlayCount` key at all has not told us zero."""
    payload = {
        "Id": "movie-1",
        "Name": "Example Movie",
        "Type": "Movie",
        "UserData": {"PlaybackPositionTicks": 0, "Played": False},
    }
    state = to_watch_state(payload, source_user_id="u1", play_history_is_trustworthy=True)
    assert state is not None
    assert state.play_count is None
```

async def test_get_watch_state_uses_the_single_item_route(
    emby: FakeEmbyServer, adapter: EmbyAdapter
) -> None:
    """One request against `/Users/{u}/Items/{id}`, which is the route the

    would cost 5,634 pages to answer one question."""

    emby.set_watch_state(
        SourceWatchState(
            external_id=MOVIE_PAYLOAD["Id"],
            position_seconds=1840,
            played=True,
            play_count=7,
            last_played_at=datetime(2026, 7, 20, 21, 4, tzinfo=UTC),
        )
    )

        )
    )
    states = [state async for state in adapter.watch_state()]
    assert len(states) == 1
    assert states[0].played is True
    assert states[0].play_count is None
    assert states[0].last_played_at is None

- [x] **Step 3: Change the mapper**

Replace `to_watch_state` in `src/usher/adapters/emby/mapping.py`:

```python
def to_watch_state(
    payload: Mapping[str, Any],
    *,
    source_user_id: str | None,
    play_history_is_trustworthy: bool,
) -> SourceWatchState | None:
    """One Emby item's `UserData` into a `SourceWatchState`.

    `None` when the item carries no `UserData` at all, which means the field
    was not requested or this item type has none. That is a different claim
    from a zero state: emitting zeros here would push "unwatched" over
    whatever Usher already knows. A `UserData` block that *is* present and
    happens to be all zeros is emitted -- see the port's `watch_state`
    docstring for why filtering those is a correctness bug.

    **`play_history_is_trustworthy` names the route, not a preference.**
    Verified 2026-07-31 against Emby 4.9.5.0: `GET /Users/{u}/Items` reports
    `PlayCount: 0` and omits `LastPlayedDate` entirely, for the very item
    whose `GET /Users/{u}/Items/{id}` reports `PlayCount: 2` and a real
    date. No `Fields` value, no `EnableUserData`, and no `Ids` restriction
    changes it. So a listing's zero is not a count, it is the absence of
    one, and the caller -- which is the only thing that knows which route it
    called -- has to say so. Passing `True` from a listing walk is the exact
    bug this parameter exists to make un-writable by accident.

    Even under `True`, a key that is simply not present yields `None`
    rather than `0`: trusting the route is not the same as inventing a
    value.
    """
    external_id = as_text(payload.get("Id"))
    user_data = payload.get("UserData")
    if external_id is None or not isinstance(user_data, Mapping):
        return None
    ticks = as_int(user_data.get("PlaybackPositionTicks")) or 0
    play_count: int | None = None
    last_played_at: AwareDatetime | None = None
    if play_history_is_trustworthy:
        counted = as_int(user_data.get("PlayCount"))
        play_count = max(counted, 0) if counted is not None else None
        last_played_at = parse_datetime(user_data.get("LastPlayedDate"))
    return SourceWatchState(
        external_id=external_id,
        position_seconds=max(ticks, 0) // TICKS_PER_SECOND,
        played=bool(user_data.get("Played", False)),
        play_count=play_count,
        last_played_at=last_played_at,
        source_user_id=source_user_id,
    )
```

Add `AwareDatetime` to that module's `from pydantic import ...` line if it is not already imported.

- [x] **Step 4: Change the adapter**

In `src/usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py`, replace the `watch_state` docstring's final two paragraphs and `_watch_state`, and add `get_watch_state`:

```python
    def watch_state(self, since: AwareDatetime | None = None) -> AsyncIterator[SourceWatchState]:
        """Walk this user's watch state.

        **This walk reports `play_count` and `last_played_at` as `None`,
        always, and that is a property of the server rather than a
        limitation of this code.** Verified 2026-07-31 against Emby 4.9.5.0:
        a *listing* reports `PlayCount: 0` and omits `LastPlayedDate`, even
        for an item whose single-item `GET /Users/{user}/Items/{item}`
        reports `PlayCount: 2` and a real `LastPlayedDate`.
        `position_seconds` and `played` are correct in both. Neither
        `Fields=UserDataPlayState`, `Fields=UserData`, `EnableUserData=true`,
        nor restricting the listing to specific `Ids` changes it.

        Reporting the listing's `0` would write zero over real history at
        every merge. Reporting `None` says exactly what is true -- this read
        cannot determine it -- and `WatchStateRepository.merge_from_source`
        is `COALESCE`-shaped for precisely this value. `get_watch_state`
        below is the authoritative read, at one request per item.
        """
        return self._watch_state(since)

    async def _watch_state(self, since: AwareDatetime | None) -> AsyncIterator[SourceWatchState]:
        user_id = await self._session.user_id()
        async for payload in self._walk(since_param=USER_DATA_SINCE_PARAM, since=since):
            # play_history_is_trustworthy=False: this is the listing route.
            state = to_watch_state(
                payload, source_user_id=user_id, play_history_is_trustworthy=False
            )
            if state is not None:
                yield state

    async def get_watch_state(self, external_id: str) -> SourceWatchState | None:
        """Authoritative watch state for one item, from the single-item
        route that carries the play history the listing route does not.

        Reuses `_fetch`, so a 404 is `None` and every other failure raises,
        exactly as `get_item` behaves -- the two must not diverge or a
        caller learns to tell a deletion from an outage by which method it
        called.
        """
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span("source.get_watch_state") as span:
            span.set_attribute("usher.source", self._source.name)
            span.set_attribute("usher.external_id", external_id)
            payload = await self._fetch(external_id)
            span.set_attribute("usher.found", payload is not None)
            if payload is None:
                return None
            return to_watch_state(
                payload,
                source_user_id=await self._session.user_id(),
                play_history_is_trustworthy=True,
            )
```

ADR-0014, `docs/prd/decisions/0014-absence-is-not-zero.md`:

```markdown
# ADR-0014 — Absence is not zero on `SourceWatchState`

**Status:** Accepted

## Context

`SourceAdapter.watch_state()` walks a source's listing. Verified 2026-07-31
against Emby 4.9.5.0, a `GET /Users/{user}/Items` listing reports
`PlayCount: 0` and omits `LastPlayedDate` entirely, for the very item whose
`GET /Users/{user}/Items/{item}` reports `PlayCount: 2` and a real
`LastPlayedDate`. `PlaybackPositionTicks` and `Played` are correct in both.
Neither `Fields=UserDataPlayState`, `Fields=UserData`, `EnableUserData=true`,
nor restricting the listing to explicit `Ids` changes it.

`SourceWatchState.play_count` was `int` with a default of `0`, so the walk
had no way to say anything other than "zero plays". M4 is the first caller
that writes these fields. Recovering them per item costs one request each
against a library measured at 1,126,674 items.

## Decision

`play_count: int | None = None` and `last_played_at: AwareDatetime | None =
None`. `None` means "this read could not determine it"; `0` and a real
datetime are positive claims a caller must honour.

`SourceAdapter` gains `get_watch_state(external_id) -> SourceWatchState |
None` — one request, the authoritative read, `None` for a deleted item and
a raise for an unreachable source, matching `get_item` exactly.

`WatchStateRepository.merge_from_source` is `COALESCE`-shaped: `None` leaves
the stored column untouched, `0` overwrites it. Recovering history is a
queued backfill over `played = true AND play_count = 0`, bounded by the
household's watched items rather than by the library.

## Consequences

**Gained:** a walk cannot zero real play history, because the value it
carries is not a number. The contract suite can state the rule
source-agnostically — a reported number must be true, but presence is never
required — so a source whose listing *is* complete (Jellyfin may well be) is
not forced to lie in either direction.

**Given up:** every read site needs a null check, and `SourceAdapter` grows
a tenth abstract method every future adapter must implement.
`watch_states.play_count` stays `int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, so
"played but count unknown" is spelled `played AND play_count = 0` rather
than `IS NULL` — very slightly lossy, self-healing after one background
request, and far cheaper than pushing three-valued play counts through
`WatchState`, PRD 02, every API response, and every dashboard query.

**Rejected:** leaving the port alone and making M4 simply not write the
fields. That is a rule in a docstring rather than a property of a type, and
M5's push lane re-walks `watch_state(since=…)` with nothing to stop it
inheriting the same trap.

## Evidence

`tests/contract/source_adapter_contract.py::
SourceAdapterContract::test_a_walk_never_reports_play_history_it_cannot_know`
asserts the walked value is `None` *or* the seeded value and nothing else.
It passes on the `== 7` branch against `FakeSourceAdapter` and on the `is
None` branch against `EmbyAdapter` over `FakeEmbyServer` — both branches
live across the two runs. Mutation-tested three ways (M4 plan, Task 2 Step
5 and Task 3 Step 7), including the pair that goes green together: adapter
trusting the listing *and* fake server rendering a listing that carries
history. Only a live run separates those, which is why Task 28 exists.
```

## Task 4: `Season` and `Episode`

999,827 of this deployment's 1,126,674 items are episodes. Without these two models the ingest pipeline can store 11% of the library, so this is not a TV-support nicety.

Both are frozen `DomainModel`s. Neither is a `Title`: `TitleKind` stays `movie | series` (PRD 02), and an episode's canonical parent is the series `Title`, which is what makes episode watch state survive a source change exactly as title watch state does.

# tests/unit/test_domain_episode.py
"""Season and Episode.

Every constraint here is asserted with a value that violates it, not with
one that satisfies it -- a test that only constructs a valid model passes
against a model with no validators at all.
"""

import uuid
from datetime import date

import pytest
from pydantic import ValidationError

from usher.domain.episode import Episode, Season
from usher.domain.ids import new_id

TITLE_ID = new_id()
SEASON_ID = new_id()


def test_a_season_is_identified_by_ushers_own_id() -> None:
    season = Season(title_id=TITLE_ID, season_number=2)
    assert isinstance(season.id, uuid.UUID)
    assert season.id.version == 7
    assert season.tmdb_id is None


def test_a_season_number_may_be_zero_for_specials() -> None:
    """TMDb numbers a series' specials as season 0, and Emby emits
    `ParentIndexNumber: 0` for them. Rejecting zero would drop every
    special in the library."""
    assert Season(title_id=TITLE_ID, season_number=0).season_number == 0


def test_a_negative_season_number_is_rejected() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Season(title_id=TITLE_ID, season_number=-1)


def test_a_negative_episode_count_is_rejected() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Season(title_id=TITLE_ID, season_number=1, episode_count=-1)


def test_an_episode_carries_its_place_in_the_series() -> None:
    episode = Episode(
        title_id=TITLE_ID,
        season_id=SEASON_ID,
        season_number=2,
        episode_number=5,
        name="Kissed by Fire",
        air_date=date(2013, 4, 28),
        runtime_minutes=57,
        tmdb_id=63_070,
        imdb_id="tt2178782",
    )
    assert (episode.season_number, episode.episode_number) == (2, 5)
    assert episode.absolute_number is None


def test_a_negative_episode_number_is_rejected() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Episode(title_id=TITLE_ID, season_id=SEASON_ID, season_number=1, episode_number=-1)


def test_a_negative_runtime_is_rejected() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Episode(
            title_id=TITLE_ID,
            season_id=SEASON_ID,
            season_number=1,
            episode_number=1,
            runtime_minutes=-1,
        )


def test_a_malformed_imdb_id_is_rejected() -> None:
    """Same `^tt\\d{7,8}$` pattern `Title.imdb_id` carries. An episode's
    IMDb id is a matcher input exactly as a title's is, and a source that
    emitted `"2178782"` without the prefix would otherwise be stored and
    never match anything."""
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Episode(
            title_id=TITLE_ID,
            season_id=SEASON_ID,
            season_number=1,
            episode_number=1,
            imdb_id="2178782",
        )


def test_both_models_are_frozen_and_evolve() -> None:
    season = Season(title_id=TITLE_ID, season_number=1)
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        season.season_number = 2  # type: ignore[misc]
    assert season.evolve(season_number=2).season_number == 2


def test_both_models_reject_an_unknown_field() -> None:
    """`extra="forbid"` from `DomainModel`. A typo'd keyword in the TMDb
    mapper (`episode_num=`) must fail at construction, not be dropped."""
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Season(title_id=TITLE_ID, season_number=1, episode_num=3)

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the models**

```python
# src/usher/domain/episode.py
"""The hierarchy under a series `Title`.

Neither model is a `Title` and neither carries a `TitleKind`: PRD 02 keeps
`TitleKind` at `movie | series` and hangs seasons and episodes off the
series. That is what makes episode watch state behave like title watch
state -- it attaches to canonical state rather than to a `MediaItem`, so it
survives the series becoming available on a second source or the first
source going away.

`Season.season_number` and `Episode.season_number` are both stored, and the
duplication is deliberate: an episode is looked up by
`(title_id, season_number, episode_number)` during ingest, before its
`Season` row is necessarily known, and a join to discover the number a
source already told us would be one query per episode across 999,827 of
them.
"""

import uuid
from datetime import UTC, date, datetime

from pydantic import AwareDatetime, Field

from usher.domain.base import DomainModel
from usher.domain.ids import new_id


class Season(DomainModel):
    """One season of a series.

    `season_number` may be `0`: TMDb numbers a series' specials as season 0
    and Emby emits `ParentIndexNumber: 0` for them, so a `ge=1` bound would
    silently drop every special in the library.
    """

    id: uuid.UUID = Field(default_factory=new_id)
    title_id: uuid.UUID
    season_number: int = Field(ge=0)

    name: str | None = None
    overview: str | None = None
    air_date: date | None = None
    episode_count: int | None = Field(default=None, ge=0)
    tmdb_id: int | None = None

    created_at: AwareDatetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(UTC))
    updated_at: AwareDatetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(UTC))


class Episode(DomainModel):
    """One episode. First-class -- it carries watch state and it is what a
    source actually holds -- but not independently searchable in v1
    (PRD 05).

    `absolute_number` is nullable and stays that way: it is TVDb's ordering
    concept, TMDb does not supply it, and an alternate-ordering provider is
    an explicitly post-v1 candidate (PRD 09).
    """

    id: uuid.UUID = Field(default_factory=new_id)
    title_id: uuid.UUID
    season_id: uuid.UUID
    season_number: int = Field(ge=0)
    episode_number: int = Field(ge=0)
    absolute_number: int | None = Field(default=None, ge=0)

    name: str | None = None
    overview: str | None = None
    air_date: date | None = None
    runtime_minutes: int | None = Field(default=None, ge=0)
    tmdb_id: int | None = None
    imdb_id: str | None = Field(default=None, pattern=r"^tt\d{7,8}$")

    created_at: AwareDatetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(UTC))
    updated_at: AwareDatetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(UTC))
```

## Task 5: `Job`, and the queue's vocabulary

PRD 08's job-reliability rules are three sentences that each pin a behaviour: exponential backoff with jitter and a per-job attempt counter; a poison threshold after which a job is *parked with its error*; and startup requeuing anything left in progress.

`JobStatus` has no `DONE` member. A completed job's row is deleted. Keeping 1.1M terminal rows to represent "nothing left to do" makes `usher.jobs.queued` a `WHERE status = 'pending'` count over a table that only grows, and PRD 10's queue-depth panel would need a partial index to stay usable. Deletion also makes redelivery trivially safe, which PRD 08 already requires of every job ("idempotent by construction").

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/domain/jobs.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_domain_jobs.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_domain_jobs.py
"""The queue's domain vocabulary."""

import uuid
from datetime import UTC, datetime

import pytest
from pydantic import ValidationError

from usher.domain.ids import new_id
from usher.domain.jobs import Job, JobKind, JobPriority, JobStatus


def test_a_job_is_pending_with_no_attempts_by_default() -> None:
    job = Job(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key=str(new_id()))
    assert job.status is JobStatus.PENDING
    assert job.attempts == 0
    assert job.last_error is None
    assert isinstance(job.id, uuid.UUID)


def test_there_is_no_done_status() -> None:
    """A completed job's row is deleted, not marked. Keeping 1.1M terminal
    rows makes PRD 10's queue-depth panel a scan over a table that only
    grows, and it is the reason `complete()` on the port returns nothing to
    inspect."""
    assert set(JobStatus) == {JobStatus.PENDING, JobStatus.RUNNING, JobStatus.PARKED}


def test_priorities_match_the_prd_table_and_higher_is_more_urgent() -> None:
    """PRD 03's read-through table. The direction is load-bearing: a queue
    ordered `ORDER BY priority` ascending would serve background backfill
    ahead of a title a client is waiting on, and the numbers alone do not
    say which way round the ORDER BY goes."""
    assert JobPriority.DEMAND == 100
    assert JobPriority.VISIBLE == 80
    assert JobPriority.NEW == 50
    assert JobPriority.BACKFILL == 20
    assert JobPriority.DEMAND > JobPriority.VISIBLE > JobPriority.NEW > JobPriority.BACKFILL


def test_a_priority_outside_the_scale_is_rejected() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Job(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="k", priority=101)
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Job(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="k", priority=-1)


def test_a_negative_attempt_count_is_rejected() -> None:
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Job(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="k", attempts=-1)


def test_an_empty_key_is_rejected() -> None:
    """`(kind, key)` is the dedup target. An empty key would collapse every
    job of a kind onto one row -- 1.1M match jobs becoming one."""
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        Job(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="")


def test_the_three_kinds_m4_ships() -> None:
    """`index` is deliberately absent: PRD 03 stage 4 writes a search
    document and an embedding, neither of which exists before M6, and a job
    kind whose handler is a stub is a queue that grows forever."""
    assert set(JobKind) == {JobKind.MATCH, JobKind.ENRICH, JobKind.WATCH_HISTORY}


def test_a_job_carries_a_traceparent_so_a_slow_title_is_one_query() -> None:
    """PRD 10: "why did the title I just opened take 45 seconds" is one
    query. The enqueue happens inside a request's span and the execution
    happens in a worker minutes later, so the only thing that joins them is
    the W3C trace context carried on the row."""
    job = Job(
        kind=JobKind.ENRICH,
        key="k",
        traceparent="00-d14524c7eba73194c64d589cdd69488a-770641a119523a53-01",
    )
    assert job.traceparent is not None


def test_a_job_is_frozen() -> None:
    job = Job(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="k")
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        job.attempts = 3  # type: ignore[misc]
    assert job.evolve(attempts=3, run_after=datetime.now(UTC)).attempts == 3
```

```python
# src/usher/domain/jobs.py
"""The priority work queue's domain vocabulary (PRD 03, PRD 08).

One row per outstanding unit of work, deduplicated on `(kind, key)`. A
completed job's row is **deleted** rather than marked -- hence no `DONE`
member on `JobStatus` -- because the queue's only two interesting
populations are "waiting" and "poisoned", and keeping a terminal row per
title would make PRD 10's `usher.jobs.queued` gauge a count over a table
that grows without bound.
"""

import uuid
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from enum import IntEnum, StrEnum

from pydantic import AwareDatetime, Field

from usher.domain.base import DomainModel
from usher.domain.ids import new_id


class JobKind(StrEnum):
    """What a worker does with a claimed job.

    `index` is deliberately absent. PRD 03's fourth stage updates a search
    document and computes an embedding; neither artefact exists before M6,
    which owns both. A kind whose handler is a stub is a queue that grows
    forever, and adding the member later is one line plus a backfill
    enqueue.
    """

    MATCH = "match"
    ENRICH = "enrich"
    WATCH_HISTORY = "watch_history"


class JobStatus(StrEnum):
    """A job is waiting, held by a worker, or poisoned.

    Not a ladder -- unlike `EnrichmentState` (ADR-0008) there is no "is this
    an improvement" comparison to get wrong -- so no rank mapping exists and
    none is needed.
    """

    PENDING = "pending"
    RUNNING = "running"
    PARKED = "parked"


class JobPriority(IntEnum):
    """PRD 03's read-through table, as named constants.

    **Higher is more urgent**, so every claim query orders
    `priority DESC, created_at ASC`. An `IntEnum` rather than a `StrEnum`
    because the column is an integer a `GREATEST()` runs over during
    promotion, and because the ordering has to be arithmetic rather than
    lexicographic -- which is the same trap `ENRICHMENT_RANK` exists for,
    avoided here by the type rather than by a side table.

    `DEMAND` and `VISIBLE` are unused in M4: nothing here serves a client.
    They are defined now because the promotion clause in the enqueue
    statement (`SET priority = GREATEST(...)`) is written in M4 and would
    otherwise be written against a scale that does not yet have a top.
    """

    DEMAND = 100  # a client opened this title right now (M5)
    VISIBLE = 80  # in a row the client just requested (M5)
    NEW = 50  # newly seen on a source
    BACKFILL = 20  # background sweep


class Job(DomainModel):
    """One outstanding unit of work.

    `key` is the kind's own identifier for the work -- a `Title.id` for
    `enrich`, a `MediaItem.id` for `match`, a `MediaItem.id` for
    `watch_history` -- as a string, so one column serves every kind without
    a polymorphic payload. `(kind, key)` is unique; enqueueing the same work
    twice promotes rather than duplicates.

    `traceparent` is a W3C trace context captured at enqueue time. The
    worker starts its span with a `Link` to it rather than as a child, since
    the request that enqueued the work has usually already returned -- a
    child span of a finished parent is a lie about causality, a link is
    not. PRD 10's "why did the title I just opened take 45 seconds" is that
    link followed backwards.
    """

    id: uuid.UUID = Field(default_factory=new_id)
    kind: JobKind
    key: str = Field(min_length=1)
    priority: int = Field(default=JobPriority.NEW, ge=0, le=100)
    status: JobStatus = JobStatus.PENDING
    attempts: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    run_after: AwareDatetime | None = None
    traceparent: str | None = None
    last_error: str | None = None

    created_at: AwareDatetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(UTC))
    updated_at: AwareDatetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(UTC))
```

## Task 6: `SyncRun`, and `MatchMethod`

`SyncRun` is what makes the availability sweep safe: the sweep is a statement that reads `started_at` off a run whose status is about to become `COMPLETED`, and it never runs at all for a run that ended `FAILED`. It is also PRD 02's `sync_runs` table ("per-source run bookkeeping: kind, cursor, status, stats") and PRD 10's dashboard-3 panel "sync run outcomes and duration".

`MatchMethod` goes in `usher.domain.enums` rather than in a port module because it is a label on PRD 10's `usher.match.result` counter, and `domain/enums.py`'s own docstring already says values there are stable wire identifiers.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/domain/sync.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/domain/enums.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_domain_sync.py`, `tests/unit/test_enums.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_domain_sync.py
"""Per-source run bookkeeping (PRD 02's `sync_runs`)."""

from datetime import UTC, datetime

import pytest
from pydantic import ValidationError

from usher.domain.ids import new_id
from usher.domain.sync import SyncRun, SyncRunKind, SyncRunStatus

SOURCE_ID = new_id()


def test_a_run_starts_running_with_zeroed_counters() -> None:
    run = SyncRun(source_id=SOURCE_ID, kind=SyncRunKind.FULL)
    assert run.status is SyncRunStatus.RUNNING
    assert (run.items_seen, run.items_matched, run.items_unmatched) == (0, 0, 0)
    assert run.items_retracted == 0
    assert run.finished_at is None
    assert run.error is None


def test_a_full_run_carries_no_cursor_and_a_delta_run_does() -> None:
    """A full walk is defined by having no `since`; a delta walk is defined
    by having one. Storing the cursor on the run is what lets the next delta
    start from the last *successful* one rather than from the last attempt."""
    cursor = datetime(2026, 7, 30, 3, 0, tzinfo=UTC)
    assert SyncRun(source_id=SOURCE_ID, kind=SyncRunKind.FULL).cursor_at is None
    delta = SyncRun(source_id=SOURCE_ID, kind=SyncRunKind.DELTA, cursor_at=cursor)
    assert delta.cursor_at == cursor


def test_the_three_kinds_a_run_can_have() -> None:
    assert set(SyncRunKind) == {SyncRunKind.FULL, SyncRunKind.DELTA, SyncRunKind.WATCH_STATE}


def test_negative_counters_are_rejected() -> None:
    for field in ("items_seen", "items_matched", "items_unmatched", "items_retracted"):
        with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
            SyncRun(source_id=SOURCE_ID, kind=SyncRunKind.FULL, **{field: -1})


def test_a_run_is_frozen_and_evolves() -> None:
    run = SyncRun(source_id=SOURCE_ID, kind=SyncRunKind.FULL)
    with pytest.raises(ValidationError):
        run.items_seen = 5  # type: ignore[misc]
    finished = run.evolve(
        status=SyncRunStatus.COMPLETED, items_seen=5, finished_at=datetime.now(UTC)
    )
    assert finished.items_seen == 5
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the model and the enum**

```python
# src/usher/domain/sync.py
"""Per-source sync bookkeeping (PRD 02's `sync_runs`).

One row per attempt, not one per source: unlike `ImportRun` (a checkpoint
updated in place) this is a history, because PRD 10's dashboard 3 plots
"sync run outcomes and duration" over time and because the availability
sweep's safety argument rests on being able to say *which* run last
finished cleanly.

`started_at` is the sweep's own input. A run marks every item it observes
with `last_seen_at >= started_at`; when -- and only when -- the walk returns
normally, everything with `last_seen_at < started_at` is retracted. A run
that raised never reaches that statement, which is the single property that
stops a flaky network from marking a healthy library unavailable.
"""

import uuid
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from enum import StrEnum

from pydantic import AwareDatetime, Field

from usher.domain.base import DomainModel
from usher.domain.ids import new_id


class SyncRunKind(StrEnum):
    """Which of PRD 03's reconciliation lanes this run is.

    `FULL` is the nightly walk with no `since`; `DELTA` is a walk from a
    stored cursor; `WATCH_STATE` walks `watch_state(since=…)` rather than
    `list_items`, because the two use different upstream filters
    (`MinDateLastSaved` vs `MinDateLastSavedForUser`, measured as genuinely
    different: 28,934 vs 29,005 items over the same 30-day window) and a
    single run kind could not record both cursors.
    """

    FULL = "full"
    DELTA = "delta"
    WATCH_STATE = "watch_state"


class SyncRunStatus(StrEnum):
    RUNNING = "running"
    COMPLETED = "completed"
    FAILED = "failed"


class SyncRun(DomainModel):
    """One attempt at reconciling a source.

    `cursor_at` is the `since` this run was started from -- `None` for a
    full walk. The *next* cursor is `started_at` (widened by the adapter's
    own one-second rule), and it is advanced only by a run that completed,
    which is why it is read off this table rather than kept in memory.
    """

    id: uuid.UUID = Field(default_factory=new_id)
    source_id: uuid.UUID
    kind: SyncRunKind
    status: SyncRunStatus = SyncRunStatus.RUNNING
    cursor_at: AwareDatetime | None = None

    items_seen: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    items_matched: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    items_unmatched: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)
    items_retracted: int = Field(default=0, ge=0)

    error: str | None = None
    started_at: AwareDatetime = Field(default_factory=lambda: datetime.now(UTC))
    finished_at: AwareDatetime | None = None
```

Append to `src/usher/domain/enums.py`:

```python
class MatchMethod(StrEnum):
    """How a source item was resolved to a canonical Title.

    PRD 03's stage-2 ladder, plus the two outcomes that ladder implies but
    does not name: creating a stub from a trusted provider id the catalog
    does not yet hold, and giving up.

    A label on PRD 10's `usher.match.result` counter, so these values are
    wire identifiers and stable. Ordered here by descending confidence,
    which is the order `MatchService` tries them in -- but nothing compares
    members, so unlike `EnrichmentState` there is no rank map and no trap.
    """

    TMDB_ID = "tmdb_id"
    IMDB_ID = "imdb_id"
    TVDB_ID = "tvdb_id"
    NAME_YEAR = "name_year"
    PROVIDER_SEARCH = "provider_search"
    CREATED_STUB = "created_stub"
    UNMATCHED = "unmatched"
```

and add `MatchMethod` to `tests/unit/test_enums.py`'s import.
