ADR-0015, `docs/prd/decisions/0015-availability-is-retracted-only-by-a-finished-walk.md`:

```markdown
# ADR-0015 — Availability is retracted only by a walk that finished, and never wholesale

**Status:** Accepted

## Context

PRD 03's nightly full reconcile is "walk the source; upsert everything; mark
unseen items `available = false`". `SourceAdapter.list_items` is contracted
to raise rather than truncate silently, and `usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py`
calls a lost item "the one failure this port exists to make impossible" --
because a generator that swallowed an error and stopped is indistinguishable
from one that finished, and the reconciler would retract everything it never
reached.

That guarantee only covers a walk that *fails*. A walk that **succeeds** and
returns far less than the library holds is a real and undetectable case: an
unmounted drive, a library an operator removed from the source by accident, a
permissions change on the account Usher authenticates as. Emby answers 200
with a short listing and there is nothing in the response that says why.

## Decision

Two separate mechanisms, both required.

1. **Retraction is a distinct call the reconciler makes only after the walk
   returns normally.** `upsert_many` never retracts as a side effect;
   `mark_unseen_unavailable(source_id, seen_since=run.started_at, …)` is
   reached only on the success path of `ReconcileService`. A run that raised
   records `FAILED` and sweeps nothing.
2. **The sweep refuses to retract more than `sync_max_retract_fraction` of a
   source in one run**, raising `AvailabilitySweepRefused` and changing
   nothing. Default `0.25`. `1.0` disables it, which is what an operator
   deliberately removing a library passes.

## Consequences

**Gained:** neither a transport failure nor a plausible source-side
misconfiguration can retract a library. A refusal is loud — it fails the sync
run, it is counted, and it names the two numbers an operator needs.

**Given up:** a genuine mass deletion (an operator really did delete 80% of a
library) needs a second, explicit run with the ceiling raised. Accepted: the
two outcomes are indistinguishable from inside Usher, one is reversible by
re-running a sync and the other is not, and asking once is cheap.

**Also:** availability goes *stale* rather than wrong when a source is
unreachable, which is exactly what PRD 08's failure table already promises
("Availability goes stale, not wrong").

## Evidence

`tests/contract/media_item_repository_contract.py::
test_marking_unseen_unavailable_refuses_to_retract_a_whole_library` asserts
the raise *and* that nothing changed. Mutation-tested six ways against the
real Postgres implementation (M4 plan, Task 10 Step 5), including the
division-by-zero the obvious spelling of the guard produces on an empty
source.
```

---

## Task 11: `WatchStateRepository` — the `COALESCE` that makes ADR-0014 real

The port change in Task 1 is only a promise until this exists. Port, contract, fake, and Postgres implementation land together, because the fake's version of the rule is correct *by accident* (Python's `if value is not None` is naturally `COALESCE`-shaped) and only the SQL is load-bearing.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/repository.py`
- Create: `tests/contract/watch_state_repository_contract.py`, `tests/fakes/watch_state_repository.py`
- Create: `src/usher/db/repositories/watch_state.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_watch_state_repository_contract.py`, `tests/integration/test_watch_state_repository.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the port**

Append to `src/usher/ports/repository.py`:

```python
class WatchStateRepository(ABC):
    """Persistence for watch state, and the inbound merge from a source.

    Same session/transaction ownership as `TitleRepository`: flushes, never
    commits.

    **`merge_from_source` is `COALESCE`-shaped, and that is a correctness
    property rather than an implementation detail.** `WatchStateMerge`'s
    `play_count`/`last_played_at` are `int | None`/`datetime | None`, where
    `None` means "the read that produced this could not determine it" and
    `0`/a datetime are positive claims. A source's *listing* frequently
    cannot determine them -- verified against Emby 4.9.5.0, where a listing
    reports `PlayCount: 0` for an item played twice -- so an implementation
    that wrote `None` through as `0`, or that wrote the DTO's default,
    replaces the household's real play history with zeros on every nightly
    walk, silently. ADR-0014.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    async def merge_from_source(self, merges: Sequence[WatchStateMerge]) -> int:
        """Apply inbound watch records, returning how many rows changed.

        Upserts on `(user_id, title_id)` or `(user_id, episode_id)` with
        `origin = source`.

        - `position_seconds`, `played` and `runtime_seconds` are always
          written.
        - `play_count` and `last_played_at` are written **only when not
          `None`**; `None` leaves the stored value exactly as it was.
        - A stored row whose `updated_at` is newer than `observed_at` is left
          alone entirely. PRD 03: "latest `updated_at` wins" -- a client that
          set a resume position thirty seconds ago knows more than a walk
          that started an hour ago, and without this the nightly reconcile
          stomps it.

        A merge naming neither a `title_id` nor an `episode_id`, or naming
        both, raises `PortDataMalformed` -- `WatchState`'s own model
        validator and the `num_nonnulls(title_id, episode_id) = 1` CHECK say
        the same thing, and a caller must not receive that as a raw storage
        exception.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def list_needing_history(
        self, *, limit: int = 500
    ) -> list[tuple[uuid.UUID, uuid.UUID | None, uuid.UUID | None]]:
        """`(user_id, title_id, episode_id)` for rows that are played but
        whose play count is unknown.

        "Unknown" is spelled `played AND play_count = 0`, because
        `watch_states.play_count` is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` and a walk that
        could not determine the count leaves the default in place. Slightly
        lossy -- an item genuinely played once whose history a source then
        cleared matches too -- and self-healing, because the backfill's
        single-item read is idempotent and Emby never leaves a played item
        at `PlayCount: 0`. ADR-0014 records the alternative (a nullable
        column) and why it was not taken.

        Bounded by `limit` and ordered oldest-first, because this is the
        queue-filling query for a backfill that costs one upstream request
        per row and must never be handed the whole library.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def get_for_title(
        self, user_id: uuid.UUID, title_id: uuid.UUID
    ) -> WatchState | None:
        """One user's state for one title, or None."""
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing contract suite**

```python
# tests/contract/watch_state_repository_contract.py
"""Behaviour every `WatchStateRepository` implementation must satisfy.

The suite that makes ADR-0014 real. `FakeWatchStateRepository` satisfies the
`COALESCE` cases by accident -- Python's `if value is not None` is naturally
that shape -- so the Postgres run is the one with teeth, and the mutation in
Task 11 Step 6 is what proves the SQL is load-bearing rather than decorative.
"""

import uuid
from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta

import pytest

from usher.domain.enums import WatchStateOrigin
from usher.ports.errors import PortDataMalformed
from usher.ports.ingest import WatchStateMerge

WALK_AT = datetime(2026, 7, 31, 3, 0, tzinfo=UTC)
LAST_PLAYED = datetime(2026, 7, 20, 21, 4, tzinfo=UTC)


def _merge(
    user_id: uuid.UUID,
    title_id: uuid.UUID | None,
    **overrides: object,
) -> WatchStateMerge:
    fields: dict[str, object] = {
        "user_id": user_id,
        "title_id": title_id,
        "episode_id": None,
        "position_seconds": 90,
        "played": False,
        "runtime_seconds": 7200,
        "observed_at": WALK_AT,
    }
    fields.update(overrides)
    return WatchStateMerge(**fields)  # type: ignore[arg-type]


class WatchStateRepositoryContract:
    async def test_a_first_merge_creates_the_row(self, repository, user_id, title_id) -> None:
        assert await repository.merge_from_source([_merge(user_id, title_id)]) == 1
        stored = await repository.get_for_title(user_id, title_id)
        assert stored is not None
        assert stored.position_seconds == 90
        assert stored.origin is WatchStateOrigin.SOURCE

    async def test_absent_play_history_leaves_a_stored_count_alone(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id
    ) -> None:
        """The whole point of ADR-0014, and the failure M4 was handed.

        A backfill recorded `play_count=7`. The next nightly walk cannot
        determine it -- Emby's listing reports `PlayCount: 0` for an item
        played twice -- so it merges `None`. An implementation that wrote
        `COALESCE(:play_count, 0)`, or that let the DTO's default reach the
        column, reads back `0` and the household's history is gone with
        nothing reporting it.
        """
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [_merge(user_id, title_id, played=True, play_count=7, last_played_at=LAST_PLAYED)]
        )
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [
                _merge(
                    user_id, title_id, played=True, position_seconds=1840,
                    observed_at=WALK_AT + timedelta(hours=1),
                )
            ]
        )
        stored = await repository.get_for_title(user_id, title_id)
        assert stored is not None
        assert stored.play_count == 7
        assert stored.position_seconds == 1840, "the fields the walk *can* determine still update"

    async def test_absent_last_played_at_leaves_a_stored_timestamp_alone(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id
    ) -> None:
        """Separate from the case above on purpose: a `COALESCE` fix applied
        to one column and not the other passes that one and fails here."""
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [_merge(user_id, title_id, played=True, play_count=7, last_played_at=LAST_PLAYED)]
        )
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [_merge(user_id, title_id, played=True, observed_at=WALK_AT + timedelta(hours=1))]
        )
        stored = await repository.get_for_title(user_id, title_id)
        assert stored is not None
        assert stored.last_played_at == LAST_PLAYED

    async def test_a_reported_zero_play_count_is_written(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id
    ) -> None:
        """Over-correcting the two cases above into "play_count is never
        written from a merge" makes a reset impossible to propagate -- the
        same correctness bug as filtering all-zero states out of a walk. A
        source that *can* count and reports zero is reporting a reset."""
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [_merge(user_id, title_id, played=True, play_count=7, last_played_at=LAST_PLAYED)]
        )
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [
                _merge(
                    user_id, title_id, played=False, play_count=0,
                    observed_at=WALK_AT + timedelta(hours=1),
                )
            ]
        )
        stored = await repository.get_for_title(user_id, title_id)
        assert stored is not None
        assert stored.play_count == 0
        assert stored.played is False

    async def test_a_merge_does_not_overwrite_a_newer_api_write(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id
    ) -> None:
        """PRD 03: "latest `updated_at` wins". A nightly walk started at 03:00
        must not stomp a resume position a client set at 03:20 -- and it
        would, because the walk's own data is an hour old by the time it
        reaches this row."""
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [_merge(user_id, title_id, position_seconds=3600, observed_at=WALK_AT)]
        )
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [_merge(user_id, title_id, position_seconds=10, observed_at=WALK_AT - timedelta(days=1))]
        )
        stored = await repository.get_for_title(user_id, title_id)
        assert stored is not None
        assert stored.position_seconds == 3600

    async def test_a_merge_rejects_a_state_attached_to_neither_target(
        self, repository, user_id
    ) -> None:
        with pytest.raises(PortDataMalformed):
            await repository.merge_from_source([_merge(user_id, None)])

    async def test_a_merge_rejects_a_state_attached_to_both_targets(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id, episode_id
    ) -> None:
        with pytest.raises(PortDataMalformed):
            await repository.merge_from_source(
                [_merge(user_id, title_id, episode_id=episode_id)]
            )

    async def test_a_played_item_with_no_known_history_is_listed_for_backfill(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id
    ) -> None:
        await repository.merge_from_source([_merge(user_id, title_id, played=True)])
        assert (user_id, title_id, None) in await repository.list_needing_history()

    async def test_an_unplayed_item_is_not_listed_for_backfill(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id
    ) -> None:
        """`list_needing_history` returning everything is a backfill of
        1,126,674 single-item requests."""
        await repository.merge_from_source([_merge(user_id, title_id, played=False)])
        assert await repository.list_needing_history() == []

    async def test_a_backfilled_item_stops_being_listed(
        self, repository, user_id, title_id
    ) -> None:
        """The predicate has to converge, or the backfill re-fetches the same
        rows forever. Emby's own `POST /Users/{u}/PlayedItems/{item}` never
        leaves a played item at `PlayCount: 0` (verified), so one successful
        backfill is enough."""
        await repository.merge_from_source([_merge(user_id, title_id, played=True)])
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [
                _merge(
                    user_id, title_id, played=True, play_count=2, last_played_at=LAST_PLAYED,
                    observed_at=WALK_AT + timedelta(hours=1),
                )
            ]
        )
        assert await repository.list_needing_history() == []

    async def test_episode_state_is_stored_against_the_episode(
        self, repository, user_id, episode_id
    ) -> None:
        await repository.merge_from_source(
            [_merge(user_id, None, episode_id=episode_id, played=True)]
        )
        assert (user_id, None, episode_id) in await repository.list_needing_history()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Run it to verify it fails, then write the fake and the implementation**

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher && uv run pytest tests/unit/test_watch_state_repository_contract.py -q 2>&1 | tail -3
```

Expected: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tests.fakes.watch_state_repository'`.

The fake stores `WatchState` domain models in a dict keyed on `(user_id, title_id, episode_id)`, applies `value if value is not None else stored.value` for the two history fields, skips a row whose stored `updated_at > observed_at`, and raises `PortDataMalformed` when `(title_id is None) == (episode_id is None)`. Its module docstring records the divergence: **the `COALESCE` cases pass here by accident, because Python's `is not None` guard is naturally that shape; only SQL can get it wrong.**

The Postgres implementation is one staged upsert per batch:

```python
_UPSERT_BY_TITLE = """
WITH deduped AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT ON (user_id, title_id) *
    FROM stg_watch_states WHERE title_id IS NOT NULL
    ORDER BY user_id, title_id, observed_at DESC
), upserted AS (
    INSERT INTO watch_states (
        id, user_id, title_id, episode_id, position_seconds, runtime_seconds,
        played, play_count, last_played_at, updated_at, origin
    )
    SELECT id, user_id, title_id, NULL, position_seconds, runtime_seconds,
           played, COALESCE(play_count, 0), last_played_at, observed_at, 'source'
    FROM deduped
    ON CONFLICT (user_id, title_id) DO UPDATE SET
        position_seconds = excluded.position_seconds,
        runtime_seconds = COALESCE(excluded.runtime_seconds, watch_states.runtime_seconds),
        played = excluded.played,
        -- The two COALESCEs ADR-0014 exists for. `deduped.play_count` is
        -- NULL when the read could not determine it, and NULL must leave the
        -- stored count exactly as it was; a reported 0 is a positive claim
        -- and overwrites. Note this reads `deduped`, not `excluded`:
        -- `excluded.play_count` has already been through the SELECT's own
        -- `COALESCE(play_count, 0)` above (which exists because the column
        -- is NOT NULL) and is therefore 0 rather than NULL by this point --
        -- reading it here is the single most natural way to write this
        -- clause and the single most damaging, because it silently zeroes
        -- every history on every walk. It is the mutation in Step 6.
        play_count = COALESCE(deduped.play_count, watch_states.play_count),
        last_played_at = COALESCE(deduped.last_played_at, watch_states.last_played_at),
        updated_at = excluded.updated_at,
        origin = 'source'
    WHERE watch_states.updated_at <= excluded.updated_at
    RETURNING 1
)
SELECT count(*) FROM upserted
"""
```

> **`ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` cannot reference a CTE by name in its `SET`
> clause** — only `excluded` and the target table are in scope. Verify this
> against real Postgres *before* writing the implementation:
> ```bash
> docker exec usher-m4 psql -U usher -d usher -c \
>   "WITH d AS (SELECT 1 AS a) INSERT INTO jobs (id, kind, key)
>    SELECT gen_random_uuid(), 'match', 'x' FROM d
>    ON CONFLICT (kind, key) DO UPDATE SET priority = d.a"
> ```
> If that errors with `missing FROM-clause entry for table "d"` (expected),
> the `COALESCE` must read a value that is still NULL in `excluded`. The way
> to get one is to **not** collapse it in the `SELECT`: insert
> `play_count` as `COALESCE(play_count, 0)` for the *insert* path but carry
> the raw value into a second column the conflict clause can read. Two
> workable spellings, pick the first:
> 1. Add `play_count_reported integer` and `last_played_reported timestamptz`
>    to the staging table, `SELECT ... , play_count AS play_count_reported`
>    into a matching *nullable* pair of columns on a temporary view, and read
>    `excluded.play_count_reported`. This requires those columns to exist on
>    `watch_states`, which they should not.
> 2. **Split the statement.** One `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` for
>    rows that do not exist, then one `UPDATE ... FROM deduped` for rows that
>    do — where `deduped.play_count` really is NULL and really is in scope.
>    Two statements per batch instead of one, both set-based, and the
>    `COALESCE` reads the value it must. Take this one.

Write it as the two-statement form, with that reasoning in the module docstring.

## Task 12: `JobQueue` — the port, the contract, the fake

PRD 08's job-reliability section is five bullets and each one is a contract case. The port's shape follows from two of them: a job is claimed for a duration rather than dequeued (so an unclean shutdown is recoverable), and a completed job's row is deleted (so the queue's size is its depth).

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/ports/jobs.py`
- Create: `tests/contract/job_queue_contract.py`, `tests/fakes/job_queue.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_job_queue_contract.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the port**

```python
# src/usher/ports/jobs.py
"""The priority work queue (PRD 03's read-through queue, PRD 08's job
reliability rules).

A driven port like every other, so `services/` depends on this ABC and never
on `usher.db` (ADR-0009). `usher.db.repositories.jobs.PostgresJobQueue` is
the concrete implementation, and it is the one place `SELECT … FOR UPDATE
SKIP LOCKED` appears.

Same session/transaction ownership as the repository ports: every method
flushes, none commits. That matters more here than elsewhere -- a worker
claims a job, does its work, and completes it, and the claim must be
committed before the work starts or a second worker sees an unclaimed job
while the first is running it. `JobWorker` commits between claim and work
for exactly that reason, and the port says so rather than leaving it to be
discovered.
"""

import uuid
from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass

from usher.domain.jobs import Job, JobKind


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class JobRequest:
    """Work to enqueue. Deliberately not a `Job`: a caller does not choose
    an id, a status, or an attempt count, and letting it hand over a whole
    `Job` would make "enqueue this as already-parked with nine attempts" a
    reachable state."""

    kind: JobKind
    key: str
    priority: int
    traceparent: str | None = None


class JobQueue(ABC):
    @abstractmethod
    async def enqueue(self, requests: Sequence[JobRequest]) -> int:
        """Add work, deduplicated on `(kind, key)`. Returns rows written.

        **Re-enqueueing existing work promotes it and never demotes it.** A
        second request for the same `(kind, key)` at a higher priority raises
        the stored priority; at a lower one it leaves it alone. That is what
        makes M5's demand promotion ("requesting an unenriched title promotes
        its job to the front of the queue") a priority update rather than a
        schema change, and it is what stops a background backfill sweep from
        demoting a job a client is waiting on.

        Re-enqueueing does **not** un-park a parked job. Poison that a human
        has not looked at is not fixed by asking for it again, and PRD 08's
        whole point is that parked work stays visible rather than
        recirculating.

        A batch may contain the same `(kind, key)` twice -- a walk can see
        the same item twice -- so an implementation deduplicates rather than
        assuming.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def claim(self, kinds: Sequence[JobKind], *, limit: int = 1) -> list[Job]:
        """Take up to `limit` runnable jobs, marking them `RUNNING`.

        Runnable means `status = pending` and `run_after` is null or in the
        past. Ordered **`priority` descending, then `created_at` ascending**:
        higher priority first (PRD 03's scale puts 100 at the top), oldest
        first within a priority so nothing starves.

        Two workers must never claim the same job. Against a SQL store that
        is `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; `FOR UPDATE` alone serialises the
        workers instead of distributing them, and a plain `SELECT` followed
        by an `UPDATE` hands the same row to both.

        The claim must be committed before the work starts -- see the module
        docstring.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def complete(self, job_id: uuid.UUID) -> None:
        """The work succeeded. **Deletes the row.**

        Not a status change: `JobStatus` has no `DONE` member, because the
        only two interesting populations are "waiting" and "poisoned" and a
        terminal row per title would make PRD 10's `usher.jobs.queued` gauge
        a count over a table that only grows. Redelivery is safe by
        construction (PRD 08), so losing the record of a success costs
        nothing.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def fail(self, job_id: uuid.UUID, *, error: str, retryable: bool) -> Job | None:
        """The work raised. Back it off, or park it.

        `retryable=False` **parks immediately**, whatever the attempt count.
        That is `PortDataMalformed`'s contract in queue form: "the upstream
        answered, and the answer was wrong. Retrying does not help." Backing
        it off five times first is five identical failures and a five-times
        longer wait before a human sees it.

        `retryable=True` increments `attempts` and sets `run_after` to an
        exponentially-backed-off, jittered instant -- **unless** `attempts`
        has reached the implementation's ceiling, in which case the job is
        parked with `error`. PRD 08: "after N attempts a job is *parked* with
        its error, not retried forever and not silently dropped."

        Returns the job as it now stands, so a caller can log or count the
        park, or `None` if the id is unknown (a worker whose claim was
        requeued out from under it by a restart).

        `error` is `str(exc)`, never the exception object and never a
        payload: PRD 08's credentials-never-logged rule applies to a column
        an operator reads.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def requeue_running(self, *, older_than_seconds: float = 0.0) -> int:
        """Return claimed-but-unfinished jobs to `pending`. Returns how many.

        PRD 08: "Startup requeues anything left `in_progress` by an unclean
        shutdown." Called once at worker startup with the default (everything
        currently `RUNNING`), which is correct when exactly one worker
        process exists -- the deployment shape M4 ships. `older_than_seconds`
        is there so a future multi-worker deployment can requeue only claims
        older than any plausible job, rather than stealing a live worker's
        work, and it is documented now because adding it later would mean
        changing every call site.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def depth(self) -> dict[JobKind, int]:
        """Pending count per kind, for PRD 10's `usher.jobs.queued` gauge.
        Always returns every `JobKind` as a key, `0` for an empty one -- a
        `GROUP BY` returns only non-empty kinds, and a gauge that stops
        reporting a series is indistinguishable from one reporting zero."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def parked(self, *, limit: int = 100) -> list[Job]:
        """Parked jobs, newest first. PRD 08: "Parked jobs are listed in the
        admin API and counted in metrics. Silent failure is the thing worth
        engineering against."""
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing contract suite**

```python
# tests/contract/job_queue_contract.py
"""Behaviour every `JobQueue` implementation must satisfy.

One case -- `test_two_workers_never_claim_the_same_job` -- needs two
genuinely concurrent sessions and is **skipped** unless the subclass sets
`requires_concurrency = True`. `FakeJobQueue` leaves it `False` and its run
claims nothing about locking, which is honest: a single-threaded dict cannot
express `SKIP LOCKED`, and a case that "passed" for it would ratify a plain
`SELECT` in the real implementation.
"""

import uuid
from datetime import UTC, datetime

import pytest

from usher.domain.jobs import JobKind, JobPriority, JobStatus
from usher.ports.jobs import JobRequest


class JobQueueContract:
    requires_concurrency: bool = False

    async def test_enqueued_work_is_claimable(self, queue) -> None:
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        assert [job.key for job in claimed] == ["t1"]
        assert claimed[0].status is JobStatus.RUNNING

    async def test_a_claim_only_takes_the_kinds_it_asked_for(self, queue) -> None:
        """A worker pool that runs only `enrich` must not claim and then
        abandon every `match` job in the queue."""
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="m1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        assert await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH]) == []

    async def test_a_job_is_claimed_by_priority_then_age(self, queue) -> None:
        """`ORDER BY priority DESC, created_at ASC`. Ascending priority
        serves background backfill ahead of a title a client is waiting on;
        no age tiebreak starves the oldest job at a given priority forever."""
        await queue.enqueue(
            [
                JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="old-low", priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL),
                JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="new-high", priority=JobPriority.DEMAND),
                JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="old-high", priority=JobPriority.DEMAND),
            ]
        )
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH], limit=3)
        assert [job.key for job in claimed][0] in {"new-high", "old-high"}
        assert [job.key for job in claimed][-1] == "old-low"

    async def test_completing_a_job_removes_it_from_the_queue(self, queue) -> None:
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        await queue.complete(claimed[0].id)
        assert await queue.depth() == dict.fromkeys(JobKind, 0)

    async def test_a_failed_job_is_retried_after_a_backoff(self, queue) -> None:
        """Re-claiming instantly turns one broken upstream into a hot loop
        against it."""
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        job = await queue.fail(claimed[0].id, error="upstream said no", retryable=True)
        assert job is not None
        assert job.status is JobStatus.PENDING
        assert job.attempts == 1
        assert job.run_after is not None and job.run_after > datetime.now(UTC)
        assert await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH]) == []

    async def test_a_job_is_parked_after_the_attempt_ceiling_with_its_error(self, queue) -> None:
        """PRD 08: "after N attempts a job is *parked* with its error, not
        retried forever and not silently dropped.""" ""
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        job = None
        for _ in range(10):
            claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH], limit=1)
            if not claimed:
                await queue.clear_backoff()  # test hook; see the fixture
                claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH], limit=1)
            if not claimed:
                break
            job = await queue.fail(claimed[0].id, error="upstream said no", retryable=True)
        assert job is not None
        assert job.status is JobStatus.PARKED
        assert job.last_error == "upstream said no"
        assert [parked.key for parked in await queue.parked()] == ["t1"]

    async def test_malformed_data_parks_immediately_rather_than_backing_off(self, queue) -> None:
        """`PortDataMalformed`'s own docstring: "the upstream answered, and
        the answer was wrong. Retrying does not help." An implementation that
        backs it off first produces five identical failures and delays a
        human seeing it by the whole backoff schedule. Asserting on
        `attempts == 1` is what distinguishes this from the ceiling park --
        that one reports the ceiling."""
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        job = await queue.fail(claimed[0].id, error="TMDb returned a list", retryable=False)
        assert job is not None
        assert job.status is JobStatus.PARKED
        assert job.attempts == 1

    async def test_a_parked_job_is_not_claimed(self, queue) -> None:
        """A claim query missing `status = 'pending'` retries poison forever
        -- which is the failure parking exists to prevent, restored."""
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        await queue.fail(claimed[0].id, error="bad", retryable=False)
        await queue.clear_backoff()
        assert await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH]) == []

    async def test_enqueueing_the_same_work_twice_does_not_duplicate_it(self, queue) -> None:
        """A nightly walk enqueues a match job per item. Without
        `(kind, key)` uniqueness the second night's walk adds 1,126,674 more
        on top of the first night's."""
        request = JobRequest(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="m1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)
        await queue.enqueue([request])
        await queue.enqueue([request])
        assert (await queue.depth())[JobKind.MATCH] == 1

    async def test_a_duplicate_inside_one_batch_is_tolerated(self, queue) -> None:
        request = JobRequest(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="m1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)
        await queue.enqueue([request, request])
        assert (await queue.depth())[JobKind.MATCH] == 1

    async def test_re_enqueueing_at_a_higher_priority_promotes_the_existing_job(
        self, queue
    ) -> None:
        """M5's demand promotion, mechanically. `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`
        makes it impossible without a schema change."""
        await queue.enqueue(
            [JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL)]
        )
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.DEMAND)])
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        assert claimed[0].priority == JobPriority.DEMAND

    async def test_re_enqueueing_at_a_lower_priority_does_not_demote(self, queue) -> None:
        """`SET priority = excluded.priority` lets a background backfill
        sweep demote the job a client is blocked on."""
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.DEMAND)])
        await queue.enqueue(
            [JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL)]
        )
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        assert claimed[0].priority == JobPriority.DEMAND

    async def test_re_enqueueing_does_not_unpark(self, queue) -> None:
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        await queue.fail(claimed[0].id, error="bad", retryable=False)
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.DEMAND)])
        await queue.clear_backoff()
        assert await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH]) == []

    async def test_requeue_running_recovers_jobs_from_an_unclean_shutdown(self, queue) -> None:
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        assert await queue.requeue_running() == 1
        assert [job.key for job in await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])] == ["t1"]

    async def test_depth_reports_every_kind_including_the_empty_ones(self, queue) -> None:
        """A `GROUP BY` returns only non-empty kinds, and a Prometheus gauge
        that stops reporting a series is indistinguishable from one reporting
        zero."""
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key="m1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        assert set(await queue.depth()) == set(JobKind)
        assert (await queue.depth())[JobKind.ENRICH] == 0

    @pytest.mark.skipif_no_concurrency
    async def test_two_workers_never_claim_the_same_job(self, queue, second_queue) -> None:
        """`FOR UPDATE` without `SKIP LOCKED` serialises the workers instead
        of distributing them; a plain `SELECT` followed by an `UPDATE` hands
        the same row to both, and the job runs twice.

        Skipped for any implementation that does not set
        `requires_concurrency` -- see the module docstring for why pretending
        otherwise would be worse than skipping.
        """
        if not self.requires_concurrency:
            pytest.skip("this implementation cannot express concurrent claims")
        await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
        first = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        second = await second_queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
        assert len(first) + len(second) == 1
```

`clear_backoff()` is a test-only hook: the contract needs to advance past a backoff without sleeping. Rather than putting it on the port (where nothing in `src/` would ever call it), define it on the two *test* subclasses — `FakeJobQueue.clear_backoff` sets every `run_after` to `None`, and the Postgres test fixture wraps the queue in a small subclass that runs `UPDATE jobs SET run_after = NULL`. Replace the `queue.clear_backoff()` calls above with a `clear_backoff` fixture the subclass supplies, so the contract stays free of a method the port does not have.

Expected: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'tests.fakes.job_queue'`.

The fake is a `dict[tuple[JobKind, str], Job]` plus the four rules — promote-never-demote on enqueue, `priority DESC, created_at ASC` on claim, delete on complete, and park-or-back-off on fail using a fixed (unjittered) schedule. Its docstring states the divergence: **single-threaded, so `test_two_workers_never_claim_the_same_job` is skipped and this run says nothing about locking**; and **its backoff is not jittered**, so nothing here catches a real implementation that computed the same instant for every retry and produced a thundering herd.

## Task 13: `PostgresJobQueue` — `SKIP LOCKED`, and a park that is one statement

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/db/repositories/jobs.py`
- Modify: `src/usher/config.py`
- Test: `tests/integration/test_job_queue.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`tests/integration/test_job_queue.py` subclasses `JobQueueContract` with `requires_concurrency = True`, a `queue` fixture over the shared `session`, and a `second_queue` fixture that opens **its own engine-bound session** — the shared `session` fixture is a single connection inside one rolled-back transaction, and two `PostgresJobQueue` instances over it would be one Postgres session and could not contend for a lock at all. This is the same shape `tests/integration/test_bootstrap_concurrency.py` already uses, and its module docstring is the precedent to copy.

Plus two Postgres-only cases:

```python
async def test_the_claim_query_uses_the_partial_index(session: AsyncSession) -> None:
    """`ix_jobs_claim` is `(priority DESC, created_at) WHERE status =
    'pending'`. A claim that sorts instead of scanning it is a sort over the
    whole queue on every single claim, and at a 1.1M-item backfill that is
    the difference between a worker and a bottleneck."""
    queue = PostgresJobQueue(session, max_attempts=5, backoff_seconds=1.0)
    await queue.enqueue(
        [JobRequest(kind=JobKind.MATCH, key=f"m-{index}", priority=JobPriority.NEW)
         for index in range(500)]
    )
    plan = "\n".join(
        (await session.execute(text(
            "EXPLAIN SELECT id FROM jobs WHERE status = 'pending' "
            "AND (run_after IS NULL OR run_after <= now()) "
            "ORDER BY priority DESC, created_at LIMIT 1 FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED"
        ))).scalars().all()
    )
    assert "ix_jobs_claim" in plan, plan


async def test_backoff_is_jittered(session: AsyncSession) -> None:
    """A fixed backoff makes every job that failed in the same batch retry
    at the same instant -- a thundering herd against the upstream that was
    already struggling. The fake's schedule is unjittered and cannot catch
    this; only the real one is asserted on."""
    queue = PostgresJobQueue(session, max_attempts=5, backoff_seconds=60.0)
    await queue.enqueue(
        [JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key=f"t-{index}", priority=JobPriority.NEW)
         for index in range(20)]
    )
    claimed = await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH], limit=20)
    instants = set()
    for job in claimed:
        failed = await queue.fail(job.id, error="upstream said no", retryable=True)
        assert failed is not None and failed.run_after is not None
        instants.add(failed.run_after)
    assert len(instants) > 1, "every retry landed on the same instant"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the implementation**

The four statements, each set-based:

```python
_ENQUEUE = """
INSERT INTO jobs (id, kind, key, priority, status, traceparent, created_at, updated_at)
SELECT DISTINCT ON (kind, key)
       id, kind, key, priority, 'pending', traceparent, now(), now()
FROM stg_jobs
ORDER BY kind, key, priority DESC
ON CONFLICT (kind, key) DO UPDATE SET
    -- GREATEST, never `excluded.priority`: re-enqueueing at a lower
    -- priority must not demote. A background backfill sweep runs over the
    -- whole catalog and would otherwise demote every job a client is
    -- waiting on, which is the exact inverse of what PRD 03's demand
    -- promotion is for.
    priority = GREATEST(jobs.priority, excluded.priority),
    traceparent = COALESCE(excluded.traceparent, jobs.traceparent),
    updated_at = now()
-- Parked work is not un-parked by asking for it again. PRD 08's whole
-- point is that poison stays visible rather than recirculating; a human
-- resolves it, and `attach_title`/a fixed upstream is what makes the retry
-- meaningful.
WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked'
"""

_CLAIM = """
WITH claimable AS (
    SELECT id FROM jobs
    WHERE status = 'pending'
      AND kind = ANY(:kinds)
      AND (run_after IS NULL OR run_after <= now())
    ORDER BY priority DESC, created_at
    LIMIT :limit
    -- SKIP LOCKED, not bare FOR UPDATE: without it a second worker blocks
    -- on the first worker's rows instead of moving past them, which turns a
    -- pool into a queue of one. Without FOR UPDATE at all, both workers
    -- read the same row and both run it.
    FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED
)
UPDATE jobs SET status = 'running', updated_at = now()
FROM claimable WHERE jobs.id = claimable.id
RETURNING jobs.*
"""

_FAIL = """
UPDATE jobs SET
    attempts = attempts + 1,
    last_error = :error,
    status = CASE
        WHEN NOT :retryable THEN 'parked'
        WHEN attempts + 1 >= :max_attempts THEN 'parked'
        ELSE 'pending'
    END,
    run_after = CASE
        WHEN NOT :retryable OR attempts + 1 >= :max_attempts THEN NULL
        -- Exponential with full jitter: the delay is a *uniform draw* from
        -- [0, base * 2^attempts], not that value exactly. Twenty jobs that
        -- failed in the same batch otherwise retry at the same instant --
        -- a thundering herd against an upstream that was already
        -- struggling. `random()` is evaluated per row by Postgres.
        ELSE now() + make_interval(
            secs => random() * :backoff_seconds * power(2, attempts)
        )
    END,
    updated_at = now()
WHERE id = :id
RETURNING *
"""

_REQUEUE = """
UPDATE jobs SET status = 'pending', updated_at = now()
WHERE status = 'running'
  AND updated_at <= now() - make_interval(secs => :older_than_seconds)
"""
```

The class takes `max_attempts: int` and `backoff_seconds: float` as constructor arguments rather than reading `Settings` — `db/` must not import `config` for the same reason `services/` must not, and the composition roots already pass every other tunable this way.

Add to `src/usher/config.py`:

```python
    # The ingest pipeline (PRD 03). Same reasoning as the bulk and source
    # settings above: PRD 08's TOML config layer does not exist yet.
    sync_batch_size: int = Field(default=1_000, ge=1, le=50_000)
    # The fraction of a source's items one reconcile may mark unavailable
    # before it refuses and changes nothing (ADR-0015). 1.0 disables the
    # guard, which is what an operator deliberately removing a library
    # passes on the command line.
    sync_max_retract_fraction: float = Field(default=0.25, ge=0.0, le=1.0)
    job_batch_size: int = Field(default=20, ge=1, le=500)
    job_max_attempts: int = Field(default=5, ge=1)
    job_backoff_seconds: float = Field(default=30.0, gt=0)
```

| Mutation | Expected failure |
|---|---|
| `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` → `FOR UPDATE` | `test_two_workers_never_claim_the_same_job` — **hangs** rather than failing; that is the point, and the run needs `--timeout`. If `pytest-timeout` is not installed, assert the failure by observing the hang and interrupting, and record it. |
| `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` → (removed entirely) | `test_two_workers_never_claim_the_same_job`, `assert 2 == 1` |
| `ORDER BY priority DESC` → `ORDER BY priority` | `test_a_job_is_claimed_by_priority_then_age` |
| `GREATEST(jobs.priority, excluded.priority)` → `excluded.priority` | `test_re_enqueueing_at_a_lower_priority_does_not_demote` |
| Drop `WHERE jobs.status <> 'parked'` from the enqueue | `test_re_enqueueing_does_not_unpark` |
| Drop the `WHEN NOT :retryable THEN 'parked'` arm | `test_malformed_data_parks_immediately_rather_than_backing_off` |
| `random() * :backoff_seconds * power(2, attempts)` → `:backoff_seconds` | `test_backoff_is_jittered` |
| Drop `AND status = 'pending'` from `_CLAIM` | `test_a_parked_job_is_not_claimed` |

## Task 14: The four remaining repositories

`EpisodeRepository`, `TitleMatchRepository`, `SyncRunRepository`, and `RawPayloadStore`. Smaller than the three above and written together because none of them has a decision in it that the others do not already settle — except `TitleMatchRepository`, whose whole reason to exist is that matching 1,126,674 items one query at a time is the design defect this milestone is warned about.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/repository.py`
- Create: `src/usher/db/repositories/episode.py`, `matching.py`, `sync.py`
- Create: `tests/contract/episode_repository_contract.py`, `tests/contract/title_match_repository_contract.py`
- Create: `tests/fakes/episode_repository.py`, `title_match_repository.py`, `sync_run_repository.py`, `raw_payload_store.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_episode_repository_contract.py`, `tests/unit/test_title_match_repository_contract.py`, `tests/integration/test_episode_repository.py`, `tests/integration/test_title_match_repository.py`, `tests/integration/test_sync_run_repository.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the ports**

```python
class TitleMatchRepository(ABC):
    """Batch lookups for PRD 03's match stage.

    Separate from `TitleRepository` because the shape is different in the
    way that matters: `TitleRepository.get_by_tmdb_id` answers one question,
    and a walk asks 1,126,674 of them. At ~0.1 ms per indexed point lookup
    that is minutes of pure round trips per sync, and the name+year tier is
    far worse -- measured at 300k rows, an unindexed name+year match
    seq-scans in 14.6 ms, which extrapolates to ~600 ms per item at the
    catalog's real 1,271,138.

    So every method here takes a batch and returns a mapping. `MatchService`
    turns one page of source items into three sets, issues three statements,
    and joins the answers in memory.

    Reads only. Same session-wide precondition as `TitleRepository`: the
    session must carry no unflushed, invalid state when these are called.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    async def match_by_provider_ids(
        self, refs: Sequence[ProviderRef]
    ) -> dict[ProviderRef, uuid.UUID]:
        """Resolve provider references to title ids, in one round trip.

        Keys absent from the result mean "no title carries this", which is a
        different answer from "not asked" -- so an implementation must never
        silently drop a ref it found nothing for, and a caller can iterate
        its own probes rather than the result.

        `ProviderRef.kind` is honoured where it is set and ignored where it
        is `None`. TMDb keys movies and series in overlapping integer spaces
        (26,968 shared ids, measured) so a TMDb ref without its kind names
        nothing; IMDb's namespace is global, so an IMDb ref carries none.
        ADR-0011.

        A ref whose `value` is not a valid integer for a provider whose
        column is an integer is skipped, not raised on: a source is free to
        report `ProviderIds.Tmdb: "unknown"` and that is a matching failure,
        not a pipeline failure.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def match_by_name_year(
        self, probes: Sequence[NameYearProbe]
    ) -> dict[NameYearProbe, uuid.UUID]:
        """PRD 03 stage 3: normalised name plus a year within +/-1, scoped by
        kind.

        Case-insensitive, via the same `lower(name)` the
        `ix_titles_name_lower_year` expression index is built on -- an
        implementation that lowercases in Python and compares against the raw
        column cannot use that index and seq-scans 1,271,138 rows per probe.

        **An ambiguous probe resolves to nothing.** Several titles sharing a
        name, a kind, and a year within one is common (remakes, and IMDb's
        own duplicate entries), and PRD 03 stage 5 is explicit that no
        *confident* match means the review queue. Picking the first row a
        scan reaches is a coin flip that attaches watch state to the wrong
        film.

        A probe with `year=None` resolves to nothing rather than matching on
        name alone -- a bare name is not an identity claim at a catalog of
        1.27M titles.
        """


class EpisodeRepository(ABC):
    """Persistence for the season/episode hierarchy under a series `Title`.

    Seasons and episodes are one aggregate here rather than two ports: an
    episode cannot exist without its season, both arrive from the same
    provider payload, and every write is a batch over one series.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    async def upsert_seasons(self, seasons: Sequence[Season]) -> BulkWriteResult:
        """Insert or update, keyed on `(title_id, season_number)`.
        Flushes, never commits. A `title_id` no title carries raises
        `RepositoryConflict` rather than a raw storage error."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def upsert_episodes(self, episodes: Sequence[Episode]) -> BulkWriteResult:
        """Insert or update, keyed on `(title_id, season_number,
        episode_number)`.

        Never overwrites a non-null field with a null one: ingest creates an
        episode from a source's own numbers alone (no name, no air date) and
        enrichment fills the rest in, and the next nightly walk must not
        blank what enrichment wrote. Same `COALESCE` rule
        `MediaItemRepository.upsert_many` applies to `title_id`, for the same
        reason.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def resolve(
        self, title_id: uuid.UUID, numbers: Sequence[tuple[int, int]]
    ) -> dict[tuple[int, int], uuid.UUID]:
        """`(season_number, episode_number)` -> episode id, in one round
        trip. 999,827 episodes means this cannot be a lookup per item."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def list_for_title(self, title_id: uuid.UUID) -> tuple[
        list[Season], list[Episode]
    ]:
        """Everything under one series. Used by enrichment to decide what
        changed, and by the CLI's report."""


class SyncRunRepository(ABC):
    """Per-source sync history. Flushes, never commits."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def add(self, run: SyncRun) -> None:
        """Insert. A duplicate id raises `RepositoryConflict`."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def save(self, run: SyncRun) -> None:
        """Update an existing run. An unknown id raises
        `RepositoryNotFound`."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def latest_completed_cursor(
        self, source_id: uuid.UUID, kind: SyncRunKind
    ) -> AwareDatetime | None:
        """`started_at` of the newest run of this kind that **completed**,
        or `None` if none has.

        Deliberately not "the newest run": a delta walk resuming from a run
        that failed halfway would skip everything that run never reached, and
        would do it silently. Reading only completed runs means a failure
        costs a re-walk of a window rather than a hole in the catalog.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    async def list_for_source(self, source_id: uuid.UUID, *, limit: int = 20) -> list[SyncRun]:
        """Newest first. PRD 10's dashboard 3 ("sync run outcomes and
        duration") and the CLI's `sync-status`."""


class RawPayloadStore(ABC):
    """The provider response cache (PRD 02's `raw_payloads`).

    **Providers only, never source items.** PRD 03's ingest stage previously
    said to store every source item's payload here; at 1,126,674 items and
    ~8 kB apiece that is ~9 GB against a database PRD 08 budgets at 8-12 GB
    total, to avoid a refetch that costs one request. ADR-0016.

    `fetched_at` is also the TMDb <=6-month cache-term clock (PRD 04's
    licensing constraint), which is why PRD 02's separate
    `provider_cache_meta` table is not created.
    """

    @abstractmethod
    async def get(self, provider: str, kind: str, reference: str) -> tuple[
        dict[str, Any], AwareDatetime
    ] | None:
        """The cached payload and when it was fetched, or None."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def put(
        self, provider: str, kind: str, reference: str, payload: dict[str, Any]
    ) -> None:
        """Store or replace, stamping `fetched_at` to now. Flushes, never
        commits."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def oldest_fetched_at(self, provider: str) -> AwareDatetime | None:
        """The compliance query: the oldest cache entry for a provider, which
        is what PRD 10's dashboard-5 panel plots against TMDb's 6-month
        ceiling."""
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing contract suites**

`tests/contract/title_match_repository_contract.py` — the five cases named in "What the new contract cases rule out", each seeding titles through a `catalog` fixture the subclass supplies:

```python
class TitleMatchRepositoryContract:
    async def test_provider_id_lookup_is_namespaced_by_kind(self, repository, catalog) -> None:
        """ADR-0011 in batch form. 26,968 TMDb ids are live in both spaces
        (measured), so an implementation keyed on the bare number returns one
        of the two arbitrarily -- and it is a coin flip which."""
        movie = await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, tmdb_id=550, name="Fight Club")
        series = await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.SERIES, tmdb_id=550, name="Rescue Me")
        resolved = await repository.match_by_provider_ids(
            [
                ProviderRef(provider="tmdb", value="550", kind=TitleKind.MOVIE),
                ProviderRef(provider="tmdb", value="550", kind=TitleKind.SERIES),
            ]
        )
        assert resolved[ProviderRef(provider="tmdb", value="550", kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)] == movie
        assert resolved[ProviderRef(provider="tmdb", value="550", kind=TitleKind.SERIES)] == series

    async def test_an_imdb_ref_is_global(self, repository, catalog) -> None:
        title = await catalog.given_title(
            kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, imdb_id="tt0111161", name="The Shawshank Redemption"
        )
        ref = ProviderRef(provider="imdb", value="tt0111161", kind=None)
        assert (await repository.match_by_provider_ids([ref]))[ref] == title

    async def test_a_batch_lookup_answers_every_probe_it_was_given(
        self, repository, catalog
    ) -> None:
        """An implementation that silently drops refs it found nothing for
        leaves the caller unable to tell "no match" from "not asked", and the
        review queue then fills with items that were matched."""
        await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, tmdb_id=550, name="Fight Club")
        known = ProviderRef(provider="tmdb", value="550", kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)
        unknown = ProviderRef(provider="tmdb", value="999999999", kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)
        resolved = await repository.match_by_provider_ids([known, unknown])
        assert known in resolved
        assert unknown not in resolved

    async def test_a_non_numeric_tmdb_ref_is_skipped_not_raised_on(
        self, repository, catalog
    ) -> None:
        """A source is free to report `ProviderIds.Tmdb: "unknown"`. That is
        a matching failure, not a pipeline failure, and an implementation
        that cast it straight into an integer column aborts a whole batch of
        5,000 items over one bad string."""
        ref = ProviderRef(provider="tmdb", value="unknown", kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)
        assert await repository.match_by_provider_ids([ref]) == {}

    async def test_name_year_lookup_accepts_a_year_within_one(
        self, repository, catalog
    ) -> None:
        """PRD 03 stage 3's "+/-1". Source and IMDb routinely disagree by one
        on a film released near a year boundary."""
        title = await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Arrival", year=2016)
        probe = NameYearProbe(name="Arrival", year=2017, kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)
        assert (await repository.match_by_name_year([probe]))[probe] == title

    async def test_name_year_lookup_is_case_insensitive_and_kind_scoped(
        self, repository, catalog
    ) -> None:
        movie = await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Fargo", year=1996)
        await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.SERIES, name="Fargo", year=2014)
        probe = NameYearProbe(name="fARGO", year=1996, kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)
        assert (await repository.match_by_name_year([probe]))[probe] == movie

    async def test_an_ambiguous_name_year_match_resolves_to_nothing(
        self, repository, catalog
    ) -> None:
        """Remakes, and IMDb's own duplicate entries. Picking whichever row a
        scan reaches first attaches the household's watch history to the
        wrong film, silently. PRD 03 stage 5: no *confident* match means the
        review queue."""
        await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="The Killers", year=1964)
        await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="The Killers", year=1964)
        probe = NameYearProbe(name="The Killers", year=1964, kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)
        assert probe not in await repository.match_by_name_year([probe])

    async def test_a_probe_with_no_year_resolves_to_nothing(
        self, repository, catalog
    ) -> None:
        """A bare name is not an identity claim at 1,271,138 titles."""
        await catalog.given_title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Solaris", year=1972)
        probe = NameYearProbe(name="Solaris", year=None, kind=TitleKind.MOVIE)
        assert probe not in await repository.match_by_name_year([probe])
```

`tests/contract/episode_repository_contract.py` covers: a round trip; the `(title_id, season_number, episode_number)` uniqueness; inserts and updates reported separately; `upsert_episodes` not blanking an enriched `name`/`air_date` with nulls; `resolve` answering a batch; and a `title_id` no title carries raising `RepositoryConflict` (the case the fake's docstring names as its own divergence, since a dict has no foreign keys).

- [ ] **Step 3: Run them to verify they fail, then write the fakes and implementations**

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher && uv run pytest tests/unit/test_title_match_repository_contract.py \
  tests/unit/test_episode_repository_contract.py -q 2>&1 | tail -3
```

Expected: `ModuleNotFoundError`.

The two Postgres implementations are one statement each. `match_by_provider_ids` unnests the batch and joins:

```python
_MATCH_PROVIDER = """
SELECT p.provider, p.value, p.kind, t.id
FROM unnest(
    CAST(:providers AS text[]), CAST(:values AS text[]), CAST(:kinds AS text[])
) AS p(provider, value, kind)
JOIN titles t ON (
    (p.provider = 'tmdb' AND t.tmdb_id = p.value::integer AND t.kind = p.kind)
    OR (p.provider = 'imdb' AND t.imdb_id = p.value)
    OR (p.provider = 'tvdb' AND t.tvdb_id = p.value::integer)
)
"""
```

> **`p.value::integer` casts every row, including the IMDb ones, unless the
> planner short-circuits — and it does not guarantee to.** Verified before
> writing this: a batch containing `('imdb', 'tt0111161', NULL)` alongside a
> TMDb ref raises `invalid input syntax for type integer: "tt0111161"`. Split
> the statement by provider — three small `WHERE … = ANY(:ids)` queries
> against three different indexes — rather than one clever join. Each uses
> its own index directly (`ix_titles_tmdb_id_kind`, `ix_titles_imdb_id`,
> `ix_titles_tvdb_id`), and non-numeric values are filtered in Python before
> they reach a bind parameter, which is also what
> `test_a_non_numeric_tmdb_ref_is_skipped_not_raised_on` requires.

`match_by_name_year` is one statement, and the shape is what makes it usable:

```python
_MATCH_NAME_YEAR = """
WITH probe AS (
    SELECT * FROM unnest(
        CAST(:names AS text[]), CAST(:years AS integer[]), CAST(:kinds AS text[])
    ) AS p(name, year, kind)
), candidate AS (
    SELECT p.name, p.year, p.kind, t.id, count(*) OVER (
        PARTITION BY p.name, p.year, p.kind
    ) AS matches
    FROM probe p
    -- lower(t.name), not lower(:name) against t.name: the index is
    -- `ix_titles_name_lower_year (lower(name), year)`, and an expression
    -- index is only usable when the query names the same expression.
    -- Measured at 300k rows: 14.6 ms seq scan without it, which is ~600 ms
    -- per probe extrapolated to this catalog's 1,271,138.
    JOIN titles t
      ON lower(t.name) = lower(p.name)
     AND t.kind = p.kind
     AND t.year BETWEEN p.year - 1 AND p.year + 1
)
SELECT name, year, kind, id FROM candidate WHERE matches = 1
"""
```

The `matches = 1` filter is what `test_an_ambiguous_name_year_match_resolves_to_nothing` pins: an ambiguous probe drops out entirely rather than picking a winner.

- [ ] **Step 5: Assert the plan, not just the rows**

The one thing a fake cannot catch here is a query that returns the right answer by seq-scanning 1,271,138 rows. Add to `tests/integration/test_title_match_repository.py`:

```python
async def test_name_year_matching_uses_the_expression_index(session: AsyncSession) -> None:
    """`ix_titles_name_lower_year` is on `(lower(name), year)`. A query that
    lowercases the *probe* instead of the column cannot use it, and the fake
    -- which matches on `name.lower()` in Python -- agrees with either
    spelling. Only the plan tells them apart."""
    repository = PostgresTitleMatchRepository(session)
    for index in range(200):
        await PostgresTitleRepository(session).add(
            Title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name=f"Movie {index}", sort_name=f"Movie {index}",
                  year=2000 + index % 20)
        )
    await session.flush()
    await session.execute(text("ANALYZE titles"))
    plan = "\n".join(
        (await session.execute(text(
            "EXPLAIN SELECT id FROM titles "
            "WHERE lower(name) = lower('Movie 7') AND kind = 'movie' "
            "AND year BETWEEN 2006 AND 2008"
        ))).scalars().all()
    )
    assert "ix_titles_name_lower_year" in plan, plan
```

> 200 rows is below the threshold at which Postgres prefers an index scan
> over a seq scan for many plans. If this assertion is flaky, raise the row
> count until the planner reliably chooses the index (500–2,000 is the
> expected range) and record the number it took in the test's docstring —
> a flaky plan assertion is worse than none.

## Task 15: `MatchService` — five tiers, one batch at a time

PRD 03 stage 2's ladder, plus the two outcomes it implies but does not name. The shape is the decision: **one batch in, one batch out, three statements in between.** A per-item matcher is correct and unusable at 1,126,674 items.

Two departures from PRD 03's list, both stated in the PRD in the same commit:

- **A `tvdb_id` tier is added, between IMDb and name+year.** M2's bootstrap linked 50,793 titles to a `tvdb_id`, and Emby series frequently carry `ProviderIds.Tvdb` without a TMDb one. It costs one more `WHERE tvdb_id = ANY(...)` against an index that already exists.
- **Tier 4 (TMDb search) does not run inline.** It is a network call per unmatched item, and a full walk produces unmatched items in the hundreds of thousands on a first run. Running it inside the walk makes the walk's duration a function of TMDb's rate limit. It is enqueued as a `match` job at `BACKFILL` priority instead, so the queue's own concurrency limit is what bounds it — which is what a priority queue is for.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/matching.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_services_matching.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_matching.py
"""The match stage, against port fakes. No network, no database.

Every case names the tier it exercises and the wrong implementation it
fails, because the ladder's whole value is that it stops at the first hit --
a matcher that tried every tier and took the best answer would resolve a
film by name when its own TMDb id said otherwise.
"""

import pytest

from tests.fakes.job_queue import FakeJobQueue
from tests.fakes.title_match_repository import FakeTitleMatchRepository
from tests.fakes.title_repository import FakeTitleRepository
from usher.domain.enums import EnrichmentState, MatchMethod, TitleKind
from usher.domain.ids import new_id
from usher.domain.title import Title
from usher.ports.source import SourceItem, SourceItemKind
from usher.services.matching import MatchService


def _item(external_id: str, **overrides: object) -> SourceItem:
    fields: dict[str, object] = {
        "external_id": external_id,
        "name": "Example Movie",
        "kind": SourceItemKind.MOVIE,
        "year": 2021,
        "provider_ids": {},
    }
    fields.update(overrides)
    return SourceItem(**fields)  # type: ignore[arg-type]


@pytest.fixture
def service() -> MatchService:
    return MatchService(
        titles=FakeTitleRepository(),
        matching=FakeTitleMatchRepository(),
        queue=FakeJobQueue(),
    )


async def test_a_tmdb_id_wins_over_everything_else(service: MatchService) -> None:
    """Tier 1. The item's name and year both point at a *different* title,
    seeded on purpose: a matcher that took the best of all tiers rather than
    the first hit would resolve this by name."""
    by_id = Title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Renamed", sort_name="Renamed", tmdb_id=438631)
    by_name = Title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Example Movie", sort_name="Example Movie", year=2021)
    await service._matching.given(by_id)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    await service._matching.given(by_name)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    outcomes = await service.match([_item("m1", provider_ids={"tmdb": "438631"})])
    assert outcomes[0].title_id == by_id.id
    assert outcomes[0].method is MatchMethod.TMDB_ID


async def test_an_imdb_id_is_the_second_tier(service: MatchService) -> None:
    title = Title(
        kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Anything", sort_name="Anything", imdb_id="tt1160419"
    )
    await service._matching.given(title)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    outcomes = await service.match([_item("m1", provider_ids={"imdb": "tt1160419"})])
    assert (outcomes[0].title_id, outcomes[0].method) == (title.id, MatchMethod.IMDB_ID)


async def test_a_tvdb_id_is_the_third_tier(service: MatchService) -> None:
    """M2 linked 50,793 titles to a tvdb_id, and Emby series routinely carry
    `ProviderIds.Tvdb` with no TMDb id at all -- so a ladder that stopped at
    IMDb would push most television to the review queue."""
    title = Title(kind=TitleKind.SERIES, name="Anything", sort_name="Anything", tvdb_id=121361)
    await service._matching.given(title)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    outcomes = await service.match(
        [_item("s1", kind=SourceItemKind.SERIES, provider_ids={"tvdb": "121361"})]
    )
    assert (outcomes[0].title_id, outcomes[0].method) == (title.id, MatchMethod.TVDB_ID)


async def test_name_and_year_are_the_fourth_tier(service: MatchService) -> None:
    title = Title(
        kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Example Movie", sort_name="Example Movie", year=2021
    )
    await service._matching.given(title)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    outcomes = await service.match([_item("m1")])
    assert (outcomes[0].title_id, outcomes[0].method) == (title.id, MatchMethod.NAME_YEAR)


async def test_a_trusted_provider_id_the_catalog_lacks_creates_a_stub(
    service: MatchService,
) -> None:
    """PRD 03's stub-on-sight. The catalog holds 1,271,138 titles but only
    291,737 with a `tmdb_id`, so a source item naming a TMDb id Usher has
    never seen is common rather than exotic -- and a TMDb or IMDb id is an
    identity claim strong enough to create a canonical title on."""
    outcomes = await service.match(
        [_item("m1", provider_ids={"tmdb": "999999", "imdb": "tt9999999"})]
    )
    assert outcomes[0].method is MatchMethod.CREATED_STUB
    assert outcomes[0].title_id is not None
    created = await service._titles.get(outcomes[0].title_id)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert created is not None
    assert created.enrichment_state is EnrichmentState.STUB
    assert created.tmdb_id == 999999
    assert created.imdb_id == "tt9999999"


async def test_an_item_with_no_usable_id_and_no_name_match_is_unmatched(
    service: MatchService,
) -> None:
    """PRD 03 stage 5. A bare name is not an identity claim, so this lands in
    the review queue rather than fabricating a title -- and it is *not*
    dropped, which PRD 02 states as a rule."""
    outcomes = await service.match([_item("m1", name="Unknown Thing", year=None)])
    assert outcomes[0].title_id is None
    assert outcomes[0].method is MatchMethod.UNMATCHED


async def test_an_unmatched_item_is_enqueued_for_a_remote_search(
    service: MatchService,
) -> None:
    """PRD 03's tier 4 is "TMDb search API as a last resort" -- one network
    call per unmatched item, and a first full walk produces those in the
    hundreds of thousands. Running it inline makes the walk's duration a
    function of TMDb's rate limit, so it is queued at BACKFILL priority and
    the queue's concurrency is what bounds it."""
    await service.match([_item("m1", name="Unknown Thing", year=None)])
    assert [job.key for job in await service._queue.claim([JobKind.MATCH])] == ["m1"]  # type: ignore[attr-defined]


async def test_a_stub_created_for_one_item_is_reused_by_the_next_in_the_same_batch(
    service: MatchService,
) -> None:
    """A movie and its two alternate cuts arrive in one page carrying the
    same TMDb id. A matcher that created a stub per item would produce three
    titles, two of which then fail on `ix_titles_tmdb_id_kind` -- or worse,
    succeed and fragment the watch history."""
    outcomes = await service.match(
        [
            _item("m1", provider_ids={"tmdb": "999999"}),
            _item("m2", provider_ids={"tmdb": "999999"}),
        ]
    )
    assert outcomes[0].title_id == outcomes[1].title_id


async def test_a_stub_that_loses_a_race_attaches_to_the_winner(
    service: MatchService,
) -> None:
    """`RepositoryConflict.constraint` exists for exactly this: two workers
    creating the same stub, one losing on `ix_titles_tmdb_id_kind`. The
    loser must look up the winner and attach, not fail the item -- the
    alternative is an item in the review queue whose title exists."""
    existing = Title(kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Winner", sort_name="Winner", tmdb_id=999999)
    await service._titles.add(existing)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    # The match repository does not know about it, so tier 1 misses and the
    # service tries to create -- which is the race, deterministically.
    outcomes = await service.match([_item("m1", provider_ids={"tmdb": "999999"})])
    assert outcomes[0].title_id == existing.id


async def test_matching_a_page_costs_a_bounded_number_of_lookups(
    service: MatchService,
) -> None:
    """The scale property, asserted rather than hoped for. 500 items must
    not be 500 round trips: the service issues one provider-id lookup and
    one name+year lookup per batch, whatever the batch holds."""
    for index in range(500):
        await service._matching.given(  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
            Title(
                kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name=f"Movie {index}", sort_name=f"Movie {index}",
                tmdb_id=index + 1,
            )
        )
    service._matching.reset_calls()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    outcomes = await service.match(
        [_item(f"m{index}", provider_ids={"tmdb": str(index + 1)}) for index in range(500)]
    )
    assert all(outcome.title_id is not None for outcome in outcomes)
    assert service._matching.calls <= 2, service._matching.calls  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
```

`FakeTitleMatchRepository` grows a `given(title)` seeding helper, a `calls` counter, and `reset_calls()`; those are test-double affordances, not port methods.
