```bash
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_services_matching.py -q 2>&1 | tail -3
```

Expected: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'usher.services.matching'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the service**

```python
# src/usher/services/matching.py
"""PRD 03 stage 2: resolve a source item to a canonical `Title`.

**One batch in, one batch out.** The ladder below is per-item logic, but
every *lookup* it performs is issued once per batch over a set: a page of
500 items costs one provider-id statement and one name+year statement, not
1,000 round trips. At the 1,126,674 items this deployment holds, the
per-item shape is not slow, it is a design defect -- which is why
`TitleMatchRepository` exists as a separate port from `TitleRepository`
rather than as three more methods on it.

The ladder, ordered by confidence and stopping at the first hit:

1. `provider_ids["tmdb"]` -> `(tmdb_id, kind)` (ADR-0011: the kind is not
   optional; 26,968 ids are live in both TMDb spaces).
2. `provider_ids["imdb"]` -> `imdb_id` (one global namespace, no kind).
3. `provider_ids["tvdb"]` -> `tvdb_id`. **Added to PRD 03's list**: M2
   linked 50,793 titles this way and Emby series routinely carry a TVDb id
   and no TMDb one, so a ladder stopping at IMDb pushes most television into
   the review queue for no reason.
4. Normalised name + year within +/-1, scoped by kind, **and only when
   unambiguous** -- several titles sharing a name, kind and year is common,
   and picking one attaches watch history to the wrong film.
5. A *trusted provider id the catalog does not hold* -> create a stub. This
   is PRD 03's "stub-on-sight", and it is deliberately narrower than the
   PRD's prose: an id from TMDb or IMDb is an identity claim strong enough
   to build a canonical title on, a bare name is not. The catalog holds
   1,271,138 titles and only 291,737 carry a `tmdb_id`, so this tier is the
   common path for anything modern, not an edge case.
6. Otherwise unmatched -- `title_id` stays NULL, the item is in the review
   queue (PRD 02: "Unmatched items are never dropped"), and a `match` job is
   enqueued at BACKFILL priority for tier 4's remote search.

**Why the remote search is queued rather than inline.** PRD 03 lists "TMDb
search API as a last resort" as part of this stage. It is one network call
per unmatched item; a first full walk against an unbootstrapped catalog
produces those in the hundreds of thousands, and running them inside the
walk makes the walk's duration a function of TMDb's rate limit rather than
of the source's. Queueing it is what the priority queue is for, and it means
a sync finishes tonight and the remote matches trickle in behind it.
"""

import uuid
from collections.abc import Sequence

from loguru import logger
from opentelemetry import metrics, trace

from usher.domain.enums import EnrichmentState, MatchMethod, TitleKind
from usher.domain.jobs import JobKind, JobPriority
from usher.domain.title import Title
from usher.ports.errors import RepositoryConflict
from usher.ports.ingest import MatchOutcome, NameYearProbe, ProviderRef
from usher.ports.jobs import JobQueue, JobRequest
from usher.ports.repository import TitleMatchRepository, TitleRepository
from usher.ports.source import SourceItem, SourceItemKind

_tracer = trace.get_tracer("usher.match")
_meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.match")
_result_counter = _meter.create_counter(
    "usher.match.result", unit="1", description="Source items resolved, by method"
)

# A source item's kind is narrower than a title's -- sources address
# episodes directly (`SourceItemKind`'s own docstring). An episode's
# canonical parent is its *series*, so an episode item matches as a series
# and `IngestService` hangs the episode off the result.
_TITLE_KIND: dict[SourceItemKind, TitleKind] = {
    SourceItemKind.MOVIE: TitleKind.MOVIE,
    SourceItemKind.SERIES: TitleKind.SERIES,
    SourceItemKind.EPISODE: TitleKind.SERIES,
}

# Ordered by descending confidence. `kind_scoped` is ADR-0011: TMDb keys
# movies and series separately and TVDb series ids are only ever series;
# IMDb's `tt` ids are one global namespace.
_PROVIDER_TIERS: tuple[tuple[str, MatchMethod, bool], ...] = (
    ("tmdb", MatchMethod.TMDB_ID, True),
    ("imdb", MatchMethod.IMDB_ID, False),
    ("tvdb", MatchMethod.TVDB_ID, True),
)


class MatchService:
    def __init__(
        self,
        titles: TitleRepository,
        matching: TitleMatchRepository,
        queue: JobQueue,
    ) -> None:
        self._titles = titles
        self._matching = matching
        self._queue = queue

    async def match(self, items: Sequence[SourceItem]) -> list[MatchOutcome]:
        """Resolve a batch. Returns one outcome per item, in order."""
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span("match.title") as span:
            span.set_attribute("usher.batch.items", len(items))
            refs = {
                item.external_id: self._refs_for(item) for item in items
            }
            by_ref = await self._matching.match_by_provider_ids(
                sorted({ref for item_refs in refs.values() for ref, _ in item_refs})
            )
            probes = {item.external_id: self._probe_for(item) for item in items}
            by_probe = await self._matching.match_by_name_year(
                sorted({probe for probe in probes.values() if probe is not None})
            )
            outcomes: list[MatchOutcome] = []
            # Stubs created earlier in this batch, so a film and its two
            # alternate cuts -- three items carrying one TMDb id, which is
            # what a multi-version library looks like -- produce one title
            # rather than three.
            created: dict[ProviderRef, uuid.UUID] = {}
            for item in items:
                outcomes.append(
                    await self._resolve(item, refs[item.external_id], by_ref, probes, by_probe, created)
                )
            await self._enqueue_remote_searches(outcomes)
            for outcome in outcomes:
                _result_counter.add(
                    1,
                    {
                        "method": outcome.method.value,
                        "confident": str(outcome.title_id is not None).lower(),
                    },
                )
            span.set_attribute(
                "usher.matched", sum(1 for o in outcomes if o.title_id is not None)
            )
            return outcomes

    def _refs_for(self, item: SourceItem) -> list[tuple[ProviderRef, MatchMethod]]:
        kind = _TITLE_KIND[item.kind]
        refs: list[tuple[ProviderRef, MatchMethod]] = []
        for provider, method, kind_scoped in _PROVIDER_TIERS:
            value = item.provider_ids.get(provider)
            if value:
                refs.append(
                    (
                        ProviderRef(
                            provider=provider, value=value, kind=kind if kind_scoped else None
                        ),
                        method,
                    )
                )
        return refs

    def _probe_for(self, item: SourceItem) -> NameYearProbe | None:
        # An episode is never matched by its own name and year: "Kissed by
        # Fire, 2013" would match a film of the same name, and the episode's
        # canonical parent is the series it belongs to, not itself.
        if item.kind is SourceItemKind.EPISODE or item.year is None:
            return None
        return NameYearProbe(name=item.name, year=item.year, kind=_TITLE_KIND[item.kind])

    async def _resolve(
        self,
        item: SourceItem,
        refs: list[tuple[ProviderRef, MatchMethod]],
        by_ref: dict[ProviderRef, uuid.UUID],
        probes: dict[str, NameYearProbe | None],
        by_probe: dict[NameYearProbe, uuid.UUID],
        created: dict[ProviderRef, uuid.UUID],
    ) -> MatchOutcome:
        for ref, method in refs:
            found = by_ref.get(ref)
            if found is not None:
                return MatchOutcome(external_id=item.external_id, title_id=found, method=method)
        probe = probes[item.external_id]
        if probe is not None:
            found = by_probe.get(probe)
            if found is not None:
                return MatchOutcome(
                    external_id=item.external_id, title_id=found, method=MatchMethod.NAME_YEAR
                )
        for ref, _ in refs:
            already = created.get(ref)
            if already is not None:
                return MatchOutcome(
                    external_id=item.external_id,
                    title_id=already,
                    method=MatchMethod.CREATED_STUB,
                )
        if refs:
            title_id = await self._create_stub(item, refs)
            for ref, _ in refs:
                created[ref] = title_id
            return MatchOutcome(
                external_id=item.external_id, title_id=title_id, method=MatchMethod.CREATED_STUB
            )
        return MatchOutcome(
            external_id=item.external_id, title_id=None, method=MatchMethod.UNMATCHED
        )

    async def _create_stub(
        self, item: SourceItem, refs: list[tuple[ProviderRef, MatchMethod]]
    ) -> uuid.UUID:
        """PRD 03's stub-on-sight: a canonical title from the source's own
        metadata, `enrichment_state = stub`, queryable immediately.

        Only reached when the item carries a provider id, so this never
        fabricates a title from a bare name -- see the module docstring.
        """
        known = {ref.provider: ref.value for ref, _ in refs}
        title = Title(
            kind=_TITLE_KIND[item.kind],
            name=item.name,
            # No normalisation: `Title.sort_name` has an explicit
            # no-normalisation contract, and inventing one here would be the
            # adapter-side convention that model deliberately refused.
            sort_name=item.name,
            year=item.year,
            tmdb_id=_as_int(known.get("tmdb")),
            imdb_id=known.get("imdb"),
            tvdb_id=_as_int(known.get("tvdb")),
            enrichment_state=EnrichmentState.STUB,
        )
        try:
            await self._titles.add(title)
        except RepositoryConflict as exc:
            # Two workers creating the same stub, or a title the match
            # repository's own read did not see. `RepositoryConflict.
            # constraint` exists for exactly this branch: without it, "this
            # id already exists, look it up" and "some *other* row holds one
            # of these provider ids" are the same exception and the same
            # message. Losing the race must attach to the winner -- the
            # alternative is an item sitting in a review queue whose title
            # exists.
            existing = await self._lookup_conflict(title, exc)
            if existing is None:
                raise
            logger.debug(
                "stub for {external_id} lost a race; attaching to {title_id}",
                external_id=item.external_id,
                title_id=existing,
            )
            return existing
        return title.id

    async def _lookup_conflict(self, title: Title, exc: RepositoryConflict) -> uuid.UUID | None:
        if title.tmdb_id is not None:
            found = await self._titles.get_by_tmdb_id(title.tmdb_id, title.kind)
            if found is not None:
                return found.id
        if title.imdb_id is not None:
            found = await self._titles.get_by_imdb_id(title.imdb_id)
            if found is not None:
                return found.id
        return None

    async def _enqueue_remote_searches(self, outcomes: Sequence[MatchOutcome]) -> None:
        unmatched = [
            JobRequest(
                kind=JobKind.MATCH,
                key=outcome.external_id,
                priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL,
                traceparent=_current_traceparent(),
            )
            for outcome in outcomes
            if outcome.title_id is None
        ]
        if unmatched:
            await self._queue.enqueue(unmatched)


def _as_int(value: str | None) -> int | None:
    """A source is free to report `ProviderIds.Tmdb: "unknown"`. That is a
    matching failure, not a pipeline failure, and it must not abort a batch
    of 5,000 items."""
    if value is None:
        return None
    try:
        return int(value)
    except ValueError:
        return None
```

`_current_traceparent()` is the two-line helper Task 24 introduces (`usher/telemetry.py`); until then it returns `None`. Write it in Task 24, and in this task import it as:

```python
from usher.telemetry import current_traceparent
```

adding to `src/usher/telemetry.py` now:

```python
def current_traceparent() -> str | None:
    """The active span as a W3C `traceparent`, or `None` outside a span.

    Carried on a job row so a worker's span can `Link` back to whatever
    enqueued the work -- PRD 10's "why did the title I just opened take 45
    seconds" spans a request and a background execution minutes later, and
    nothing else joins them. A `Link` rather than a parent, because the
    request has usually already returned and a child span of a finished
    parent misstates causality.
    """
    carrier: dict[str, str] = {}
    TraceContextTextMapPropagator().inject(carrier)
    return carrier.get("traceparent")
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Mutation-test the ladder**

| Mutation | Expected failure |
|---|---|
| Reorder `_PROVIDER_TIERS` to put `imdb` first | none of these cases — **write the case first**: seed a title under a TMDb id and a *different* one under an IMDb id, both on one item, and assert the TMDb one wins |
| Try name+year before provider ids | `test_a_tmdb_id_wins_over_everything_else` |
| Drop the `created` dict (create a stub per item) | `test_a_stub_created_for_one_item_is_reused_by_the_next_in_the_same_batch` |
| Drop the `RepositoryConflict` handler | `test_a_stub_that_loses_a_race_attaches_to_the_winner` |
| Create a stub when `refs` is empty (from name alone) | `test_an_item_with_no_usable_id_and_no_name_match_is_unmatched` |
| Move `match_by_provider_ids` inside the per-item loop | `test_matching_a_page_costs_a_bounded_number_of_lookups`, `assert 500 <= 2` |
| Drop the `_enqueue_remote_searches` call | `test_an_unmatched_item_is_enqueued_for_a_remote_search` |

The first row is the important one: it names a mutation the suite does **not** catch, and the fix is a new test, not a shrug.

## Task 16: `IngestService` — one batch of a walk

Everything that happens to one page of `SourceItem`s: match them, turn them into `MediaItemUpsert`s, attach episodes to their series, and enqueue enrichment for anything newly stubbed. No walk, no run lifecycle, no availability — those are Task 17, deliberately, so this is testable without an adapter.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/ingest.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_services_ingest.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_ingest.py
"""One batch of a walk, against port fakes."""

from datetime import UTC, datetime

import pytest

from tests.fakes.episode_repository import FakeEpisodeRepository
from tests.fakes.job_queue import FakeJobQueue
from tests.fakes.media_item_repository import FakeMediaItemRepository
from tests.fakes.title_match_repository import FakeTitleMatchRepository
from tests.fakes.title_repository import FakeTitleRepository
from usher.domain.enums import HdrFormat, TitleKind
from usher.domain.ids import new_id
from usher.domain.jobs import JobKind
from usher.domain.title import Title
from usher.ports.source import SourceItem, SourceItemKind
from usher.services.ingest import IngestService
from usher.services.matching import MatchService

SOURCE_ID = new_id()
RUN_AT = datetime(2026, 7, 31, 3, 0, tzinfo=UTC)

MOVIE = SourceItem(
    external_id="movie-1",
    name="Example Movie",
    kind=SourceItemKind.MOVIE,
    year=2021,
    provider_ids={"tmdb": "438631"},
    container="mkv",
    video_codec="hevc",
    hdr_format=HdrFormat.DOLBY_VISION,
    width=3840,
    height=2160,
    runtime_seconds=9360,
)
SERIES = SourceItem(
    external_id="series-1",
    name="Example Series",
    kind=SourceItemKind.SERIES,
    year=2011,
    provider_ids={"tvdb": "121361"},
)
EPISODE = SourceItem(
    external_id="episode-1",
    name="Kissed by Fire",
    kind=SourceItemKind.EPISODE,
    provider_ids={"imdb": "tt2178782"},
    container="mkv",
    series_external_id="series-1",
    season_number=3,
    episode_number=5,
)


@pytest.fixture
def service() -> IngestService:
    titles = FakeTitleRepository()
    matching = FakeTitleMatchRepository()
    queue = FakeJobQueue()
    return IngestService(
        matcher=MatchService(titles=titles, matching=matching, queue=queue),
        titles=titles,
        media_items=FakeMediaItemRepository(),
        episodes=FakeEpisodeRepository(),
        queue=queue,
    )


async def test_a_movie_becomes_a_media_item_carrying_its_quality_facts(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [MOVIE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    stored = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(SOURCE_ID, "movie-1")  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert stored is not None
    assert stored.hdr_format is HdrFormat.DOLBY_VISION
    assert stored.container == "mkv"
    assert stored.last_seen_at == RUN_AT
    assert stored.title_id is not None


async def test_every_item_in_a_batch_carries_the_runs_instant_not_its_own(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """The availability sweep compares `last_seen_at < run.started_at`. If
    each row stamped its own `now()`, a batch that took a second to write
    would have rows on both sides of the comparison and the sweep would
    retract items it had just seen."""
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [MOVIE, SERIES], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    for external_id in ("movie-1", "series-1"):
        stored = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(SOURCE_ID, external_id)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        assert stored is not None
        assert stored.last_seen_at == RUN_AT


async def test_a_newly_stubbed_title_is_enqueued_for_enrichment(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """PRD 03: "Newly added to a source" is priority 50. A stub that is never
    enqueued stays a stub forever and the read-through design does nothing."""
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [MOVIE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    enrich = await service._queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH], limit=10)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert len(enrich) == 1
    assert enrich[0].priority == 50


async def test_an_already_enriched_title_is_not_re_enqueued(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """A nightly walk sees every item every night. Enqueueing enrichment for
    all 1,126,674 of them each time makes the queue permanently the size of
    the library and starves everything else."""
    enriched = Title(
        kind=TitleKind.MOVIE, name="Example Movie", sort_name="Example Movie",
        year=2021, tmdb_id=438631, enrichment_state=EnrichmentState.ENRICHED,
    )
    await service._titles.add(enriched)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    await service._matching.given(enriched)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [MOVIE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    assert await service._queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH], limit=10) == []  # type: ignore[attr-defined]


async def test_an_episode_is_hung_off_its_series_title(service: IngestService) -> None:
    """The series is ingested first, in the same batch. `SourceItem` carries
    `series_external_id`, `season_number` and `episode_number` precisely so
    this is possible without a second upstream request."""
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [SERIES, EPISODE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    series_item = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(SOURCE_ID, "series-1")  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    episode_item = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(SOURCE_ID, "episode-1")  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert series_item is not None and episode_item is not None
    assert episode_item.title_id == series_item.title_id
    assert episode_item.episode_id is not None
    seasons, episodes = await service._episodes.list_for_title(series_item.title_id)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert [season.season_number for season in seasons] == [3]
    assert [(e.season_number, e.episode_number) for e in episodes] == [(3, 5)]


async def test_an_episode_whose_series_is_not_yet_known_is_left_unmatched(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """A walk is sorted by creation date, which offers no guarantee a series
    is seen before its episodes -- and an episode whose series arrives in a
    later page must not be dropped, nor attached to a guess. It goes to the
    review queue and is re-enqueued; the next batch or the next run resolves
    it."""
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [EPISODE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    stored = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(SOURCE_ID, "episode-1")  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert stored is not None
    assert stored.title_id is None
    assert stored.episode_id is None
    assert [job.key for job in await service._queue.claim([JobKind.MATCH], limit=10)] == [  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        "episode-1"
    ]


async def test_an_episode_finds_a_series_ingested_by_an_earlier_batch(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """The batch-boundary case the one above sets up. `resolve_series_titles`
    is a database read, not an in-batch dict, for exactly this."""
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [SERIES], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [EPISODE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    stored = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(SOURCE_ID, "episode-1")  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert stored is not None
    assert stored.title_id is not None
    assert stored.episode_id is not None


async def test_an_unmatched_movie_is_stored_rather_than_dropped(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """PRD 02: "Unmatched items are never dropped." A MediaItem with a NULL
    title_id is a legitimate, expected state and the review queue is what it
    is for."""
    orphan = SourceItem(
        external_id="orphan-1", name="Home Video 2004", kind=SourceItemKind.MOVIE
    )
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [orphan], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    unmatched = await service._media_items.list_unmatched(SOURCE_ID)  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    assert [item.external_id for item in unmatched] == ["orphan-1"]


async def test_ingesting_the_same_batch_twice_changes_nothing(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """PRD 03: "Four idempotent, resumable stages. Any stage can be re-run
    without duplicating work." A resumed sync replays the batch that was in
    flight when it died."""
    first = await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [SERIES, EPISODE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    second = await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, [SERIES, EPISODE], observed_at=RUN_AT)
    assert first.inserted == 2
    assert second.inserted == 0
    assert await service._media_items.count_for_source(SOURCE_ID) == 2  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the service**

```python
# src/usher/services/ingest.py
"""PRD 03 stage 1: one page of a walk, into canonical state.

Deliberately owns no walk and no run. `ReconcileService` drives the
adapter, batches, checkpoints, and decides whether the availability sweep
may run; this owns what happens to one batch once it exists. The split is
what makes every case in `tests/unit/test_services_ingest.py` runnable with
no adapter at all.

**`observed_at` is the run's start instant, passed in, never `now()`.** The
availability sweep retracts everything with `last_seen_at < run.started_at`,
so a batch whose rows stamped their own write time would straddle the
comparison and the sweep would retract items it had just seen. It is a
parameter rather than a default for that reason, and the contract case
`test_every_item_in_a_batch_carries_the_runs_instant_not_its_own` is what
keeps it one.

**Episodes are attached, never invented.** An episode's canonical parent is
its series' `Title`, and a walk sorted by creation date gives no guarantee
the series was seen first. An episode whose series is not yet matched is
stored unmatched and re-enqueued rather than dropped or guessed at -- PRD
02's "unmatched items are never dropped", applied to the 999,827 items that
are episodes.
"""

import uuid
from collections.abc import Sequence

from pydantic import AwareDatetime
from opentelemetry import metrics, trace

from usher.domain.enums import ENRICHMENT_RANK, EnrichmentState
from usher.domain.episode import Episode, Season
from usher.domain.ids import new_id
from usher.domain.jobs import JobKind, JobPriority
from usher.ports.ingest import MatchOutcome, MediaItemUpsert
from usher.ports.jobs import JobQueue, JobRequest
from usher.ports.repository import (
    BulkWriteResult,
    EpisodeRepository,
    MediaItemRepository,
    TitleRepository,
)
from usher.ports.source import SourceItem, SourceItemKind
from usher.services.matching import MatchService
from usher.telemetry import current_traceparent

_tracer = trace.get_tracer("usher.ingest")
_meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.ingest")
_items_counter = _meter.create_counter(
    "usher.ingest.items", unit="1", description="Source items ingested, by result"
)


class IngestService:
    def __init__(
        self,
        matcher: MatchService,
        titles: TitleRepository,
        media_items: MediaItemRepository,
        episodes: EpisodeRepository,
        queue: JobQueue,
    ) -> None:
        self._matcher = matcher
        self._titles = titles
        self._media_items = media_items
        self._episodes = episodes
        self._queue = queue

    async def ingest_batch(
        self, source_id: uuid.UUID, items: Sequence[SourceItem], *, observed_at: AwareDatetime
    ) -> BulkWriteResult:
        """Match, store, attach episodes, enqueue enrichment. Idempotent."""
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span("ingest.item") as span:
            span.set_attribute("usher.source_id", str(source_id))
            span.set_attribute("usher.batch.items", len(items))
            outcomes = {
                outcome.external_id: outcome for outcome in await self._matcher.match(items)
            }
            outcomes = await self._attach_episodes(source_id, items, outcomes)
            result = await self._media_items.upsert_many(
                [
                    self._upsert_for(source_id, item, outcomes[item.external_id], observed_at)
                    for item in items
                ]
            )
            await self._enqueue_enrichment(outcomes)
            await self._enqueue_rematches(outcomes)
            for outcome in outcomes.values():
                _items_counter.add(
                    1, {"source": str(source_id), "result": outcome.method.value}
                )
            span.set_attribute("usher.inserted", result.inserted)
            span.set_attribute("usher.updated", result.updated)
            return result

    async def _attach_episodes(
        self,
        source_id: uuid.UUID,
        items: Sequence[SourceItem],
        outcomes: dict[str, MatchOutcome],
    ) -> dict[str, MatchOutcome]:
        """Resolve each episode item's series, create its season and episode
        rows, and rewrite its outcome to carry both ids.

        One `resolve_series_titles` call per batch, not one per episode:
        999,827 of this deployment's items are episodes, and a per-item
        lookup here is the difference between a walk that finishes and one
        that does not.
        """
        episodes = [item for item in items if item.kind is SourceItemKind.EPISODE]
        if not episodes:
            return outcomes
        # A series in this very batch has an outcome but no `media_items`
        # row yet -- `upsert_many` has not run. Merge the two sources of
        # truth, in-batch first.
        in_batch = {
            item.external_id: outcomes[item.external_id].title_id
            for item in items
            if item.kind is SourceItemKind.SERIES
            and outcomes[item.external_id].title_id is not None
        }
        stored = await self._media_items.resolve_series_titles(
            source_id,
            [
                item.series_external_id
                for item in episodes
                if item.series_external_id and item.series_external_id not in in_batch
            ],
        )
        series_titles = {**stored, **{k: v for k, v in in_batch.items() if v is not None}}
        resolved = dict(outcomes)
        for item in episodes:
            title_id = series_titles.get(item.series_external_id or "")
            if title_id is None or item.season_number is None or item.episode_number is None:
                # Unmatched rather than guessed. The series may arrive in a
                # later page, or may itself be unmatched; either way this
                # item goes to the review queue and gets re-enqueued.
                resolved[item.external_id] = MatchOutcome(
                    external_id=item.external_id,
                    title_id=None,
                    method=MatchMethod.UNMATCHED,
                )
                continue
            season_id = await self._ensure_season(title_id, item.season_number)
            episode_id = await self._ensure_episode(title_id, season_id, item)
            resolved[item.external_id] = MatchOutcome(
                external_id=item.external_id,
                title_id=title_id,
                method=outcomes[item.external_id].method,
                episode_id=episode_id,
            )
        return resolved

    async def _ensure_season(self, title_id: uuid.UUID, season_number: int) -> uuid.UUID:
        season = Season(title_id=title_id, season_number=season_number)
        await self._episodes.upsert_seasons([season])
        found = await self._episodes.resolve_season(title_id, season_number)
        return found or season.id

    async def _ensure_episode(
        self, title_id: uuid.UUID, season_id: uuid.UUID, item: SourceItem
    ) -> uuid.UUID:
        assert item.season_number is not None and item.episode_number is not None
        episode = Episode(
            title_id=title_id,
            season_id=season_id,
            season_number=item.season_number,
            episode_number=item.episode_number,
            # The name is the only thing a source reliably has that
            # enrichment will later improve on. `upsert_episodes` never
            # blanks a non-null field with a null one, so a nightly walk
            # cannot undo an enriched overview.
            name=item.name,
            imdb_id=item.provider_ids.get("imdb"),
        )
        await self._episodes.upsert_episodes([episode])
        resolved = await self._episodes.resolve(
            title_id, [(item.season_number, item.episode_number)]
        )
        return resolved.get((item.season_number, item.episode_number), episode.id)

    def _upsert_for(
        self,
        source_id: uuid.UUID,
        item: SourceItem,
        outcome: MatchOutcome,
        observed_at: AwareDatetime,
    ) -> MediaItemUpsert:
        return MediaItemUpsert(
            source_id=source_id,
            external_id=item.external_id,
            title_id=outcome.title_id,
            episode_id=outcome.episode_id,
            container=item.container,
            video_codec=item.video_codec,
            audio_codec=item.audio_codec,
            width=item.width,
            height=item.height,
            hdr_format=item.hdr_format,
            audio_channels=item.audio_channels,
            file_size_bytes=item.file_size_bytes,
            runtime_seconds=item.runtime_seconds,
            added_at=item.added_at,
            last_seen_at=observed_at,
        )

    async def _enqueue_enrichment(self, outcomes: dict[str, MatchOutcome]) -> None:
        """Enqueue only titles that are not already enriched.

        A nightly walk sees all 1,126,674 items every night. Enqueueing
        enrichment for each one makes the queue permanently the size of the
        library and starves every demand-promoted job behind it -- and the
        `(kind, key)` uniqueness would collapse them to one per title, which
        merely turns "1.1M inserts" into "1.1M no-op upserts" every night.
        `ENRICHMENT_RANK`, never a direct comparison: `EnrichmentState` is a
        `StrEnum` and `ENRICHED > SKELETON` is `False` (ADR-0008).
        """
        title_ids = {
            outcome.title_id for outcome in outcomes.values() if outcome.title_id is not None
        }
        requests: list[JobRequest] = []
        traceparent = current_traceparent()
        for title_id in title_ids:
            title = await self._titles.get(title_id)
            if title is None:
                continue
            if ENRICHMENT_RANK[title.enrichment_state] >= ENRICHMENT_RANK[
                EnrichmentState.ENRICHED
            ]:
                continue
            requests.append(
                JobRequest(
                    kind=JobKind.ENRICH,
                    key=str(title_id),
                    priority=JobPriority.NEW,
                    traceparent=traceparent,
                )
            )
        if requests:
            await self._queue.enqueue(requests)

    async def _enqueue_rematches(self, outcomes: dict[str, MatchOutcome]) -> None:
        """An episode whose series was not yet known gets a `match` job so
        the next pass picks it up without waiting for the nightly walk.
        `MatchService` already enqueues one for every genuinely unmatched
        item, and `(kind, key)` uniqueness makes the overlap free."""
        requests = [
            JobRequest(
                kind=JobKind.MATCH,
                key=outcome.external_id,
                priority=JobPriority.BACKFILL,
                traceparent=current_traceparent(),
            )
            for outcome in outcomes.values()
            if outcome.title_id is None
        ]
        if requests:
            await self._queue.enqueue(requests)
```

`EpisodeRepository` gains `resolve_season(title_id, season_number) -> uuid.UUID | None` — add it to the port, the contract, both implementations and the fake in this task, since it is only discovered here.

> **Per-episode `upsert_seasons`/`upsert_episodes` calls are a scale defect
> and must not ship.** `_attach_episodes` above issues two writes and one
> read per episode — 999,827 × 3 round trips per walk. Restructure before
> committing: collect every `Season` and every `Episode` for the batch,
> issue **one** `upsert_seasons`, **one** `resolve_season` for the batch's
> `(title_id, season_number)` pairs, **one** `upsert_episodes`, and **one**
> `resolve`. The per-item form is written above only because it reads more
> clearly; `test_ingest_costs_a_bounded_number_of_writes_per_batch` (write
> it, mirroring `test_matching_a_page_costs_a_bounded_number_of_lookups`) is
> what makes the batched form mandatory rather than advisory.

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the scale case, then run**

```python
async def test_ingest_costs_a_bounded_number_of_writes_per_batch(
    service: IngestService,
) -> None:
    """500 episodes must not be 1,500 round trips. The batched form issues
    one season upsert, one season resolve, one episode upsert, one episode
    resolve, and one media-item upsert, whatever the batch holds."""
    series = [SERIES]
    episodes = [
        SourceItem(
            external_id=f"e{index}", name=f"Episode {index}", kind=SourceItemKind.EPISODE,
            series_external_id="series-1", season_number=1, episode_number=index,
        )
        for index in range(500)
    ]
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, series, observed_at=RUN_AT)
    service._episodes.reset_calls()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
    await service.ingest_batch(SOURCE_ID, episodes, observed_at=RUN_AT)
    assert service._episodes.calls <= 4, service._episodes.calls  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
```

## Task 17: `ReconcileService` — the run that is allowed to retract

The walk, the batching, the checkpoint, and the one decision this milestone is most warned about: **whether the availability sweep runs at all.**

`adapter.py`'s own comment calls a lost item "the one failure this port exists to make impossible". The port's half of that is `list_items` raising rather than truncating. This is the other half: the sweep is on the success path only, and even there it can refuse.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/reconcile.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_services_reconcile.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_reconcile.py
"""The reconcile lane, against port fakes and `FakeSourceAdapter`.

The three cases that matter are the three failure paths. A reconciler that
sweeps after a failed walk is the one bug the whole `list_items` contract
exists to prevent, restored one layer up.
"""

from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta

import pytest

from tests.fakes.source_adapter import FakeSourceAdapter
from usher.domain.sync import SyncRunKind, SyncRunStatus
from usher.ports.errors import PortUnavailable
from usher.ports.ingest import AvailabilitySweepRefused


async def test_a_full_walk_stores_everything_and_retracts_what_vanished(
    service, adapter, source
) -> None:
    adapter.seed(_item("a"), T0)
    adapter.seed(_item("b"), T0)
    first = await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    assert first.status is SyncRunStatus.COMPLETED
    assert first.items_seen == 2
    adapter.forget("b")
    second = await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    assert second.items_retracted == 1
    gone = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(source.id, "b")
    assert gone is not None and gone.available is False


async def test_a_walk_that_raises_sweeps_nothing(service, adapter, source) -> None:
    """**The failure this milestone is most warned about.** A generator that
    stops because the adapter gave up is indistinguishable from one that
    finished, which is why `list_items` is contracted to raise -- and that
    guarantee is worth exactly nothing if the reconciler sweeps either way.

    The seeded items are all still present on the source; only the transport
    failed. A reconciler that swept here marks a healthy 1,126,674-item
    library unavailable over one flaky request.
    """
    for index in range(7):
        adapter.seed(_item(f"m{index}"), T0)
    await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    adapter.fail_after(3)
    run = await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    assert run.status is SyncRunStatus.FAILED
    assert run.error is not None
    assert run.items_retracted == 0
    for index in range(7):
        stored = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(source.id, f"m{index}")
        assert stored is not None
        assert stored.available is True, f"m{index} was retracted by a walk that failed"


async def test_a_refused_sweep_fails_the_run_and_changes_nothing(
    service, adapter, source
) -> None:
    """The residual `list_items`' contract does not cover: a walk that
    *completes* and returns almost nothing. ADR-0015."""
    for index in range(10):
        adapter.seed(_item(f"m{index}"), T0)
    await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    for index in range(10):
        adapter.forget(f"m{index}")
    adapter.seed(_item("m0"), T0)
    run = await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    assert run.status is SyncRunStatus.FAILED
    assert "refusing to mark" in (run.error or "")
    assert run.items_retracted == 0
    stored = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(source.id, "m5")
    assert stored is not None and stored.available is True


async def test_a_delta_walk_uses_the_last_completed_cursor(service, adapter, source) -> None:
    """Resuming from the newest run of *any* status would skip everything a
    failed run never reached, silently. `latest_completed_cursor` is the
    method, and this is why."""
    adapter.seed(_item("a"), T0)
    completed = await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    adapter.fail_after(0)
    await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.DELTA)
    adapter.clear_failure()
    adapter.seed(_item("b"), T1)
    run = await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.DELTA)
    assert run.cursor_at == completed.started_at


async def test_a_delta_walk_never_sweeps(service, adapter, source) -> None:
    """A delta walk returns only what changed, so by construction almost
    everything is "unseen". Sweeping after one retracts the entire library
    -- and the guard would catch it, which would make every delta run fail
    rather than merely be wrong. Only a full walk may retract."""
    adapter.seed(_item("a"), T0)
    adapter.seed(_item("b"), T0)
    await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    adapter.seed(_item("a"), T1)
    run = await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.DELTA)
    assert run.items_retracted == 0
    stored = await service._media_items.get_by_external_id(source.id, "b")
    assert stored is not None and stored.available is True


async def test_a_run_checkpoints_every_batch(service, adapter, source) -> None:
    """1,126,674 items is hours. A run that recorded its counters only at
    the end tells an operator nothing while it is going, and PRD 10's
    dashboard-3 panel plots exactly those counters."""
    for index in range(7):
        adapter.seed(_item(f"m{index}"), T0)
    service._batch_size = 2
    await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
    assert service._commits >= 4


async def test_the_reconcile_span_is_a_child_of_whatever_is_active(
    service, adapter, source, span_recorder
) -> None:
    """M1 wired `FastAPIInstrumentor` specifically so a pipeline triggered by
    a request nests under that request's server span. A service that started
    a root span -- `tracer.start_span(..., context=Context())`, or work
    handed to a task created before the span existed -- throws that away and
    "what happened in this request" stops being answerable."""
    adapter.seed(_item("a"), T0)
    tracer = trace.get_tracer("test")
    with tracer.start_as_current_span("server") as server:
        await service.reconcile(source, SyncRunKind.FULL)
        expected_trace = server.get_span_context().trace_id
    pipeline = [span for span in span_recorder.finished if span.name == "sync.reconcile"]
    assert pipeline, [span.name for span in span_recorder.finished]
    assert pipeline[0].context.trace_id == expected_trace
    assert pipeline[0].parent is not None
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the service**

```python
# src/usher/services/reconcile.py
"""PRD 03's reconciliation lanes: the nightly full walk and the delta walk.

**The availability sweep is on the success path, and nowhere else.**
`SourceAdapter.list_items` is contracted to raise rather than truncate
precisely so a caller can tell "the library ended" from "the adapter gave
up"; `usher/adapters/emby/adapter.py` calls a lost item "the one failure
this port exists to make impossible". That guarantee is worth nothing unless
the thing that acts on it declines to act when the walk raised, so:

- a walk that raises records the run `FAILED` with its error and reaches no
  sweep at all;
- a walk that completes reaches the sweep, which may still refuse
  (ADR-0015) if it would retract more of the source than the configured
  ceiling;
- **only a `FULL` walk sweeps.** A delta walk returns only what changed, so
  by construction nearly everything is "unseen"; sweeping after one would
  retract the library, and the ADR-0015 guard would then make every delta
  run fail rather than merely be wrong.

Batches are committed as they go, with the run's counters, for the same
reason `BootstrapService` commits a batch and its cursor together: 1,126,674
items is hours, and a crash must cost the batch in flight rather than the
walk. Unlike bootstrap there is no mid-walk cursor to resume from -- the
port offers `since` and nothing finer -- so a crashed full walk is re-run
from the start, which is safe because every write is an upsert and the sweep
never ran.

`commit` is injected rather than a session being passed in: `services/` may
depend only on `domain/` and `ports/` (PRD 01, layering rule 2), and a
session is neither. Same shape `BootstrapService` already uses.
"""

import time
from collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable, Sequence
from datetime import UTC, datetime

from loguru import logger
from opentelemetry import metrics, trace

from usher.domain.source import Source
from usher.domain.sync import SyncRun, SyncRunKind, SyncRunStatus
from usher.ports.errors import UsherPortError
from usher.ports.ingest import AvailabilitySweepRefused
from usher.ports.repository import MediaItemRepository, SyncRunRepository
from usher.ports.source import SourceAdapter, SourceItem
from usher.services.ingest import IngestService

_tracer = trace.get_tracer("usher.reconcile")
_meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.reconcile")
_run_duration = _meter.create_histogram(
    "usher.sync.run.duration", unit="s", description="Wall time per sync run"
)


class ReconcileService:
    def __init__(
        self,
        ingest: IngestService,
        media_items: MediaItemRepository,
        runs: SyncRunRepository,
        commit: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]],
        *,
        batch_size: int = 1_000,
        max_retract_fraction: float = 0.25,
    ) -> None:
        self._ingest = ingest
        self._media_items = media_items
        self._runs = runs
        self._commit = commit
        self._batch_size = batch_size
        self._max_retract_fraction = max_retract_fraction

    async def reconcile(
        self, source: Source, kind: SyncRunKind, adapter: SourceAdapter
    ) -> SyncRun:
        """Walk `source` and reconcile it. Never raises a `UsherPortError`.

        Like `BootstrapService.import_dataset`, a failed run leaves a
        durable, inspectable record rather than a traceback: an operator
        running `usher sync` across three sources needs the second and third
        to run when the first is unreachable. Anything that is *not* a
        `UsherPortError` propagates untouched -- a bug here is not an
        upstream failure and must not be recorded as one.
        """
        started = time.perf_counter()
        cursor = (
            None
            if kind is SyncRunKind.FULL
            else await self._runs.latest_completed_cursor(source.id, kind)
        )
        run = SyncRun(source_id=source.id, kind=kind, cursor_at=cursor)
        await self._runs.add(run)
        await self._commit()
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span("sync.reconcile") as span:
            span.set_attribute("usher.source", source.name)
            span.set_attribute("usher.sync.kind", kind.value)
            try:
                run = await self._walk(source, run, adapter, cursor)
                # Reached only when the walk returned normally. The whole
                # safety argument is one `try` boundary wide.
                run = await self._sweep(run, kind)
                run = run.evolve(
                    status=SyncRunStatus.COMPLETED, finished_at=datetime.now(UTC)
                )
            except UsherPortError as exc:
                run = run.evolve(
                    status=SyncRunStatus.FAILED,
                    # str(exc), never the exception object and never a
                    # payload -- PRD 08's credentials-never-logged rule, and
                    # `error` is a Text column an operator reads.
                    error=str(exc),
                    finished_at=datetime.now(UTC),
                )
                span.set_attribute("usher.failed", True)
                logger.error(
                    "{kind} sync of {source} failed after {seen} items: {error}",
                    kind=kind.value,
                    source=source.name,
                    seen=run.items_seen,
                    error=str(exc),
                )
            await self._runs.save(run)
            await self._commit()
            span.set_attribute("usher.items_seen", run.items_seen)
            span.set_attribute("usher.items_retracted", run.items_retracted)
        _run_duration.record(
            time.perf_counter() - started,
            {"source": source.name, "kind": kind.value, "status": run.status.value},
        )
        return run

    async def _walk(
        self,
        source: Source,
        run: SyncRun,
        adapter: SourceAdapter,
        cursor: datetime | None,
    ) -> SyncRun:
        batch: list[SourceItem] = []
        async for item in adapter.list_items(since=cursor):
            batch.append(item)
            if len(batch) >= self._batch_size:
                run = await self._flush(run, batch)
                batch = []
        if batch:
            run = await self._flush(run, batch)
        return run

    async def _flush(self, run: SyncRun, batch: Sequence[SourceItem]) -> SyncRun:
        # `run.started_at`, not `now()`: every row in every batch of this run
        # carries the same `last_seen_at`, so the sweep's
        # `last_seen_at < started_at` comparison cannot straddle a batch the
        # walk has already written. See IngestService's docstring.
        await self._ingest.ingest_batch(
            run.source_id, batch, observed_at=run.started_at
        )
        unmatched = await self._media_items.list_unmatched(run.source_id, limit=1)
        run = run.evolve(items_seen=run.items_seen + len(batch))
        await self._runs.save(run)
        # One commit per batch, exactly like BootstrapService: a crash costs
        # the batch in flight, never the walk.
        await self._commit()
        return run

    async def _sweep(self, run: SyncRun, kind: SyncRunKind) -> SyncRun:
        """Retract availability -- full walks only, and only after one
        finished."""
        if kind is not SyncRunKind.FULL:
            return run
        try:
            result = await self._media_items.mark_unseen_unavailable(
                run.source_id,
                seen_since=run.started_at,
                max_retract_fraction=self._max_retract_fraction,
            )
        except AvailabilitySweepRefused as exc:
            logger.error(
                "availability sweep refused for source {source_id}: {error}",
                source_id=run.source_id,
                error=str(exc),
            )
            # Re-raised so `reconcile`'s own handler records it as a failed
            # run -- a refusal is not a successful reconcile with a footnote.
            # `AvailabilitySweepRefused` is a `UsherPortError`, so it lands
            # in the same branch a transport failure does.
            raise
        return run.evolve(items_retracted=result.retracted)
```

`FakeSourceAdapter` gains `clear_failure()` alongside its existing `fail_after`, for `test_a_delta_walk_uses_the_last_completed_cursor`.

```bash
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_services_reconcile.py -q
```

Expected: 7 passed.

- [ ] **Step 5: Mutation-test the safety argument**

The most important sweep in this milestone. Each of these is a plausible refactor.

| Mutation | Expected failure |
|---|---|
| Move `self._sweep(...)` into a `finally:` | `test_a_walk_that_raises_sweeps_nothing` — seven healthy items retracted |
| Move the sweep before `_walk` | `test_a_full_walk_stores_everything_and_retracts_what_vanished` |
| Drop the `if kind is not SyncRunKind.FULL` guard | `test_a_delta_walk_never_sweeps` |
| Catch `AvailabilitySweepRefused` and continue instead of re-raising | `test_a_refused_sweep_fails_the_run_and_changes_nothing` |

Where ADR-0014 becomes an actual behaviour: the walk merges what it can determine and leaves what it cannot, and the items it flagged as played-with-unknown-history get a queued single-item read.

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_watch_sync.py
"""Inbound watch state, against port fakes and a source adapter that lies
the way Emby's listing route does."""

async def test_a_walk_merges_position_and_played(service, adapter, source, user) -> None:
    adapter.seed(_item("movie-1"), T0)
    adapter.seed_state(
        SourceWatchState(external_id="movie-1", position_seconds=1840, played=False)
    )
    await service.sync(source, adapter, user_id=user.id)
    stored = await service._watch_states.get_for_title(user.id, _title_of("movie-1"))
    assert stored is not None
    assert (stored.position_seconds, stored.played) == (1840, False)


async def test_a_walk_that_cannot_report_history_leaves_it_alone(
    service, lossy_adapter, source, user
) -> None:
    """The end-to-end form of ADR-0014, through the service rather than the
    repository. `lossy_adapter` reports `play_count=None` from its walk and
    the real number from `get_watch_state`, which is Emby 4.9.5.0's measured
    behaviour."""
    await service.backfill_history(source, lossy_adapter, user_id=user.id)  # records 7
    await service.sync(source, lossy_adapter, user_id=user.id)
    stored = await service._watch_states.get_for_title(user.id, _title_of("movie-1"))
    assert stored is not None
    assert stored.play_count == 7


async def test_a_played_item_with_unknown_history_is_enqueued_for_backfill(
    service, lossy_adapter, source, user
) -> None:
    """The bounded recovery. Enqueued rather than fetched inline: one request
    per item against a library of 1,126,674 is not a walk, it is a week."""
    lossy_adapter.seed(_item("movie-1"), T0)
    lossy_adapter.seed_state(
        SourceWatchState(external_id="movie-1", position_seconds=0, played=True, play_count=7)
    )
    await service.sync(source, lossy_adapter, user_id=user.id)
    queued = await service._queue.claim([JobKind.WATCH_HISTORY], limit=10)
    assert [job.key for job in queued] == ["movie-1"]
    assert queued[0].priority == JobPriority.BACKFILL


async def test_an_unplayed_item_is_not_enqueued_for_backfill(
    service, lossy_adapter, source, user
) -> None:
    """1,126,674 items, of which the household has played a few thousand. An
    enqueue predicate that ignored `played` would queue the library."""
    lossy_adapter.seed(_item("movie-1"), T0)
    lossy_adapter.seed_state(
        SourceWatchState(external_id="movie-1", position_seconds=0, played=False)
    )
    await service.sync(source, lossy_adapter, user_id=user.id)
    assert await service._queue.claim([JobKind.WATCH_HISTORY], limit=10) == []

    assert stored.play_count == 7
    assert stored.last_played_at == LAST_PLAYED

    service, lossy_adapter, source, user
) -> None:
    """`get_watch_state` returns `None` for an item the source no longer
    has, exactly as `get_item` does. A backfill job for one must complete
    rather than park: the item's disappearance is what the reconcile lane
    handles, and parking here would fill the poison list with items that
    were simply deleted."""
    await service.backfill_one(
        source, lossy_adapter, external_id="never-existed", user_id=user.id
    )


async def test_a_state_for_an_unmatched_item_is_skipped_not_raised_on(
    service, adapter, source, user
) -> None:
    """A `WatchStateMerge` with neither a title nor an episode raises
    `PortDataMalformed` by contract, and an unmatched `MediaItem` produces
    exactly that -- which would abort a batch of 5,000 states over one item
    sitting in the review queue. The service filters them and counts them
    instead."""
    adapter.seed(_item("orphan-1"), T0)
    adapter.seed_state(
        SourceWatchState(external_id="orphan-1", position_seconds=90, played=False)
    )
    run = await service.sync(source, adapter, user_id=user.id)
    assert run.items_unmatched == 1
    assert run.status is SyncRunStatus.COMPLETED
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to verify it fails, then write the service**

The service walks `adapter.watch_state(since=cursor)` in batches, resolves each `external_id` to its `(title_id, episode_id)` through `MediaItemRepository`, drops the ones that resolve to neither (counting them as `items_unmatched`), builds `WatchStateMerge`s carrying the port's `play_count`/`last_played_at` **unchanged, including `None`**, and calls `merge_from_source`. After each batch it enqueues a `WATCH_HISTORY` job for every state whose `played` is true and whose `play_count` is `None`.

The run lifecycle is `ReconcileService`'s exactly — a `SyncRun` of kind `WATCH_STATE`, `latest_completed_cursor`, commit per batch, `FAILED` on a `UsherPortError` — and there is **no sweep**, because watch state is never retracted: PRD 08 lists it as the precious set that survives everything.

`backfill_one(source, adapter, external_id, user_id)` calls `adapter.get_watch_state(external_id)`, returns quietly on `None`, and merges the result. `backfill_history(...)` drains `WatchStateRepository.list_needing_history()` through it, bounded by its `limit`.

## Task 19: `JobWorker` — claim, run, park

The queue's consumer. Three properties beyond "it calls a handler": the claim is committed before the work starts, `PortDataMalformed` parks rather than backs off, and the worker's span **links** to the enqueueing span rather than parenting under it.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/services/jobs.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_services_jobs.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_services_jobs.py
"""The worker loop."""

async def test_a_handler_runs_and_the_job_is_removed(worker, queue) -> None:
    await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
    assert await worker.run_once() == 1
    assert (await queue.depth())[JobKind.ENRICH] == 0


async def test_the_claim_is_committed_before_the_work_starts(worker, queue, commits) -> None:
    """A worker that claims and works in one uncommitted transaction holds
    the row lock for the whole job -- so a second worker blocks rather than
    skipping, and `SKIP LOCKED` buys nothing. It also means a crash mid-job
    rolls the claim back and the job runs twice concurrently next time."""
    await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
    await worker.run_once()
    assert commits.before_handler >= 1


async def test_a_transient_failure_backs_the_job_off(worker, queue) -> None:
    worker.register(JobKind.ENRICH, _raises(PortUnavailable("upstream is down")))
    await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
    await worker.run_once()
    assert (await queue.depth())[JobKind.ENRICH] == 1
    assert await queue.parked() == []


async def test_malformed_data_parks_immediately(worker, queue) -> None:
    """`PortDataMalformed`: "the upstream answered, and the answer was wrong.
    Retrying does not help, so a caller parks the work rather than backing
    off." Five identical failures and a five-times-longer wait before a human
    sees it is the alternative."""
    worker.register(JobKind.ENRICH, _raises(PortDataMalformed("TMDb returned a list")))
    await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
    await worker.run_once()
    parked = await queue.parked()
    assert [job.key for job in parked] == ["t1"]
    assert parked[0].attempts == 1


async def test_a_bug_in_a_handler_is_not_recorded_as_an_upstream_failure(
    worker, queue
) -> None:
    """A `ZeroDivisionError` is not a `UsherPortError` and must propagate.
    Swallowing it into `fail(retryable=True)` turns a crash into a job that
    retries five times and then parks with a misleading error -- and the
    worker keeps running, so nothing is ever loud."""
    worker.register(JobKind.ENRICH, _raises(ZeroDivisionError("bug")))
    await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
    with pytest.raises(ZeroDivisionError):
        await worker.run_once()


async def test_startup_requeues_jobs_left_running(worker, queue) -> None:
    """PRD 08: "Startup requeues anything left `in_progress` by an unclean
    shutdown." Without it a killed worker's claims are invisible until a
    human notices the queue has stopped moving."""
    await queue.enqueue([JobRequest(kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW)])
    await queue.claim([JobKind.ENRICH])
    assert await worker.startup() == 1
    assert await worker.run_once() == 1


async def test_a_workers_span_links_to_whatever_enqueued_the_job(
    worker, queue, span_recorder
) -> None:
    """PRD 10: "why did the title I just opened take 45 seconds" is one
    query. The enqueue happens inside a request's span and the execution
    happens minutes later in a worker; a `Link` is what joins them.

    A *link*, not a parent: the request has already returned, and a child
    span of a finished parent misstates causality -- and would make the
    request's own trace grow a branch minutes after it ended.
    """
    tracer = trace.get_tracer("test")
    with tracer.start_as_current_span("server") as server:
        expected = server.get_span_context().trace_id
        await queue.enqueue(
            [
                JobRequest(
                    kind=JobKind.ENRICH, key="t1", priority=JobPriority.NEW,
                    traceparent=current_traceparent(),
                )
            ]
        )
    await worker.run_once()
    job_spans = [span for span in span_recorder.finished if span.name == "job.enrich"]
    assert job_spans
    assert job_spans[0].parent is None, "the worker's span is a root, not a child"
    assert [link.context.trace_id for link in job_spans[0].links] == [expected]
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to verify it fails, then write the worker**

```python
# src/usher/services/jobs.py
"""The queue's consumer (PRD 08's job-reliability rules).

Three properties that are not obvious from "it calls a handler":

1. **The claim is committed before the handler runs.** A worker that claims
   and works inside one transaction holds the row lock for the whole job, so
   a second worker blocks on it rather than skipping past -- which makes
   `SKIP LOCKED` buy nothing at all. It also means a crash mid-job rolls the
   claim back rather than leaving it recoverable by `requeue_running`.
2. **`PortDataMalformed` parks immediately.** Its own docstring: "the
   upstream answered, and the answer was wrong. Retrying does not help, so a
   caller parks the work rather than backing off." Every other
   `UsherPortError` backs off and parks at the ceiling.
3. **Anything that is not a `UsherPortError` propagates.** A bug in a
   handler is not an upstream failure; recording it as one turns a crash
   into five retries and a park whose message points at the wrong thing,
   with the worker still running and nothing ever loud.

The worker's span is a **root with a link** to whatever enqueued the job,
never a child. The enqueueing request has usually already returned, and a
child span of a finished parent misstates causality and grows a branch on a
closed trace.
"""

import time
import uuid
from collections.abc import Awaitable, Callable

from loguru import logger
from opentelemetry import metrics, trace
from opentelemetry.trace import Link
from opentelemetry.trace.propagation.tracecontext import TraceContextTextMapPropagator

from usher.domain.jobs import Job, JobKind
from usher.ports.errors import PortDataMalformed, UsherPortError
from usher.ports.jobs import JobQueue

Handler = Callable[[Job], Awaitable[None]]

_tracer = trace.get_tracer("usher.jobs")
_meter = metrics.get_meter("usher.jobs")
_job_duration = _meter.create_histogram(
    "usher.jobs.duration", unit="s", description="Wall time per job"
)
_propagator = TraceContextTextMapPropagator()


class JobWorker:
    def __init__(
        self,
        queue: JobQueue,
        commit: Callable[[], Awaitable[None]],
        *,
        batch_size: int = 20,
    ) -> None:
        self._queue = queue
        self._commit = commit
        self._batch_size = batch_size
        self._handlers: dict[JobKind, Handler] = {}

    def register(self, kind: JobKind, handler: Handler) -> None:
        self._handlers[kind] = handler

    async def startup(self) -> int:
        """PRD 08's "startup requeues anything left in_progress"."""
        requeued = await self._queue.requeue_running()
        await self._commit()
        if requeued:
            logger.warning("requeued {count} jobs left running by a previous process", count=requeued)
        return requeued

    async def run_once(self) -> int:
        """Claim and run up to `batch_size` jobs. Returns how many ran."""
        claimed = await self._queue.claim(list(self._handlers), limit=self._batch_size)
        # The commit that makes SKIP LOCKED mean something -- see the module
        # docstring. It has to happen between the claim and the first
        # handler, not after the loop.
        await self._commit()
        for job in claimed:
            await self._run(job)
        return len(claimed)

    async def _run(self, job: Job) -> None:
        started = time.perf_counter()
        links = _links_for(job)
        with _tracer.start_as_current_span(f"job.{job.kind.value}", links=links) as span:
            span.set_attribute("usher.job.kind", job.kind.value)
            span.set_attribute("usher.job.key", job.key)
            span.set_attribute("usher.job.attempts", job.attempts)
            try:
                await self._handlers[job.kind](job)
            except PortDataMalformed as exc:
                await self._park(job, exc, retryable=False)
            except UsherPortError as exc:
                await self._park(job, exc, retryable=True)
            else:
                await self._queue.complete(job.id)
                await self._commit()
        _job_duration.record(time.perf_counter() - started, {"kind": job.kind.value})

    async def _park(self, job: Job, exc: UsherPortError, *, retryable: bool) -> None:
        # str(exc): PRD 08's credentials-never-logged rule applies to a
        # column an operator reads and to this log line alike.
        outcome = await self._queue.fail(job.id, error=str(exc), retryable=retryable)
        await self._commit()
        logger.warning(
            "{kind} job {key} failed ({attempts} attempts): {error}",
            kind=job.kind.value,
            key=job.key,
            attempts=None if outcome is None else outcome.attempts,
            error=str(exc),
        )


def _links_for(job: Job) -> list[Link]:
    """A `Link` to the span that enqueued this job, if it recorded one.

    `extract` yields a `Context` holding a `NonRecordingSpan` with
    `is_remote=True` (verified against the installed SDK while planning),
    which is exactly what a `Link` wants. An unparseable or absent
    `traceparent` yields no link rather than raising -- a job is not worth
    failing over its own telemetry.
    """
    if not job.traceparent:
        return []
    context = _propagator.extract({"traceparent": job.traceparent})
    span_context = trace.get_current_span(context).get_span_context()
    return [Link(span_context)] if span_context.is_valid else []
```

## Task 20: Settle `MetadataProvider`'s three 🔶 markers

All three name M4 and all three blocked on "those models do not exist yet". `Season` and `Episode` now do (Task 4), and `ProviderRef` does (Task 8), so they can be settled rather than deferred again.

- **`to_title()` returns a `Title` but the stage populates six other entities.** Settled as `EnrichmentResult` — a frozen dataclass carrying `title`, `seasons`, `episodes`, and the verbatim `payload`. `people`/`credits`/`images`/`collection` are **not** fields, because nothing in M4 stores them and a field nothing writes is a placeholder. They are added by M7 and M9 as *added fields*, which `payload` makes free: the response that would have produced them is cached in `raw_payloads`, so those milestones re-derive without a second network call.
- **`fetch(provider_id: int)` bakes in TMDb's integer id scheme.** Settled as `fetch(ref: ProviderRef)`, which carries a string value and a kind and therefore already fits IMDb's `tt` ids and anything else.
- **`changed_since(days) -> list[int]` cannot express a resumable cursor** through TMDb's paginated, 14-day-capped changes feed. Settled as `changed_since(since, cursor) -> ChangedPage`, carrying the refs and the next cursor or `None` at the end — the same shape `BulkDataset.batches` already uses for the bulk importers, so the daily re-enrichment job is resumable the way a bootstrap is.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/usher/ports/metadata.py`
- Create: `tests/fakes/metadata_provider.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_ports_metadata.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
# tests/unit/test_ports_metadata.py
"""The metadata port's settled shape.

Every 🔶 marker in `usher/ports/metadata.py` that named M4 has an assertion
here, and each is written so reverting the corresponding production line
fails it.
"""

import inspect

from usher.domain.enums import TitleKind
from usher.domain.title import Title
from usher.ports.ingest import ProviderRef
from usher.ports.metadata import ChangedPage, EnrichmentResult, MetadataProvider


def test_enrichment_returns_the_aggregate_not_just_a_title() -> None:
    """The 🔶: `to_title` returned a `Title` while the stage populates a
    hierarchy. `seasons`/`episodes` are here because M4 stores them;
    `people`/`credits`/`images` are deliberately absent because nothing in
    M4 stores those and a field nothing writes is a placeholder."""
    fields = set(EnrichmentResult.__dataclass_fields__)
    assert fields == {"title", "seasons", "episodes", "payload"}


def test_the_verbatim_payload_travels_with_the_result() -> None:
    """What makes deferring `Person`/`Credit`/`Collection`/`Image` honest
    rather than lossy: the response that would have produced them is cached,
    so M7 and M9 re-derive them with no second network call. PRD 02's whole
    stated purpose for `raw_payloads`."""
    assert EnrichmentResult.__dataclass_fields__["payload"].type is not None


def test_fetch_takes_a_provider_ref_not_a_bare_integer() -> None:
    """The 🔶: `provider_id: int` baked in TMDb's id scheme, which IMDb's
    `tt1160419` does not fit -- and PRD 01 lists additional metadata
    providers as an open extension seam."""
    signature = inspect.signature(MetadataProvider.fetch)
    assert list(signature.parameters) == ["self", "ref"]
    assert signature.parameters["ref"].annotation == "ProviderRef"


def test_changed_since_is_resumable() -> None:
    """The 🔶: `days: int` in, `list[int]` out cannot express a cursor
    through TMDb's paginated, 14-day-capped changes feed, so a partial run
    had no way to pick up where it stopped."""
    signature = inspect.signature(MetadataProvider.changed_since)
    assert list(signature.parameters) == ["self", "since", "cursor"]
    assert set(ChangedPage.__dataclass_fields__) == {"refs", "next_cursor"}


def test_there_are_no_remaining_provisional_markers_naming_m4() -> None:
    """A 🔶 that says "settle in M4" and survives M4 is worse than one that
    names a later milestone -- it reads as settled to anyone who checks the
    roadmap rather than the source."""
    import usher.ports.metadata as module

    source = inspect.getsource(module)
    assert "M4" not in source or "settled in M4" in source
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it to verify it fails, then rewrite the port**

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class EnrichmentResult:
    """Everything one provider fetch yields for one canonical title.

    `seasons`/`episodes` are empty for a movie and populated for a series.
    They are here because M4 persists them -- 999,827 of the source library's
    1,126,674 items are episodes, so a result that could not carry the
    hierarchy would leave the pipeline unable to enrich 89% of what it holds.

    **`Person`, `Credit`, `Collection` and `Image` are deliberately not
    fields**, and that is not an oversight the way the 🔶 this settles was.
    Nothing in M4 stores them: `Person`/`Credit` are first read by M7's
    "more from this director" join, `Collection` by M7's franchise
    completeness, `Image` by M9's image proxy. A field nothing writes is a
    placeholder, and adding one now would mean either a table nobody queries
    or a `tuple()` that reads as "this provider has no cast".

    `payload` is what makes that deferral honest rather than lossy: the
    provider's full response, verbatim, on its way to `raw_payloads`. Each of
    those milestones re-derives its own entities from the cached payload with
    **no second network call**, which is exactly what PRD 02 says that table
    is for. Adding `people: tuple[Person, ...]` here later is an added field,
    not a signature change.
    """

    title: Title
    seasons: tuple[Season, ...]
    episodes: tuple[Episode, ...]
    payload: dict[str, Any]


@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
class ChangedPage:
    """One page of a provider's change feed, plus where to resume.

    `next_cursor` is `None` at the end. Opaque to the caller -- TMDb's is a
    page number, another provider's could be a token -- the same shape
    `usher.ports.bulk.BulkCursor` already gives the bulk importers, so the
    daily re-enrichment job is resumable the way a bootstrap is rather than
    restarting a 14-day window every time it is interrupted.
    """

    refs: tuple[ProviderRef, ...]
    next_cursor: str | None
```

`MetadataProvider` becomes:

```python
    @abstractmethod
    async def search(self, name: str, year: int | None) -> list[MetadataCandidate]: ...

    @abstractmethod
    async def fetch(self, ref: ProviderRef) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Full raw payload for one entity. Stored verbatim in
        `raw_payloads` and consumed only by `to_result`.

        Takes a `ProviderRef` rather than an `int`: the ref carries a string
        value and a kind, so it already fits IMDb's `tt1160419` and TMDb's
        `550`/`movie` alike -- the 🔶 this settles was that `provider_id:
        int` made a second provider a signature change.
        """

    @abstractmethod
    def to_result(self, payload: dict[str, Any], title_id: uuid.UUID) -> EnrichmentResult:
        """Normalise a raw payload into canonical state. See
        `EnrichmentResult` for what it does and does not carry, and why."""

    @abstractmethod
    async def changed_since(self, since: AwareDatetime, cursor: str | None) -> ChangedPage:
        """One page of entities mutated since `since`, plus where to resume.

        TMDb's `/movie/changes` feed is paginated and capped at a 14-day
        window; `days: int` in and `list[int]` out could not express a
        resumable position through it, which is the 🔶 this settles.
        """
```

```bash
uv run pytest tests/unit/test_ports_metadata.py -q && uv run mypy src tests
```

## Task 21: `TmdbMetadataProvider`

The one implementation. Movie and TV divergence stops here (`title`/`name`, `release_date`/`first_air_date`) — `MetadataCandidate`'s own docstring already promises that, and it is why the matcher never indexes into a provider's JSON keys.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/usher/adapters/tmdb/{__init__,client,mapping,provider}.py`
- Create: `tests/fixtures/tmdb/{movie,series,season}.json`
- Modify: `src/usher/config.py`, `src/usher/adapters/factory.py`
- Test: `tests/unit/test_adapters_tmdb_{client,mapping,provider}.py`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Cover, each over `httpx.MockTransport` with no network:

- `append_to_response=credits,keywords,images,videos,external_ids,release_dates` is sent (PRD 03 names the exact list; a provider that made six requests instead of one burns the rate limit six times as fast).
- A movie payload maps `title`/`release_date`; a series payload maps `name`/`first_air_date`. **Both from the same method**, because a caller that had to choose would be indexing into TMDb's keys.
- `genres` arrive as `[{"id": …, "name": …}]` and land as a `tuple[str, ...]`; `keywords` are nested under `keywords.keywords` for movies and `keywords.results` for TV — a real divergence, and a mapper that handled one produces empty keywords for half the catalog.
- `content_rating` comes from `release_dates` for movies and `content_ratings` for TV, filtered to the configured region.
- Seasons and episodes are produced for a series and empty for a movie.
- A 429 raises `PortRateLimited` carrying `retry_after` from the header (reusing `usher.adapters.http.retry_after_seconds`, already extracted in M3).
- A 404 raises `PortDataMalformed`, **not** `PortUnavailable`: a TMDb id the catalog holds that TMDb no longer serves is a wrong answer, not an outage, and retrying it five times before parking wastes the rate limit. This is the branch that makes `JobWorker`'s park-immediately path fire in production.
- A payload with no `id` raises `PortDataMalformed`.
- The throttle: N concurrent `fetch` calls issue no more than `tmdb_requests_per_second` per second, asserted against an injected clock rather than by sleeping.
- `to_result` never sets `enrichment_state` itself — `EnrichService` does, and a provider that set it could downgrade an already-enriched title.

Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/tmdb/` are **shape-recorded and value-synthetic**, for the same licensing reason `tests/fixtures/emby/` is: TMDb's terms forbid redistributing its metadata, and `CLAUDE.md`'s "ship importers, never data" forbids committing it. Add `scripts/capture_tmdb_fixture.py` alongside `capture_emby_fixture.py` — same shape, same "not a test, output never committed" module docstring.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run to verify they fail, then write the adapter**

`TmdbClient` owns one `httpx.AsyncClient`, the API key, a token-bucket throttle taking `time.monotonic` as an injected callable, and error translation. `mapping.py` is pure functions over `Mapping[str, Any]`, mirroring `adapters/emby/mapping.py`. `provider.py` implements the port over the two.

Add to `src/usher/config.py`:

```python
    tmdb_base_url: str = "https://api.themoviedb.org/3"
    # PRD 10's dashboard 3 plots "TMDb requests/sec against the ~40 ceiling
    # with 429 count". 30 leaves headroom for the retry a 429 triggers
    # without the retry itself becoming the thing that trips the next one.
    tmdb_requests_per_second: float = Field(default=30.0, gt=0)
    # Which certification body's rating lands in `Title.content_rating`.
    # TMDb returns every country's; picking one is configuration, not a
    # constant, because a household outside the US wants its own.
    tmdb_region: str = Field(default="US", min_length=2, max_length=2)
```

Where the queue meets the pipeline. Three handlers, one per `JobKind`, each a thin adapter between a `Job` and a service call — so the worker knows nothing about TMDb and the services know nothing about the queue's shape.

```python
async def test_enriching_promotes_a_stub_to_enriched(service, titles, provider) -> None:
```
