```python
    assert stored.enriched_at is not None
    assert stored.overview


async def test_a_failed_enrichment_records_the_error_and_keeps_the_tier(
    service, titles, provider
) -> None:
    """ADR-0008: "failure does not consume or reset a rung on the ladder". A
    skeleton title whose enrichment failed is still a perfectly usable
    skeleton -- genres, ratings and runtime did not stop being true because
    the next attempt failed -- and a retry needs to know which tier it is
    working from."""
    title = await _given_skeleton(titles, tmdb_id=550)
    provider.fail_with(PortUnavailable("TMDb is down"))
    with pytest.raises(PortUnavailable):
        await service.enrich(title.id)
    stored = await titles.get(title.id)
    assert stored is not None
    assert stored.enrichment_state is EnrichmentState.SKELETON
    assert stored.enrichment_error == "TMDb is down"


async def test_a_successful_enrichment_clears_a_previous_error(service, titles, provider) -> None:

    broken" on every dashboard that renders it."""

    await service.enrich(title.id)
    stored = await titles.get(title.id)
    assert stored is not None
    assert stored.enrichment_error is None


async def test_enrichment_never_downgrades_a_tier(service, titles, provider) -> None:

    await service.enrich(title.id)

    the nightly walk runs after every enrichment, so the failure is a daily
    one."""
    ...
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run to verify it fails, then write the service and the handlers**

`EnrichService.enrich(title_id)`:
1. Load the title; `PortDataMalformed` if it does not exist or carries no provider id.
2. Read `raw_payloads`; fetch only if absent or older than the configured refresh window.
3. `to_result(payload, title.id)`, then merge: **fields the provider supplied win, fields it did not are left alone**, `field_provenance` records which provider set what (PRD 02: "so a second metadata provider can be added later without ambiguity"), `enrichment_state` is raised to `ENRICHED` **only via `ENRICHMENT_RANK`**, `enriched_at` stamped, `enrichment_error` cleared.
4. Upsert seasons and episodes.
5. On any `UsherPortError`: `title.evolve(enrichment_error=str(exc))` — **tier untouched** — save, commit, re-raise so `JobWorker` decides back-off vs park.

The three handlers live in `usher/services/handlers.py` (or as small closures the composition roots build):

```python
def enrich_handler(service: EnrichService) -> Handler:
    async def handle(job: Job) -> None:
        await service.enrich(uuid.UUID(job.key))
    return handle
```

`match_handler` re-reads the media item and re-runs `MatchService` including the remote-search tier; `watch_history_handler` calls `WatchStateSyncService.backfill_one`.

|---|---|
| Set `enrichment_state=EnrichmentState.SKELETON` in the failure path | `test_a_failed_enrichment_records_the_error_and_keeps_the_tier` |
| Swallow the exception instead of re-raising | the same case's `pytest.raises` |
| `if new_state > title.enrichment_state` instead of `ENRICHMENT_RANK` | `test_enrichment_never_downgrades_a_tier` |
| Do not clear `enrichment_error` on success | `test_a_successful_enrichment_clears_a_previous_error` |
| Skip the `raw_payloads` write | `test_the_provider_payload_is_cached_verbatim` |
| Always fetch, ignoring the cache | `test_a_cached_payload_within_the_ceiling_is_not_refetched` |

- [ ] **Step 2: Run to verify it fails, then wire the CLI**

`_sync(settings, source_name, kind, allow_full_retraction)` builds an engine, a session, every repository, `MatchService`/`IngestService`/`ReconcileService`, resolves the `Source` by name (or all enabled sources when none is given), builds an adapter through `ConfiguredSourceAdapterFactory`, runs the reconcile, closes the adapter in a `finally` — the same "nothing leaks" rule `SourceService` already documents — and prints a report per source. Then it runs the watch-state lane for the same source.

`_sync_status` prints each source's last runs from `SyncRunRepository.list_for_source`, plus queue depth and parked count. `_unmatched` lists or resolves through `MediaItemRepository`. `_work` runs `JobWorker.startup()` then loops `run_once()` with a sleep between empty passes, or once with `--once`.

```bash
docker compose exec usher python -m usher work --once
```

Expected: `sync-status` reports `no sync has been run yet` and a queue depth of zero for all three kinds; `unmatched` reports nothing; `work --once` reports `0 jobs`. All three must exit 0 against an empty database — a command that only works after a sync is a command an operator cannot use to diagnose why the sync did not happen.

- [ ] **Step 2: Add the two gauges, which are observable rather than recorded**

`usher.jobs.queued` and `usher.jobs.parked` are gauges over a database query, so they are **observable** instruments with a callback rather than something the code increments:

```python
def register_queue_gauges(read_depth: Callable[[], dict[str, int]]) -> None:
    """PRD 10's `usher.jobs.queued`/`usher.jobs.parked`.

    Observable rather than recorded: the queue's depth is a fact about the
    `jobs` table, not an event stream, and a counter incremented on enqueue
    and decremented on complete drifts the moment anything -- a parked job, a
    requeue, a crash -- changes the row without going through both.

    The callback runs on the SDK's own collection interval, which means it
    runs a query on a schedule. It is `SELECT status, kind, count(*) FROM
    jobs GROUP BY 1, 2` over a table with two partial indexes, so it is
    cheap; if it ever stops being, the answer is a materialised count, not a
    counter.
    """
```

The composition roots pass a callable that opens its own short-lived session — the callback fires on a timer, not inside a request, so it must not borrow a request-scoped one.

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the measurement script**

`scripts/measure_ingest.py` — **not a test**, same module-docstring convention as `scripts/measure_bulk_load.py`. It seeds a `FakeEmbyServer` with a configurable item count (default 50,000, of which 80% episodes, matching the real library's shape), runs a full reconcile against a real Postgres, and reports wall time, items/second, and **statements issued** (via a SQLAlchemy `before_cursor_execute` counter).

The number that matters is **statements per item**. A correct implementation is a small constant — one staged `COPY` plus a handful of set-based statements per batch, so at a batch size of 1,000 it is well under 0.05. Anything approaching 1.0 means something is per-item, and at 1,126,674 items that is the difference between a walk that finishes overnight and one that does not.

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
docker run -d --name usher-measure -e POSTGRES_USER=usher -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD=usher \
  -e POSTGRES_DB=usher -p 55432:5432 pgvector/pgvector:pg17
export USHER_DATABASE_URL="postgresql+asyncpg://usher:usher@localhost:55432/usher"
export USHER_SECRET_KEY="$(openssl rand -hex 32)"
uv run alembic upgrade head
uv run python scripts/measure_ingest.py --items 50000
```

Record the measured statements-per-item and items-per-second in `CLAUDE.md`'s "Verified facts" section (Task 26) with the date, the same way M2's index measurement is recorded. If statements-per-item is above 0.1, **stop and find the per-item call** before committing — it is a design defect, not a tuning opportunity.

- [ ] **Step 3: Run everything**

```bash

uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -3
uv run mypy src tests
uv run ruff check --no-cache . && uv run ruff format --check .
uv run lint-imports
```

Expected: all green; `Contracts: 6 kept, 0 broken`. If `lint-imports` reports a break, the likely cause is `services/` reaching `db/` through a new import — which is ADR-0009's whole point and must be fixed by moving the type to `ports/`, never by relaxing the contract.

## Task 26: Live verification, the mutation sweep, and the documentation

M3's live run found that every watch-state write-back would have failed in production, because the fake faithfully implemented the adapter's own guess. M4's fakes have the same property in at least one place — `FakeEmbyServer`'s listing renderer omits play history because M4 *told it to*, and if that instruction were wrong in the same direction as the adapter's, the suite would be green and the pipeline would be wrong. Only a live run separates them.

- [ ] **Step 1: Run the whole mutation sweep in one pass**

Every mutation named in Tasks 1–22, run in sequence against the full suite, in place:

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
find src tests -name __pycache__ -type d -exec rm -rf {} +
export PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1
git status --porcelain   # must be empty before starting
```

For each: apply, `uv run pytest -q`, confirm the named case (and only sensible collateral) fails, `git checkout -- <file>`, confirm green. **Never in a `/tmp` copy** — `cp -r` copies `.venv/bin/*` with absolute shebangs into the original venv, so the mutation is never imported and the suite runs green while proving nothing.

Record any mutation the suite does **not** catch. Each one is a missing test, and writing it is part of this step rather than a follow-up.

- [ ] **Step 2: Live-verify against the real Emby server**

Driven from a throwaway script **outside the working tree**, reading the operator's own secrets file, redacting every credential, token, user id and host from anything it prints — the same rules M3's run followed and `CLAUDE.md` records.

Four things only a live run can settle:

1. **`get_watch_state` really does carry `PlayCount`/`LastPlayedDate`** where the walk does not, against the same item, in one run. This is the measurement M4's entire design rests on, re-taken through the real `EmbyAdapter` rather than through a script.
2. **A bounded delta walk completes and its cursor advances.** Run `sync --kind delta` against a `MinDateLastSavedForUser` cursor 30 days back (measured at ~29,005 items) rather than a full 1,126,674-item walk, and confirm `sync_runs.cursor_at` advances only on `COMPLETED`.
3. **The availability sweep is not reached by a failed walk.** Interrupt a full walk (kill the process mid-run) and confirm `sync_runs` records `FAILED`, `media_items.available` is unchanged for everything already stored, and a re-run completes.
4. **A real ingest batch's statement count matches the measurement.** Instrument one batch against the live server and confirm the statements-per-item figure from Task 25 holds against real payload sizes and real duplicate rates.

**A full walk of 1,126,674 items is expected to take hours**, and it is worth doing once, unattended, with `sync-status` polled — but it is not a gate on this task. Items 1–4 above are. Record what was and was not run, explicitly, the way `CLAUDE.md`'s "Not verified, and the docs say so rather than implying coverage" paragraph does.

**One write to a real account** (marking an item played, to confirm the backfill reads it back) must record the item's complete `UserData` first and restore it exactly afterwards, confirmed by reading it back — same rule M3's run followed.

- [ ] **Step 3: Update the PRD, the ADRs, and `CLAUDE.md`**

PRD changes, each in the commit that earned it (Tasks 3, 7, 10, 15, 20 above already carry theirs); this step is the sweep for anything left:

| File | Change |
|---|---|
| `03-sources-and-sync.md` | The ⚠️ on `play_count`/`last_played_at` becomes the settled rule; stage 2's ladder gains `tvdb_id` and states the stub tier's provider-id requirement and the queued remote search; stage 1 stops claiming source payloads go to `raw_payloads`; stage 4 states that M6 owns it; the reconciliation table gains the retraction guard |
| `02-data-model.md` | `provider_cache_meta` removed from the supporting-tables list, with a note on `raw_payloads.fetched_at`; `Season`/`Episode` marked as landed; `jobs`/`sync_runs` likewise |
| `08-operations.md` | The job-reliability section gains the immediate-park rule for `PortDataMalformed` and names `sync_max_retract_fraction` |
| `09-roadmap.md` | M4 marked ✅ with its four boundary calls stated; M6's row gains "the index stage of PRD 03's pipeline" |
| `10-telemetry-and-dashboards.md` | `usher.sync.run.duration` added to the catalogue; dashboard 1's "unmatched review queue depth" and dashboard 3's "sync run outcomes" noted as backed by real data as of M4 |
| `README.md` | Status table |
| `decisions/README.md` | ADRs 0014–0016 |

`CLAUDE.md` gains, under "Verified facts worth not re-deriving":

- What the live run confirmed, corrected, and did not touch — in the same shape M3's entry uses.
- The measured statements-per-item and items-per-second from Task 25.
- The `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` cannot-read-a-CTE finding from Task 11, which is why the watch-state merge is two statements.
- The `p.value::integer` finding from Task 14, which is why provider matching is three statements rather than one join.
- The status line: **M4 complete**, with the new command list and test count.

ADR-0016, `docs/prd/decisions/0016-raw-payloads-cache-providers-not-sources.md`, records the PRD correction: PRD 03 stage 1 said to store every source item's raw payload in `raw_payloads`; at 1,126,674 items and ~8 kB apiece that is ~9 GB against a database PRD 08 budgets at 8–12 GB total, to avoid a refetch that costs one request against a source Usher is already walking nightly. Provider payloads are different in kind — they cost a rate-limited network call, TMDb's terms cap re-fetching at 6 months, and three later milestones re-derive entities from them. The same ADR records folding `provider_cache_meta` into `raw_payloads.fetched_at`.

- [ ] **Step 4: Final gate**

```bash
cd /home/anirudhlath/code/usher
uv run pytest -q 2>&1 | tail -3
uv run pytest tests/unit -q 2>&1 | tail -3
uv run mypy src tests
uv run ruff check --no-cache . && uv run ruff format --check .
uv run lint-imports
python3 - <<'EOF'
import re, pathlib
bad = []
for md in pathlib.Path("docs").rglob("*.md"):
    for link in re.findall(r'\]\(([^)#][^)]*\.md)\)', md.read_text()):
        if not (md.parent / link).resolve().exists():
            bad.append(f"{md}: {link}")
print("\n".join(bad) if bad else "OK")
EOF
```

Expected: all pass, `Contracts: 6 kept, 0 broken`, `OK`.


Gaps, deliberate and stated in Scope: the index stage (M6), push/reconnect/demand/SSE (M5), the three admin HTTP routes (M9), and `Person`/`Credit`/`Collection`/`Image` (M7/M9, re-derivable from `raw_payloads`).

### Placeholder scan

Tasks 1–17 and 19–20 carry complete code for every code step. Tasks 18, 21, 22, 23, 24 and 25 carry complete **test** code and describe their implementations structurally rather than line by line — that is a real thinning of the plan's promise, and it is called out here rather than hidden: an engineer executing those tasks writes the implementation from the tests plus the stated rules, which is the same TDD loop but with less transcription. If that is not acceptable, those six tasks want expanding before execution starts; the tests, the rules, the file paths, the mutations and the expected outputs are all present, so the expansion is mechanical.

Three places deliberately name something not yet written and say so explicitly rather than pretending: `current_traceparent` (defined in Task 15, used in 16 and 19), `EpisodeRepository.resolve_season` (discovered in Task 16, added to the port there), and `_constraint_name`'s move to `db/repositories/_errors.py` (Task 10).

Two places name a line that will not survive `ruff` and say so, so the engineer's first run is not spent on it: the inline `__import__("sqlalchemy").text(...)` in `EpisodeRow` (Task 7) and the unused `replace` import in `FakeMediaItemRepository` (Task 9).

One place names a SQL construction that **does not work** and tells the engineer to verify it first and take the alternative: `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` cannot read a CTE (Task 11), and `p.value::integer` casts non-numeric rows (Task 14). Both were caught while writing the plan rather than left for execution; both are recorded in `CLAUDE.md` in Task 26.

### Type consistency

Checked across tasks: `SourceWatchState.play_count: int | None` (1) → `to_watch_state(..., play_history_is_trustworthy: bool)` (3) → `WatchStateMerge.play_count: int | None` (8) → `merge_from_source`'s `COALESCE` (11) → `WatchStateSyncService` (18). `ProviderRef(provider, value, kind)` is one type across 8, 14, 15, 20, 21. `MatchOutcome(external_id, title_id, method, episode_id)` is constructed in 15 and consumed in 16. `MediaItemUpsert.last_seen_at` is required in 8, supplied from `run.started_at` in 16 and 17, and read by the sweep in 10. `JobPriority` is an `IntEnum` in 5 and compared arithmetically in 12, 13, 15, 16. `SweepResult`/`AvailabilitySweepRefused` are defined in 8, raised in 9/10, and re-raised in 17. `EnrichmentResult` is defined in 20 and consumed in 22.

One inconsistency found and fixed while reviewing: `MediaItemRepository.resolve_series_titles` is typed `-> dict[str, uuid.UUID]` on the port (Task 9) and the fake's signature in the same task returns `dict[uuid.UUID, uuid.UUID]`. The port is right — the key is an `external_id`. Fix the fake's annotation when writing it.
